    \(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} I_{j, k} (y) & = & \int | v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} (x_{1 : \ell}) | k^Q_t (x_j - y) | \phi (x_k) - \phi' (x_k) | \mathrm{d} x_{1 : \ell}\\ & = & \int | v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} (z_{1 : \ell} + y + r) | k^Q_t (r) | \phi (z_k + y + r) - \phi' (z_k + y + r) | \mathrm{d} z_{1 : \ell \setminus \{ j \}} \mathrm{d} r\\ & = & \int | v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} (z_{1 : \ell}) | k^Q_t (r) | \phi (z_k + y + r) - \phi' (z_k + y + r) | \mathrm{d} z_{1 : \ell \setminus \{ j \}} \mathrm{d} r \end{array}\)

    where the last equality follows from translation invariance of the kernels \(v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T}\). Then by Minkowski's integral inequality, the estimate \(\| u (\cdot + h) \|_{L^{2, - n}} \lesssim_h \langle h \rangle^{| n |} \| u \|_{L^{2, - n}}\) and

    \(\displaystyle \| \langle \cdot \rangle^{- n} I_{j, k} \|_{L^2} \lesssim \unicode{x2980}v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta} \| k^Q_t \|_{L^1} \| \phi - \phi' \|_{L^{2, - n}}\)

    where \(\langle r \rangle^{| n |}\) is eaten by \(\| k^Q_t \|_{L^1}\) and \(\langle z_k \rangle^{| n |}\) by the parameter \(\eta\) in the kernel norm. This shows (4.3) in the case \(\boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M} \setminus \mathfrak{M}_p\). For \(\boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M}_p\), we combine the above argument with how this case was handled in proving (4.1). Finally, (4.4) follows along similar lines from the proof of (4.2).

  4. The \(T\) and \(\rho\) limits:

    The claim about the \(T \rightarrow \infty\) limits follows from Corollary 3.15.

    For the claim about the \(\rho \rightarrow \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}\), limit, estimate, when \(\boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M} \setminus \mathfrak{M}_p\),

    \(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \| Q_t (F^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a}), \infty, \rho}_t - F^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a}), T, \rho}_t) (\phi) \|_{L^{2, - n}}^2\\ \asymp & \int \left| \int (1 - \rho (x_{1 : \ell})) v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \sum_{j \in 1 : \ell} \sigma_j k^Q_t (x_j - y) \psi_{1 : \ell} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell} \right|^2 \langle y \rangle^{- n} \mathrm{d} y\\ \lesssim & \sup_{x_{1 : \ell}} | 1 - \rho (x_{1 : \ell}) |^2 \unicode{x2980}v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta}^2 \unicode{x2980}k^Q_t \unicode{x2980}_{L^1}^2 \int \langle y \rangle^{- n} \mathrm{d} y \end{array}\)

    and apply dominated convergence. On the other hand, when \(\boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M}_p\) with \(\varkappa := \varkappa (\boldsymbol{a}) = 1\), we use neutrality and Lemma A.6 to get

    \(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \int \left| \int (1 - \rho (x_{1 : 2})) v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t (\xi_{1 : 2}) \frac{k^Q_t (x_1 - y) - k^Q_t (x_2 - y)}{| x_1 - x_2 |} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : 2} \right|^2 \langle y \rangle^{- n} \mathrm{d} y\\ \lesssim & \int \left| \int (1 - \rho (x_{1 : 2})) v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t (\xi_{1 : 2}) e^{- c \sqrt{t} | x_1 - y |} e^{c \sqrt{t} | x_1 - x_2 |} e^{- \frac{m^2}{2 t}} t^{\frac{1}{2}} \mathrm{d} x_{1 : 2} \right|^2 \langle y \rangle^{- n} \mathrm{d} y\\ \lesssim & t^{\frac{1}{2}} \sup_{x_{1 : 2}} | 1 - \rho (x_{1 : 2}) |^2 \unicode{x2980}v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta}^2 \left\| e^{- c \sqrt{t} | \cdot |} e^{- \frac{m^2}{2 t}} \right\|_{L^1} \int \langle y \rangle^{- n} \mathrm{d} y \end{array}\)

    and again conclude from dominated convergence.

    The case of \(\langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell], T}_t (\phi), \dot{G}_t X \rangle\) follows similarly, by combining the above argument with the proof of (4.2).

\(\Box\)

Combining the bounds (4.1) and (4.2), we obtain that for \(\ell', \ell''\) such that \(\ell' + \ell'' \geq \ell\), we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell'], T}_t (\phi), \dot{G}_t F^{[\ell''], T}_t (\phi) \rangle \|_{L^{\infty}} & \lesssim & \lambda_t (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell' - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{- 1} \lambda_t (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell'' - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{- 1}\\ & \lesssim & (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \end{array} \) (4.5)

again uniformly in \(\rho\), \(\phi\), and \(T \in [0, \infty]\).

We are in now position to prove the existence of the sine–Gordon scale decomposition process \(\varphi\). We start with recalling the following

Theorem 4.2. ([29]) Let \(\beta^2 < 6 \pi\) and denote by \(\tilde{F}^T := F^{[\leq 3], T}\). Then for any \(\rho \in \mathcal{C}^{\infty}_c\) and \(T \in [0, \infty)\) there exists a unique solution \((\varphi^{T, \rho}, \tilde{R}^{T, \rho})\) to the equation

\(\displaystyle \left\{ \begin{array}{lll} \varphi_t^{T, \rho} & = & - \int_0^t \dot{G}_s (\tilde{F}_s^{T, \rho} (\varphi_s^{T, \rho}) + \tilde{R}_s^{T, \rho}) \mathrm{d} s + B_t\\ \tilde{R}_t^{T, \rho} & = & \mathbb{E}_t^B \left[ \int_t^T \left( \partial_s + \frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\dot{G}_s} \right) \tilde{F}_s^{T, \rho} (\varphi_s^{T, \rho}) - \left\langle \mathrm{D} \tilde{F}_s^{T, \rho} (\varphi_s^{T, \rho}), \dot{G}_s (\tilde{F}_s^{T, \rho} (\varphi_s^{T, \rho}) + \tilde{R}_s^{T, \rho}) \right\rangle \mathrm{d} s \right] \end{array} \right. \) (4.6)

satisfying

\(\displaystyle \sup_{\rho} \sup_T \| \tilde{R}_t^{T, \rho} \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})} \lesssim | \lambda | + \lambda_t^4 \langle t \rangle^{- 3} \) (4.7)

Proof. Existence and uniqueness is Lemma 2.6 and Theorem 2.2 in [29]. The uniform boundedness estimate (4.7) follows from Lemma 4.1.\(\Box\)

Corollary 4.3. Let \(n > 2\). Then

  1. The laws of \((\varphi^{T, \rho}_{\cdot}, B_{\cdot})\) are tight on \(\mathcal{C}_{\operatorname{loc}} (\mathbb{R}_+ ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))^2\)

  2. The laws of \(\tilde{R}_{\cdot}^{T, \rho}\) are tight on \(L_{\operatorname{loc}}^2 (\mathbb{R}_+ ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\), equipped with the weak topology of \(L^2 ([0, S] ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\) for each \(S \in \mathbb{N}\).

Proof. Fix an \(S < \infty\). Denote by \(Z := \varphi - B\). It follows from (4.1), (4.7) and (A.2) that uniformly in \(T\) and \(\rho\),

\(\displaystyle \sup_{t \in [0, S]} (\| Z^{T, \rho}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; W^{1, \infty})} + \| \dot{Z}^{T, \rho}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})}) \leq C_{\lambda, S} .\)

It is clear that \(W^{1, \infty} (\mathbb{R}^2)\) is compactly embedded in \(L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})\). Indeed, pick a bounded \((u_k) \subseteq W^{1, \infty} (\mathbb{R}^2)\); on every closed ball of \(\mathbb{R}^2\) we may apply Arzelà–Ascoli, and a diagonal argument allows to extract a subsequence \((u_{k_j})\) converging locally uniformly on \(\mathbb{R}^2\) to a \(u \in \mathcal{C}_b\). By integrability of \(\langle x \rangle^{- n}\), dominated convergence yields \(u_{k_j} \rightarrow u\) in \(L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})\).

Then by Lipschitzness obtained from the bound above on \(\dot{Z}^T_t\), we may use Arzelà–Ascoli to see that \(\{ Z^{T, \rho}_{\cdot} \}_{T, \rho}\) lives almost surely in a compact subset of \(\mathcal{C} ([0, S] ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\), hence its laws are tight.

On the other hand, it follows from (4.7) that uniformly in \(T\) and \(\rho\), we have almost surely

\(\displaystyle \| \tilde{R}^{T, \rho} \|^2_{L^2 ([0, S] ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))} = \int_0^S \| \tilde{R}^{T, \rho}_s \|_{L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})}^2 \mathrm{d} s \lesssim \int_0^S | \lambda | + \lambda_s^4 \langle s \rangle^{- 3} \mathrm{d} s,\)

hence by the Banach–Alaoglu theorem and reflexivity, \(\{ \tilde{R}^{T, \rho} \}_{T, \rho}\) lives almost surely in a weakly compact subset of \(L^2 ([0, S] ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\).\(\Box\)

By Prokhorov's theorem, the sequence of the above measures admit a weakly convergent subsequence. The next proposition identifies such limits as the laws of the solution to an FBSDE corresponding to the cutoff-removed effective force coefficients.

Theorem 4.4. For every accumulation point \((\nu_{\varphi}, \nu_R)\) of \(\operatorname{Law} (\varphi^{T, \rho}, \tilde{R}^{T, \rho})_{T, \rho}\) in Theorem 4.2, there exists a filtered probability space \((\Omega, \mathscr{A}, \mathscr{F}, \mathbb{P})\) with admissible filtration \(\mathscr{F}\) such that \((\varphi, \tilde{R})\) has law \((\nu_{\varphi}, \nu_R)\) and solves

\(\displaystyle \left\{ \begin{array}{lll} \varphi_t & = & - \int_0^t \dot{G}_s \left( \tilde{F}_s^{\infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi_s) + \tilde{R}_s \right) \mathrm{d} s + B_t\\ \tilde{R}_t & = & \mathbb{E}_t^{\mathscr{F}} \left[ \int_t^{\infty} \left( \partial_s + \frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\dot{G}_s} \right) \tilde{F}_s^{\infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi_s) - \left\langle \mathrm{D} \tilde{F}_s^{\infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s \left( \tilde{F}_s^{\infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi_s) + \tilde{R}_s \right) \right\rangle \mathrm{d} s \right] \end{array} \right. \) (4.8)

Proof. Let \((T, \rho)\) index a convergent subsequence. By the Skorokhod representation theorem, there exists a probability space \((\Omega, \mathscr{A}, \mathbb{P})\) and random variables \((\varphi, \tilde{R})\) such that \(\mathbb{P}\)-almost surely \(\varphi^{T, \rho} \rightarrow \varphi\) in \(\mathcal{C} ([0, S], L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\) and \(\tilde{R}^{T, \rho} \rightarrow \tilde{R}\) weakly in \(L^2 ([0, S] ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\) for any \(S\).

Convergence of the forward equation

Write

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \int_0^t \dot{G}_s \left( \tilde{F}_s^{\infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi_s) + \tilde{R}_s \right) - \dot{G}_s (\tilde{F}_s^{T, \rho} (\varphi_s^{T, \rho}) + \tilde{R}_s^{T, \rho}) \mathrm{d} s\\ = & \int_0^t \dot{G}_s \left( \tilde{F}_s^{\infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi_s) - \tilde{F}_s^{\infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi_s^{T, \rho}) \right) \mathrm{d} s + \int_0^t \dot{G}_s \left( \tilde{F}_s^{\infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi_s^{T, \rho}) - \tilde{F}_s^{T, \rho} (\varphi_s^{T, \rho}) \right) \mathrm{d} s\\ + & \int_0^t \dot{G}_s (\tilde{R}_s - \tilde{R}_s^{T, \rho}) \mathrm{d} s \end{array}\)

The first term vanishes in \(L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})\) due to (4.3), the second due to the convergence result of Proposition 4.1, and the third weakly in \(L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})\) due to \(\tilde{R}^{T, \rho} \rightarrow \tilde{R}\). Indeed, for the latter, note that for any \(h \in L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})\) we have

\(\displaystyle \left\langle \int_0^t \dot{G}_s (\tilde{R}_s - \tilde{R}_s^{T, \rho}) \mathrm{d} s, h \right\rangle_{L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})} = \int_0^t \langle (\tilde{R}_s - \tilde{R}_s^{T, \rho}), \dot{G}_s^{\ast} h \rangle_{L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})} \mathrm{d} s \longrightarrow 0,\)

as \(s \mapsto \dot{G}_s^{\ast} h\) is in \(L^2 ([0, t] ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\) due to Lemma A.9 (recall Notation B.2). Coupled with the convergence \(\varphi^{T, \rho} \rightarrow \varphi\), we obtain that the forward equation of (4.8) holds in the limit.

Convergence of the integrand of the backward equation

We are going to show that the integrands of \(\tilde{R}_t^{T, \rho}\) converge to that with the limiting force coefficients. For any \(\ell', \ell'' \geq 0\),

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \left\langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell'], \infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi), \dot{G} F^{[\ell''], \infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi) \right\rangle - \langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell'], T, \rho} (\varphi^{T, \rho}), \dot{G} F^{[\ell''], T, \rho} (\varphi^{T, \rho}) \rangle\\ = & \left\langle \mathrm{D} \left( F^{[\ell'], \infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi) - F^{[\ell'], T, \rho} (\varphi) \right), \dot{G} F^{[\ell''], \infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi) \right\rangle\\ + & \left\langle \mathrm{D} (F^{[\ell'], T, \rho} (\varphi) - F^{[\ell'], T, \rho} (\varphi^{T, \rho})), \dot{G} F^{[\ell''], \infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi) \right\rangle\\ + & \left\langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell'], T, \rho} (\varphi^{T, \rho}), \dot{G} \left( F^{[\ell''], \infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} (\varphi) - F^{[\ell''], T, \rho} (\varphi) \right) \right\rangle\\ + & \langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell'], T, \rho} (\varphi^{T, \rho}), \dot{G} (F^{[\ell''], T, \rho} (\varphi) - F^{[\ell''], T, \rho} (\varphi^{T, \rho})) \rangle \end{array}\)

and the first term vanishes in \(\mathcal{C} ([0, S], L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\) due to the convergence result of Proposition 4.1, the second due to (4.4), the third due to (4.2) combined with the convergence result of Proposition 4.1, and the fourth due to (4.2) and (4.3).

On the other hand,

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \left\langle \mathrm{D} \tilde{F}^{\infty, {\mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} } (\varphi), \dot{G} \tilde{R} \right\rangle - \langle \mathrm{D} \tilde{F}^{T, \rho} (\varphi^{T, \rho}), \dot{G} \tilde{R}^{T, \rho} \rangle\\ = & \left\langle \mathrm{D} \left( \tilde{F}^{\infty, {\mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} } (\varphi) - \tilde{F}^{T, \rho} (\varphi) \right), \dot{G} \tilde{R}^{T, \rho} \right\rangle + \langle \mathrm{D} (\tilde{F}^{T, \rho} (\varphi) - \tilde{F}^{T, \rho} (\varphi^{T, \rho})), \dot{G} \tilde{R}^{T, \rho} \rangle\\ + & \left\langle \mathrm{D} \tilde{F}^{\infty, {\mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} } (\varphi), \dot{G} (\tilde{R} - \tilde{R}^{T, \rho}) \right\rangle \end{array}\)

and the first term vanishes in strongly in \(L^2 ([0, S] ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\) due to the convergence result of Proposition 4.1 combined with (4.7), the second due to (4.4) and (4.7). Finally, the third vanishes weakly in \(L^2 ([0, S] ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\). To see this, denote by

\(\displaystyle A_s : L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}) \rightarrow L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}), \qquad A_s (X) := \left\langle \mathrm{D} \tilde{F}^{\infty, {\mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} }_s (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s X \right\rangle .\)

Then it follows from (4.2) that

\(\displaystyle \sup_{s \in [0, S]} \| A_s \|_{\mathcal{L} (L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))} < \infty .\)

Hence for every \(h \in L^2 ([0, S] ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\), we have that \(A^{\ast} h \in L^2 ([0, S] ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\), so

\(\displaystyle \int_0^S \left\langle \left\langle \mathrm{D} \tilde{F}^{\infty, {\mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} }_s (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s (\tilde{R}_s - \tilde{R}^{T, \rho}_s) \right\rangle, h_s \right\rangle_{L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})} \mathrm{d} s = \int_0^S \langle \tilde{R}_s - \tilde{R}^{T, \rho}_s, A_s^{\ast} h_s \rangle_{L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})} \mathrm{d} s \longrightarrow 0.\)

Combined with the cancellation induced by the structure of (2.20), the statement follows.

Convergence of the backward equation

Take \(\mathscr{G}^{T, \rho}\) be the filtration generated by \(P^{T, \rho} := (\varphi_{\cdot}^{T, \rho}, B_{\cdot}, \tilde{R}_{\cdot}^{T, \rho})\). Denote by \(\mathscr{F}^{T, \rho}\) its right-continuous augmentation.

Fix \(S < \infty\) and let \(a < b \in [0, S]\). Denoting by \(I^{T, \rho}\) the integrand of the backward equation, for any \(G\) bounded continuous function and \(h \in L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})\), by Fubini and the tower rule we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \mathbb{E} \left[ \int_a^b G ( P^{T, \rho} |_{[0, a]}) \left\langle \tilde{R}^{T, \rho}_t - \int_t^T I^{T, \rho}_s \mathrm{d} s, h \right\rangle_{L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})} \mathrm{d} t \right]\\ = & \int_a^b \mathbb{E} \left[ \mathbb{E}_t^B \left\langle \mathbb{E}_t^B \left[ \int_t^T I^{T, \rho}_s \mathrm{d} s \right] - \int_t^T I^{T, \rho}_s \mathrm{d} s, G ( P^{T, \rho} |_{[0, a]}) h \right\rangle_{L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})} \right] \mathrm{d} t\\ = & 0. \end{array}\)

By the convergence of \(I^{T, \rho}\), \(\tilde{R}^{T, \rho}\) and \(P^{T, \rho}\), and the uniform tail bounds (4.7) and (4.5), we may pass to the limit in the above equation. Thus approximating in \(L^2 (\Omega)\) yields

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} \left[ X \int_a^b \left\langle \tilde{R}_t - \int_t^{\infty} I^{\infty, {\mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} }_s \mathrm{d} s, h \right\rangle_{L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})} \mathrm{d} t \right] = 0\)

for any bounded \(\mathscr{G}_a\)-measurable \(X\). Now note that the same holds with \(\tilde{R}_t\) replaced by \(\mathbb{E}^{\mathscr{F}}_t \left[ \int_t^{\infty} I^{\infty, {\mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} }_u \mathrm{d} u \right]\). Thus considering the process

\(\displaystyle M_t := \int_0^t \tilde{R}_s - \mathbb{E}^{\mathscr{F}}_s \left[ \int_s^{\infty} I^{\infty, {\mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} }_u \mathrm{d} u \right] \mathrm{d} s,\)

we have, for any bounded \(\mathscr{G}_a\)-measurable \(X\), that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [X \langle M_b - M_a, h \rangle_{L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n})}] = 0.\)

Moreover, \(M\) is continuous and \(\mathscr{F}\)-adapted, so also \(\mathscr{G}\)-adapted. Hence \(M\) is a martingale, and since it of bounded variation, it is identically zero. Thus \(\tilde{R}_s = \mathbb{E}^{\mathscr{F}}_s \left[ \int_s^{\infty} I^{\infty, {\mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}} }_u \mathrm{d} u \right]\) \(\mathrm{d} t \otimes \mathrm{d} \mathbb{P}\)-almost everywhere.

Admissibility of the filtration

Recall that \(\mathscr{F}\) was defined to be the usual augmentation of \(\mathscr{G}\), the filtration generated by \(P := (\varphi_{\cdot}, B_{\cdot}, \tilde{R}_{\cdot})\), so we only need to show that \(B\) is an \(\mathscr{F}\)-martingale. Before removing the cutoffs, since \(P^{T, \rho} |_{[0, s]}\) is \(\mathscr{F}^B_s\)-measurable, we have for any \(s < t\)

\(\displaystyle \operatorname{Law} ( P^{T, \rho} |_{[0, s]}, B_t - B_s) =\operatorname{Law} ( P^{T, \rho} |_{[0, s]}) \otimes \operatorname{Law} (B_t - B_s),\)

which passes to the limit. Now let \(A \in \mathscr{F}_s\). For any \(s < v < t\), we have \(A \in \mathscr{G}_v\), hence

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [\mathbb{1}_A (B_t - B_v)] = 0.\)

We then conclude from the fact that \(B_v \rightarrow B_s\) in \(L^2 (\Omega ; L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}))\) as \(v \rightarrow s\).\(\Box\)

Corollary 4.5. Let \(\varphi\) be as in Theorem 4.4 with associated convergent subsequence indexed by \((T, \rho)\). Then for any \(n > 2\) and \(\varepsilon > 0\), we have

\(\displaystyle \operatorname{Law} (\varphi_T^{T, \rho}) \rightarrow \operatorname{Law} (\varphi_{\infty})\)

weakly in \(H^{- \varepsilon} (\langle x \rangle^{- n})\).

Proof. Write \(E := H^{- \varepsilon} (\langle x \rangle^{- n})\) and \(Z := \varphi - B\), From (4.1), (4.7) and (A.2) and the embedding \(L^2 (\langle x \rangle^{- n}) \hookrightarrow E\) we have

\(\displaystyle \sup_{T, \rho} \| \dot{Z}_s^{T, \rho} \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; E)} \lesssim \langle s \rangle^{- 1 - \delta} \mathfrak{l}_s^c\)

so that \(Z_{\infty} := \lim_{t \rightarrow \infty} Z_t\) exists almost surely. Fixing \(S < \infty\), we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| Z_T^{T, \rho} - Z_{\infty} \|_E & \leq & \| Z_T^{T, \rho} - Z^{T, \rho}_S \|_E + \| Z^{T, \rho}_S - Z_S \|_E + \| Z_S - Z_{\infty} \|_E\\ & \lesssim & \int_S^T \langle s \rangle^{- 1 - \delta} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \mathrm{d} s + \| Z^{T, \rho}_S - Z_S \|_E + \int_S^{\infty} \langle s \rangle^{- 1 - \delta} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \mathrm{d} s. \end{array}\)

Since \(Z^{T, \rho}_S \rightarrow Z_S\) in \(E\) almost surely, the second term vanishes. Then letting \(S \rightarrow \infty\) shows \(\| Z_T^{T, \rho} - Z_{\infty} \|_E \rightarrow 0\) almost surely. The statement then follows from convergence in probability of \(B_T\).\(\Box\)

A process \(\varphi\) in the above theorem is the (UV-complete) scale decomposition process of the massive sine–Gordon model and \(\operatorname{Law} (\varphi_{\infty})\) is the sine–Gordon measure. Let us remark that when the coupling constant \(\lambda\) is small or the volume is finite, the measure is unique.

From now on, we are going to omit the reference to the removed cutoffs in (4.8) and write simply \(\tilde{F}\) instead of \(\tilde{F}^{\infty, \mathbb{1}_{\mathbb{R}^2}}\).

Let us collect some immediate consequences of this theorem, namely, basic probabilistic estimates on local functionals of \(\varphi\) that will often be used in what follows. All of these bounds follow straightforwardly from the decomposition \(\varphi_t = B_t + Z_t\) to a Gaussian \(B\) and a bounded drift \(Z\).

Lemma 4.6. Let \(\varphi = (\varphi_t)_{t \geq 0}\) be the scale decomposition process of sine–Gordon, \(x \in \mathbb{R}^2\), \(p \in [1, \infty)\). Then for all \(s < \infty\), \(n \in \mathbb{N}\), \(\alpha \in \mathbb{R}\) and multiindex \(\mu \in \mathbb{N}^2\) we have

\(\displaystyle \| (\partial^{\mu} \varphi_s)^n (x) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim_{\lambda, p, \mu} \langle s \rangle^{\frac{n | \mu |}{2}} \mathfrak{l}_s^n, \) (4.9)
\(\displaystyle \| e^{\alpha \varphi_s (x)} \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim_{\lambda, p, \alpha} e^{\frac{\alpha^2 p}{2} \mathcal{G}_s (0)} \) (4.10)

and, for some \(c \in \mathbb{R}_+\),

\(\displaystyle \| \partial^{\mu} e^{\alpha \varphi_s (x)} \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} \lesssim_{\lambda, p, \alpha, \mu} e^{\frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_s (0)} \langle s \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2}} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \) (4.11)

Proof. Denote \(\dot{Z}_s := - \dot{G}_s (\tilde{F}_s (\varphi_s) + \tilde{R}_s)\). By Young's convolution inequality, (4.1), (4.7), (A.2), and subcriticality \(\delta > 0\) we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \partial^{\mu} Z_s \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})} & \leq & \int_0^t \| \| \partial^{\mu} \dot{Z}_s \|_{L^{\infty}} \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega)} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \leq & \int_0^t \| \partial^{\mu} k^Q_s \|_{L^1} (\| Q_s F^{[\leq 3]}_s (\varphi_s) \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})} + \| Q_s \tilde{R}_s \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})}) \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \int_0^t \langle s \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2} - 1} \langle s \rangle^{- 1} (\lambda_s \mathfrak{l}_s^c + \lambda + \lambda_s^4 \langle s \rangle^{- 3}) \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & {\langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2}}} . \end{array} \) (4.12)

When \(| \mu | = 0\), for any \(q \in [1, \infty)\), by Gaussianity of \(B\), hypercontractivity and (4.12) we have

\(\displaystyle \| \varphi_s (x) \|_{L^q (\Omega)} \lesssim_q \| B_s (x) \|_{L^2 (\Omega)} + {\| \| Z_s \|_{L^{\infty}} \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega)}} \lesssim \sqrt{\mathcal{G}_s (0)} + 1 \lesssim \lambda (1 + \log (1 \vee s)),\)

and the claim then follows from Hölder with \(q := n p\). When \(| \mu | \geq 1\), since for the \(2 k^{\operatorname{th}}\) Hermite polynomial it holds that \(\operatorname{He}_{2 k} (0, \tau) \asymp \tau^k\), we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \mathbb{E} [(\partial^{\mu} B_t)^2 (x)] = (- 1)^{| \mu |} (\partial^{2 \mu} \mathcal{G}_t) (0) & \asymp & \left. \partial^{2 \mu}_{\xi} \left( \int_0^t s^{- 1} e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}} e^{- \frac{s}{4} | \xi |^2} \mathrm{d} s \right) \right|_{\xi = 0}\\ & \lesssim & \int_0^t s^{- 1} e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}} \left. \prod_{i = 1}^d s^{2 \mu_i} \operatorname{He}_{2 \mu_i} (\xi_i, s^{- 1}) e^{- \frac{s}{4} | \xi |^2} \right|_{\xi = 0} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \int_0^t e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}} s^{| \mu | - 1} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{| \mu |} . \end{array}\)

The estimate (4.10) follows simply from

\(\displaystyle \| e^{\alpha \varphi_s (x)} \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \| e^{\alpha B_s (x)} \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \left\| e^{\alpha \| Z_s \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathbb{R}^2)}} \right\|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega)} \lesssim e^{\frac{\alpha^2 p}{2} \mathcal{G}_s (0)} e^{\alpha C_{\lambda}} .\)

For (4.11), recall that given jointly Gaussian vectors \((X, Y)\), we have

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [e^{\langle a, X \rangle} e^{\langle u, Y \rangle}] = e^{\frac{1}{2} \langle a, \Sigma_X a \rangle} \mathbb{E} [e^{\langle u, Y + \Sigma_{X Y} a \rangle}]\)

for every \(u\), where \(\Sigma_X\) and \(\Sigma_{X Y}\) are the corresponding (cross-)covariance matrices. Thus

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [e^{\langle a, X \rangle} F (Y)] = e^{\frac{1}{2} \langle a, \Sigma_X a \rangle} \mathbb{E} [F (Y + \Sigma_{X Y} a)]\)

for any polynomial \(F\), so that denoting by \(P_{\mu}\) the polynomials obtained from Faà di Bruno,

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \partial^{\mu} e^{\alpha B_t (x)} \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} & = & \mathbb{E} [e^{\alpha B_t (y)} | P_{\mu} (\{ B_t^{\nu} (x) \}_{\nu \leq \mu}) |]\\ & = & e^{\frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)} \| P_{\mu} (\{ \partial^{\nu} B_t (x) + a\operatorname{Cov} (\partial^{\nu} B_t (x), B_t (x)) \}_{\nu \leq \mu}) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)}, \end{array}\)

and since

\(\displaystyle \operatorname{Cov} (\partial^{\nu} B_t (x), B_t (x)) = (- 1)^{| \nu |} \partial^{\nu} \mathcal{G}_t (0) \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \nu |}{2}} \mathfrak{l}_t^c,\)

we get

\(\displaystyle \| \partial^{\mu} e^{\alpha B_t (x)} \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} \lesssim e^{\frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2}} \mathfrak{l}_t^c .\)

Then using the above \(L^{\infty}\) bounds on \(Z\),

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \partial^{\mu} e^{\alpha \varphi_t (x)} \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} & = & \| \partial^{\mu} (e^{\alpha B_t (x)} e^{\alpha Z_t (x)}) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)}\\ & \lesssim & \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \| \partial^{\nu} e^{\alpha B_t (x)} \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} \| \partial^{\mu - \nu} e^{\alpha Z_t (x)} \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega)}\\ & \lesssim & e^{\alpha C_{\lambda}} e^{\frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2}} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \end{array}\)

as requested.\(\Box\)

Proposition 4.7. (existence of the effective force observable and its remainder) Let \(\beta^2 < 6 \pi\), \(y \in \mathbb{R}^2\). Then

  1. for any \(\tilde{\ell} \in \mathbb{N}\) with \(\tilde{\ell} > \delta^{- 1} - 1\), the germ \(F^{[\leq \tilde{\ell}]} (y)\) is coherent, and for any \(\ell' \geq \tilde{\ell}\), it satisfies (2.21).

  2. for any \(\ell^{\ast} \in \mathbb{N}_0\), \(\dot{G} R^{[> \ell^{\ast}]}\), given by (2.22), is well-defined and satisfies (2.26).

Proof. Using the symmetry of \(v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t\) under permutations of indices (which extends to the UV limit by the proof of Corollary (3.15)),

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} F^{[\ell]}_t (\phi) & = & i \beta \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M}\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{a}) = \ell \end{array}$}}} \int \rho (x_{1 : \ell}) v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{a})} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \sum_{j = 1}^{\ell} \sigma_j \delta_{x_j} \psi_{1 : \ell} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell}\\ & = & i \beta \ell \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M}\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{a}) = \ell \end{array}$}}} \int \rho (x_{1 : \ell}) v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{a})} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \sigma_1 \delta_{x_1} \psi_{1 : \ell} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell} .\\ & =: & \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M}\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{a}) = \ell \end{array}$}}} F^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t (\phi) . \end{array}\)

We may represent \(F^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t\) as a function \(y \mapsto K^{F, [\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t [\phi] (y)\) over \(\mathbb{R}^d\) via the duality

\(\displaystyle \langle K^{F, [\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t [\phi], h \rangle_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}^d)} = \langle F^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t (\phi), h \rangle_{\mathcal{S}, \mathcal{S}'}\)

for \(h \in \mathcal{S}\). That is,

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} K^{F, [\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t [\phi] (y) h (y) \mathrm{d} y\\ = & \int h (x_1) \rho (x_1) \sum_{\sigma_1 \in \{ - 1, 1 \}} i \beta \ell \sigma_1 \psi_1 \int \rho (x_{2 : \ell}) v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{a})} (\xi_1, \xi_{2 : \ell}) \psi_{2 : \ell} \mathrm{d} \xi_{2 : \ell} \mathrm{d} x_1 \end{array}\)

which yields

\(\displaystyle K^{F, [\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t [\phi] (y) = \rho (y) i \beta \ell \sum_{\sigma_1 \in \{ - 1, 1 \}} \sigma_1 \psi_{(\sigma_1, y)} \int \rho (x_{2 : \ell}) v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{a})} (\sigma_1, y, \xi_{2 : \ell}) \psi_{2 : \ell} \mathrm{d} \xi_{2 : \ell} .\)

We abuse notation and write \(F^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t (\phi)\) to mean the function \(K^{F, [\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t [\phi]\). For \(\boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M} \setminus \mathfrak{M}_p\), we then simply have, uniformly in \(\| \rho \|_{L^{\infty}} \leq 1\), that

\(\displaystyle | F^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t (\phi) (y) | \lesssim \sup_{\xi_1} \int | v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t (\xi_{1 : \ell}) | \mathrm{d} \xi_{2 : \ell} \lesssim \unicode{x2980}v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta},\)

whereas for \(\boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M}_p\), with \(\varkappa := \varkappa (\boldsymbol{a}) = 1\), by neutrality and charge conjugation symmetry of \(v^{[2] (\varkappa)}_{t, T}\) (cf. Remark 3.11 and proof of Corollary (3.15))

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & F^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t (\phi) (y)\\ = & \rho (y) i \beta \ell e^{+ i \beta \phi (y)} \int \rho (x_2) v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{a})} (+, y, -, x_2) e^{- i \beta \phi (x_2)} \mathrm{d} x_2\\ - & \rho (y) i \beta \ell e^{- i \beta \phi (y)} \int \rho (x_2) v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{a})} (-, y, +, x_2) e^{i \beta \phi (x_2)} \mathrm{d} x_2\\ = & \rho (y) i \beta \ell \left( \int \rho (x_2) v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{a})} (+, y, x_2, -) (e^{i \beta \phi (y)} e^{- i \beta \phi (x_2)} - e^{- i \beta \phi (y)} e^{i \beta \phi (x_2)}) \mathrm{d} x_2 \right) \end{array}\)

and by the neutrality condition, zeroth order Taylor expansion gives

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} g (y, x_2) & := & (e^{i \beta \phi (y)} e^{- i \beta \phi (x_2)} - e^{- i \beta \phi (y)} e^{i \beta \phi (x_2)}) | y - x_2 |^{- \varkappa}\\ & = & (1 - 1) - i \beta \frac{(x_2 - y)}{| y - x_2 |^{\varkappa}} \cdot \int_0^1 (e^{i \beta (\phi (y) - \phi (x_2^{\theta}))} + e^{- i \beta (\phi (y) - \phi (x_2^{\theta}))}) \nabla \phi (x_2^{\theta}) \mathrm{d} \vartheta \end{array}\)

where we denoted \(x_2^{\theta} := y + \vartheta (x_2 - y)\).

Thus Minkowski's integral inequality, Proposition 3.14 and Lemma 4.6 yields that for any \(p \in [1, \infty)\)

\(\displaystyle \| F^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t (\varphi_t) (y) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \unicode{x2980}v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \sum_{| \nu | = 1} \| \partial^{\nu} \varphi_t (x) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \lambda_t (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c .\)

It follows that for any \(\ell' \geq \tilde{\ell}\) we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| F^{[\leq \tilde{\ell}]}_t (\varphi_t) (y) - F^{[\leq \ell']}_t (\varphi_t) (y) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} & \leq & \sum_{\ell = \tilde{\ell} + 1}^{\ell'} \| F^{[\ell]}_t (\varphi_t) (y) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)}\\ & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{1 - \delta} \langle t \rangle^{- \delta \tilde{\ell}} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \end{array} \) (4.13)

which vanishes as \(t \rightarrow \infty\), since \(\tilde{\ell} + 1 > \delta^{- 1}\).

Recall that it follows from (2.20) that \(F^{[\ell]} = \mathrm{D} V^{[\ell]}\) satisfies the flow equation

\(\displaystyle \partial_t F^{[\ell]}_t + \frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\dot{G}_t} F^{[\ell]}_t = \frac{1}{2} \sum_{\ell' + \ell'' = \ell} \mathrm{D} \langle F^{[\ell']}_t, \dot{G}_t F^{[\ell'']}_t \rangle = \sum_{\ell' + \ell'' = \ell} \langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell']}_t, \dot{G}_t F^{[\ell'']}_t \rangle .\)

Then for any \(\ell^{\ast} \in \mathbb{N}\), we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} = & \left( \partial_s + \frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\dot{G}_s} \right) F_s^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]} - \langle \mathrm{D} F_s^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]} (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s (F^{[\leq 3]}_s (\varphi_s) + \tilde{R}_s) \rangle (y)\\ = & \sum_{\ell' + \ell'' \leq \ell^{\ast}} \langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell']}_s (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s F^{[\ell'']}_s (\varphi_s) \rangle (y) - \sum_{\ell' \leq \ell^{\ast}} \sum_{\ell'' \leq 3} \langle \mathrm{D} F_s^{[\ell']} (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s F^{[\ell'']}_s (\varphi_s) \rangle (y)\\ - & \langle \mathrm{D} F_s^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]} (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s \tilde{R}_s \rangle (y)\\ = & \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \ell'' \geq 4\\ \ell' + \ell'' \leq \ell^{\ast} \end{array}$}}} \langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell']}_s (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s F^{[\ell'']}_s (\varphi_s) \rangle (y) - \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \ell' \leq \ell^{\ast}, \ell'' \leq 3\\ \ell' + \ell'' \geq \ell^{\ast} + 1 \end{array}$}}} \langle \mathrm{D} F_s^{[\ell']} (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s F^{[\ell'']}_s (\varphi_s) \rangle (y)\\ - & \langle \mathrm{D} F_s^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]} (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s \tilde{R}_s \rangle (y) \end{array}\)

and plugging in (4.2), (4.5) and the bound on \(\tilde{R}\),

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \ell'' \geq 4\\ \ell' + \ell'' \leq \ell^{\ast} \end{array}$}}} \| \langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell']}_s (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s F^{[\ell'']}_s (\varphi_s) \rangle (y) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{- 5 \delta} \mathfrak{l}_t^c\\ \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \ell' \leq \ell^{\ast}, \ell'' \leq 3\\ \ell' + \ell'' \geq \ell^{\ast} + 1 \end{array}$}}} \| \langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell']}_s (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s F^{[\ell'']}_s (\varphi_s) \rangle (y) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{- (\ell^{\ast} + 1) \delta} \mathfrak{l}_t^c\\ \| \langle \mathrm{D} F_s^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]} (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s \tilde{R}_s \rangle (y) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{- 1 - \delta} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \end{array}\)

so that \(F_s^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]} (y)\) satisfies (2.10) provided \(\ell^{\ast} \geq \tilde{\ell} > \delta^{- 1} - 1\). Conditions 1. and 2. of Definition 2.5 are checked in a straightforward way, see Lemma 6.1 below. This proves item 1.

To prove item 2, note that it follows from item 1, in particular (4.13), that the reconstructed observable of the germ \(F^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]} (y)\) is the same as \(\tilde{F}_s (\varphi_s) + \tilde{R}_s\). Therefore, for any \(\ell^{\ast} \geq \tilde{\ell}\), we may use the germ \(F^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}\) in the generator \(\mathscr{L}^V\) and compute

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} R^{[> \ell^{\ast}]}_t & = & \mathbb{E}_t \int_t^{\infty} \mathscr{L}^V F_s^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]} (\varphi_s) \mathrm{d} s\\ & = & \mathbb{E}_t \int_t^{\infty} \left( \partial_s + \frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\dot{G}_s} \right) F_s^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]} - \langle \mathrm{D} F_s^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]} (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s (F^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_s (\varphi_s) + R^{[> \ell^{\ast}]}_s) \rangle \mathrm{d} s\\ & = & \mathbb{E}_t \int_t^{\infty} - \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \ell', \ell'' \leq \ell^{\ast}\\ \ell' + \ell'' > \ell^{\ast} \end{array}$}}} \langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell']}_s (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s F^{[\ell'']}_s (\varphi_s) \rangle - \sum_{\ell \leq \ell^{\ast}} \langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell]}_s (\varphi_s), \dot{G}_s R^{[> \ell^{\ast}]}_s \rangle \mathrm{d} s. \end{array} \) (4.14)

Now (4.2) yields

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \sum_{\ell \leq \ell^{\ast}} \| \langle \mathrm{D} F^{[\ell]}_t, \dot{G}_t R^{[> \ell^{\ast}]}_t \rangle \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})} & \lesssim & \sum_{\ell \leq \ell^{\ast}} \lambda_t (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \langle t \rangle^{- 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \| Q_t R^{[> \ell^{\ast}]}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})}\\ & \lesssim & \lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 2} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \| R^{[> \ell^{\ast}]}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})} \end{array}\)

where we used that the smoothing effect of the heat kernel forces the right-hand side of (4.14), hence \(R^{[> \ell^{\ast}]}_t\), to be function-valued. Plugging this bound and (4.5) into (4.14),

\(\displaystyle \| R^{[> \ell^{\ast}]}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})} \lesssim \int_t^{\infty} {\langle s \rangle^{- \delta (\ell^{\ast} + 1)}} \mathfrak{l}_s^c + \langle t \rangle^{- 1 - \delta} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \| R^{[> \ell^{\ast}]}_s \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})} \mathrm{d} s\)

hence by backward Grönwall

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| R^{[> \ell^{\ast}]}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})} & \lesssim & \int_t^{\infty} {\langle s \rangle^{- \delta (\ell^{\ast} + 1)}} \mathfrak{l}_s^c e^{\int_t^s \langle u \rangle^{- 1 - \delta} \mathfrak{l}_u^c \mathrm{d} u} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & {\langle t \rangle^{1 - \delta (\ell^{\ast} + 1)}} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \end{array} \) (4.15)

and (2.26) follows from Young's inequality and \(\| \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t \|_{L^1} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{- 2}\). When \(\ell^{\ast} < \tilde{\ell}\), combine the estimate just derived with (4.1).\(\Box\)

Similarly to the Taylor expansion for effective force in the previous proposition, we may obtain \(L^p (\Omega)\) bounds on \(V^{[\ell], T}_t (\varphi_t)\) that, however, depend on the volume cutoff \(\rho\). We will not use these estimates in the following and hence invite the reader to skip the remark below.

Remark 4.8. It holds for any \(p \in [1, \infty)\) uniformly in \(T\) that

\(\displaystyle \| V^{[\ell], T}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \| \rho \|_{L^1} \lambda_t (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c . \) (4.16)

Indeed, for \(\boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M} \setminus \mathfrak{M}_p\), we have via \(\sup_{x \in \mathbb{R}^d} | \exp (i \beta \phi (x)) | \leq 1\), \(\rho (x) \in [0, 1]\) and \(\omega_t^{\varsigma} \geq 1\) that

\(\displaystyle | V^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a}), T}_t (\phi) | \leq \int | v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) | \rho (x_{1 : \ell}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell} \lesssim \| \rho \|_{L^1} \unicode{x2980}v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta}\)

and the statement follows from Proposition 3.14.

For \(\boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M}_p\) with \(\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a}) = 0\) we obtain trivially \(| V^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a}), T}_t (\phi) | = | v^{[2] (0)}_{t, T} | = \unicode{x2980}v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta}\) and again conclude by Proposition 3.14. Given a neutral pair of vertex fields, Taylor expansion around \(x_1\) yields by the neutrality condition \(\sigma_1 = - \sigma_2\) that

\(\displaystyle \psi_{1 : 2} = 1 + i \beta \sigma_2 \nabla \phi (x_1) \cdot (x_2 - x_1) + \psi_1 \sum_{| \nu | = 2} \frac{2}{\nu !} (x_2 - x_1)^{\nu} \int_0^1 (1 - \vartheta) { \partial^{\nu} \psi |_{(\sigma_2, x_1 + \vartheta (x_2 - x_1))}} \mathrm{d} \vartheta \) (4.17)

thus for an \(\boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M}_p\), with \(\varkappa := \varkappa (\boldsymbol{a}) > 0\), we obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & | V^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a}), T}_t (\phi) |\\ = & \left| \int v^{[2] (\varkappa)}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : 2}) \rho (x_{1 : 2}) \frac{\psi_{\xi_1} \psi_{\xi_2} - 1}{| x_1 - x_2 |^{\varkappa}} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : 2} \right|\\ \leq & \left| \int v^{[2] (\varkappa)}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : 2}) \rho (x_{1 : 2}) \frac{i \beta \sigma_2 \nabla \phi (x_1) \cdot (x_2 - x_1)}{| x_1 - x_2 |^{\varkappa}} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : 2} \right|\\ + & \left| \int v^{[2] (\varkappa)}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : 2}) \rho (x_{1 : 2}) \psi_{\xi_1} \sum_{| \nu | = 2} \frac{2}{\nu !} \frac{(x_2 - x_1)^{\nu}}{| x_1 - x_2 |^{\varkappa}} \int_0^1 (1 - \vartheta) \left. \partial^{\nu} \psi \right|_{(\sigma_2, x_1 + \vartheta (x_2 - x_1))} \mathrm{d} \vartheta \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : 2} \right| \end{array}\)

with \(\varkappa \leq 2\). The first term is zero by the charge conjugation symmetry of \(v^{[2] (\varkappa)}_{t, T}\) (cf. Remark 3.11); hence by Minkowski's integral inequality, (3.20), Lemma 4.6

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| V^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a}), T}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} & \lesssim & \| \rho \|_{L^1} \unicode{x2980}v^{[2] (\varkappa)}_{t, T} | x_1 - x_2 |^{2 - \varkappa} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta} \sum_{| \nu | = 2} \| \partial^{\nu} \varphi_t \|_{L^p (\Omega)}\\ & \lesssim & \| \rho \|_{L^1} \unicode{x2980}v^{[2] (\varkappa)}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta} \langle t \rangle^{- \frac{2 - \varkappa}{2}} \langle t \rangle^1 \end{array}\)

and conclude from Proposition 3.14.

Note that the above argument with the first-order Taylor expansion in (4.17) allows for curing a divergence of order up to \(\varkappa \leq 2\). Since we restrict ourselves to \(\beta^2 < 6 \pi\), we could have replaced (4.17) with zeroth-order Taylor expansion to offset the divergence of order \(\varkappa = 1\); in this case we would not have had to rely on Remark 3.11.

5Observable flow equations

5.1Polynomial observables

In this section we construct all polynomial observables of the sine–Gordon field by carrying out the program of Section 2.6. Since the field \(\varphi_t (y)\) has only logarithmic blowup in the UV \(t \rightarrow \infty\), simple Wick ordering is sufficient to make sense of the observable-inserted correlation functions \(\langle \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} \phi^n (y) \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \star \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} \phi (x_1) \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \ldots \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} \phi (x_n) \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \rangle\) for any \(n \in \mathbb{N}\); in turn, this is already computed by level \(\ell = 1\) approximation in the flow equation. However, as we want to prove the prefactorization algebra property of the observables we construct (Theorem 1.2), and in the subsequent sections we will consider observables \(\mathfrak{Q} (z)\) of arbitrarily high superficial degree of divergence, we need to develop the perturbative approximation to arbitrary precision even for polynomials in order to conclude the existence of the products \(\langle \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} \phi^n (y) \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \star \mathfrak{Q} (z) \rangle\).

From now on, we fix \(y \in \mathbb{R}^d\), the point where the observable is localized. Using the notations of the previous section, we make the ansatz

\(\displaystyle q_t^{[\ell], T} (\phi) = \int \Gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} (\phi, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \) (5.1)

where we denoted \(\psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} := \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \rho (x_{1 : (\ell - 1)})\), and let

\(\displaystyle \Gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} (\phi, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) = \sum_{k = 0}^n \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \phi^k (y) \) (5.2)

with kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T}\) to be determined. That is, the start of our induction is the first-order representation

\(\displaystyle q_t^{[1], T} (\phi) = \sum_{k = 0}^n \gamma^{[1] (k)}_{t, T} (y) \phi^k (y)\)

and the periodic structure, i.e. dependence on the vertex fields \(\psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\) in \(q_t^{[\ell], T}\) enters only at \(\ell \geq 2\) iteration order through contact with the Mayer-expanded potential in the flow equation (2.19). It is straightforward to verify that the above representation is compatible with the structure of the flow equation (2.19). To this end, recall the definition of \(\dot{\mathbb{G}}_s\) from (3.2); we now also introduce

\(\displaystyle \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_I, y) := i \frac{\beta}{2} \sum_{j \in I} \sigma_j \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (y - x_j)\)

for \(I \subseteq \mathbb{N}\). Due to the nontrivial post-processing of the potential's basis, we also define

\(\displaystyle M^{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_I) := \mathbb{1}_{\boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} \setminus \mathfrak{M}_p} + \mathbb{1}_{\boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M}_p} \mathbb{1}_{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{b}) > 0} | x_{I (1)} - x_{I (2)} |^{- \varkappa (\boldsymbol{b})} . \) (5.3)

Finally, given a finite index set \(I \subseteq \mathbb{N}\) we denote by \(\xi_{I - 1}\) for the tuple obtained by omitting one index from \(I\). When such notation appears in the formulae below, each summand is understood after consistently relabeling all of its remaining variables.

Lemma 5.1. Let \(V^{[\ell]}\) be given by (3.31). Suppose that the kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T}\) satisfy the equations

\(\displaystyle \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) = e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{T, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) + \int_t^T e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \mathrm{d} s \) (5.4)

where

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \mathscr{S}^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y)\\ := & \frac{(k + 1) (k + 2)}{2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (0) \gamma^{[\ell] (k + 2)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y)\\ + & 2 (k + 1) \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \gamma^{[\ell] (k + 1)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y)\\ - & \sum_{I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell} (k + 1) C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |} \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = | I_2 | \end{array}$}}} (v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{I_2}, y) \gamma^{[| I_1 |] (k + 1)}_{s, T} (\xi_{I_1 - 1}, y)\\ - & \sum_{I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell} C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |} \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = | I_2 | \end{array}$}}} (v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{I_2}, \xi_{I_1 - 1}) \gamma^{[| I_1 |] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{I_1 - 1}, y) \end{array} \) (5.5)

for some positive combinatorial coefficients \(C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |}\). Then \(q_t^{[\ell], T}\), given by (5.1) and (5.2), satisfies the observable flow equation (2.19).

Proof. A straightforward computation; see Appendix B.2 for details.\(\Box\)

Imposing the boundary conditions

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{llll} \gamma^{[1] (k)}_{0, T} & := & \delta_{n, k} & \\ \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{T, T} & := & 0 & \operatorname{if} \ell \geq 2, \end{array} \) (5.6)

the above hierarchy of equations allows us to estimate the germ kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T}\) inductively using the already established bounds on the potential kernels. We will estimate in the norm

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta} := \int | \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) | \omega_t^{\Theta} (x_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \langle \operatorname{St} (x_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \rangle^{\eta} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \) (5.7)

where \(\omega_u^{\Theta}\) is given by (3.4) with the control chosen to be \(\Theta := \Theta (\ell) := 4^{- \ell}\) as before, and \(\eta \geq 0\) to be chosen later. The purpose of the weights \(\langle \operatorname{St} (x_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \rangle^{\eta}\) is to control the infinite volume limit.

Proposition 5.2. Let \(\beta^2 < 6 \pi\). Then there exist kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T}\) satisfying (5.4) with boundary conditions (5.6), and it holds for any \(\eta \geq 0\) that

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \lesssim (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \) (5.8)

for some \(c = c (n, k, \ell {,} \beta^2) \in \mathbb{R}_+\). Moreover, the \(\unicode{x2980} \cdot \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta}\)-limits

\(\displaystyle \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t := \lim_{T \rightarrow \infty} \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T}\)

exist, are independent of \(\eta\) and satisfy the bounds (5.8).

Proof. We proceed inductively in \(\ell, k\); the starting point is \((\ell, k) = (1, n)\); from which we descend \(k \rightarrow k - 1\) until \(k = 0\) is reached, from which we go to \((\ell, k) = (2, n)\) and so forth. As before, we do not track the log-dependence, hence the constant \(c \in \mathbb{R}_+\) in \(\mathfrak{l}_t^c\) may change from line to line.

Clearly for \(\ell = 1\) the flow equation computes the Gaussian renormalisation counterterms, hence by Lemma A.1

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[1] (k)}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (1), \eta} = | \gamma^{[1] (k)}_{t, T} (y) | \lesssim_{n, k} 1 + \mathcal{G}_t (0)^n \lesssim \mathfrak{l}_t^c .\)

For the inductive step, we can bound the source term as follows. Of course

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rrrrr} \unicode{x2980} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (0) \gamma^{[\ell] (k + 2)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta, \eta} & \lesssim & \| \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s \|_{L^{\infty}} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (k + 2)}_{s, T} \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta, \eta} & \lesssim & \langle s \rangle^{- 1} (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c\\ \unicode{x2980} \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \gamma^{[\ell] (k + 1)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \unicode{x2980}_{s {,} \Theta, \eta} & \lesssim & \| \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s \|_{L^{\infty}} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (k + 1)}_{s, T} \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta, \eta} & \lesssim & \langle s \rangle^{- 1} (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \end{array}\)

For the terms involving the potential kernels, note that for nontrivial \(M_{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_{I_2})\) we can use the technology of Lemma 3.12. Indeed, from (3.25) with \(c := 2 \cdot 4^{- \ell}\) and (3.29) we see

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \unicode{x2980}v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} (\xi_{I_2}) M^{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_{I_2}) \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{I_2}, \xi_{I_1 - 1}) \gamma^{[| I_1 |] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{I_1 - 1}, y) \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- \ell}, \eta}\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980}v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 3 \cdot 4^{- \ell}, \eta} \langle s \rangle^{- 2 + \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{b})}{2}} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[| I_1 |] (k)}_{s, T} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- \ell}, \eta}\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980}v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- (\ell - 1)}, \eta} \langle s \rangle^{- 2 + \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{b})}{2}} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[| I_1 |] (k)}_{s, T} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- \ell}, \eta}\\ \lesssim & \lambda_s (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{| I_2 | - 1} \langle s \rangle^{- 2} (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{| I_1 | - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \end{array}\)

since \(| I_2 | \leq \ell - 1\) apart from the trivial case \(| I_2 | = \ell\) when \(| I_1 | = 0\) so that \(q^{[0]} \equiv 0\). The remaining source term is bounded similarly via a straightforward modification of (3.25) to \(\dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{1 : 2}, y)\):

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \unicode{x2980}v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} (\xi_{I_2}) M^{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_{I_2}) \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{I_2}, y) \gamma^{[| I_1 |] (k + 1)}_{s, T} (\xi_{I_1 - 1}, y) \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- \ell}, \eta}\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980}v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- (\ell - 1)}, \eta} \langle s \rangle^{- 2 + \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{b})}{2}} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[| I_1 |] (k + 1)}_{s, T} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- \ell}, \eta}\\ \lesssim & \lambda_s (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{| I_2 | - 1} \langle s \rangle^{- 2} (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{| I_1 | - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c . \end{array}\)

Combining these estimates and inserting them to (5.5) and (5.4), we obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta} & \leq & \int_t^{\infty} \| e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \|_{L^{\infty}} \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \int_t^{\infty} \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta, \eta} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \int_t^{\infty} (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \langle s \rangle^{- 1} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \end{array}\)

by the positive definiteness bound \(\| e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \|_{L^{\infty}} \lesssim 1\) that holds when \(t \leq s\). This completes the induction.

The claim about the \(\unicode{x2980} \cdot \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta}\)-limits follows along the lines of Corollary 3.15. Indeed, we claim that the limiting kernels are solutions to the equations

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{llll} \gamma^{[\ell] (n)}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) & = & 1 & \operatorname{if} \ell = 1, k = n\\ \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) & = & - \int_0^t e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, \infty} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \mathrm{d} s & \operatorname{if} \ell = 1, k < n\\ \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) & = & \int_t^{\infty} e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, \infty} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \mathrm{d} s & \operatorname{if} \ell \geq 2 \end{array} \) (5.9)

where \(\mathscr{S}^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, \infty}\) are source terms obtained by replacing the \(\gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T}\) in (5.5) with the limits \(\gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t\); this is well-defined since the system is triangular in \((\ell, k)\). One then may see inductively that there exists a sufficiently small \(\varepsilon > 0\), depending only on \(\beta\), such that

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t - \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \lesssim (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon} .\)
\(\Box\)

Finally, we can show that the UV-complete germ is indeed a good approximate solution of the observable flow equation, i.e. we verify rigorously the estimate (2.27). Define

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi) & := & \int \Gamma^{[\ell]}_t (\phi, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ \Gamma^{[\ell]}_t (\phi, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) & := & \sum_{k = 0}^n \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \phi^k (y) \end{array} \) (5.10)

where the UV limit kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t\) were constructed in Proposition 5.2.

Proposition 5.3. Suppose \(\beta^2 < 6 \pi\), and let \(\gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{t, T}\) be as in Proposition 5.2. \(q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t := \sum_{\ell \leq \ell^{\ast}} q_t^{[\ell]}\), where \(q_t^{[\ell]}\) is given by (5.10), is in the space \(\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, 0, y}^{0, \ell^{\ast} \delta}\).

Proof. The boundedness of the complex exponential and \(1 \leq \omega_t^{\Theta} (x_I)\) yields

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi) & = & \int \sum_{k = 0}^n \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \phi^k (y) \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ & \leq & \sum_{k = 0}^n \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} | \phi^k (y) |, \end{array}\)

so we deduce from (4.9) and (5.8) that for all \(\ell \geq 1\) there exists a \(c = c (n, k, \ell, \beta^2) \in \mathbb{R}_+\) such that for any \(p \in [1, \infty)\),

\(\displaystyle \| q_t^{[\ell]} (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c . \) (5.11)

Since the UV limiting kernels satisfy (5.9), adapting the proof of Lemma 5.1 yields that \(q^{[\ell]}\) satisfy the observable flow equation (2.19). Thus Lemma 2.19 applies, and computing

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \langle \mathrm{D} q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi), \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \rangle\\ = & \int \sum_{k = 0}^n \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) k \phi^{k - 1} (y) \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t (y) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ + & \int \sum_{k = 0}^n \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \phi^k (y) \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} i \beta \sum_{j \in 1 : (\ell - 1)} \sigma_j \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t (x_j) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ \lesssim & \sum_{k = 0}^n \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} (| \phi^{k - 1} (y) | + | \phi^k (y) |) \| \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})}, \end{array}\)

we obtain from the estimates (5.8), (4.9) and (2.26) that

\(\displaystyle \| \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{- \ell^{\ast} \delta - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c . \) (5.12)

Combining (5.11) and (5.12) then yields the statement.\(\Box\)

5.2Vertex fields in the optimal Wick regime

Let \(y \in \mathbb{R}^d\) be the point the observable is localized; let \(\alpha \in \mathbb{C}\) and denote by

\(\displaystyle \tilde{\psi}_y := \exp (i \alpha \phi (y)) .\)

As we will see at the end of this section (Corollary 5.8), the real part of \(\alpha\) contains all difficulties and extending the results in the imaginary direction is straightforward. Since treating the general complex case from the beginning would only clutter the notation, we temporarily restrict ourselves to \(\alpha \in \mathbb{R}\).

Our ansatz for the vertex field germ is

\(\displaystyle q_t^{[\ell], T} (\phi) := \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \) (5.13)

The key to controlling the flow equation for the vertex field is understanding the action of the functional Laplacian \(\Delta_{\dot{G}_t}\) on the field content \(\tilde{\psi}_y \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\). To motivate the discussion below, let us consider the example of the \(\ell = 2\) case explicitly. Then

\(\displaystyle \frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\dot{G}_t} [\tilde{\psi}_y \psi_1] = - \left( \frac{\alpha^2}{2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) + \frac{\beta^2}{2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) + \alpha \beta \sigma_1 \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x_1 - y) \right) \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_1\)

It is clear that the sign of \(\alpha \beta \sigma_1\) influences the behaviour crucially. When \(t \leq s\), it holds that \(- \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_1 - y) \leq 0\), hence the off-diagonal part has \(L^{\infty}\) bounds

\(\displaystyle \sup_{x_1} e^{- \alpha \beta \sigma_1 \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_1 - y)} \lesssim \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} 1 & \operatorname{if} \quad \alpha \beta \sigma_1 \geq 0\\ e^{- \alpha \beta \sigma_1 \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)} & \operatorname{if} \quad \alpha \beta \sigma_1 \leq 0 \end{array} \right. .\)

That is, when \(\operatorname{sgn} (\alpha) = \sigma_1\), the external vertex particle \(\tilde{\psi}_y\) and the internal \(\psi_1\) repel each other, behaving like the charged 2-point case \(\psi_{1 : 2}\) of the potential, yielding much better bounds than the case \(\operatorname{sgn} (\alpha) \neq \sigma_1\), in which case \(\tilde{\psi}_y\) and \(\psi_1\) attract each other and form a dipole. As such, to simplify notation, we assume from now on, without loss of generality, that \(\alpha \geq 0\) (so if \(\alpha \leq 0\) everything can be done verbatim, with the straightforward modification of some signs).

Evaluating the functional Laplacian on the field content \(\tilde{\psi}_y \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\) of (5.13) results in

\(\displaystyle \dot{\mathbf{G}}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) := \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) + \left( - \frac{\alpha^2}{2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) \right) + \left( - \alpha \beta \sum_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \sigma_j \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x_j - y) \right) . \) (5.14)

We may think of \(\dot{\mathbf{G}}_t (y, \xi_I)\) as the entrywise sum of a “covariance matrix” \([\dot{\mathbf{G}}_t (y, \xi_I)]\) which factorizes as

\(\displaystyle [\dot{\mathbf{G}}_t (y, \xi_I)] = \left[ \begin{array}{cc} - \frac{\alpha^2}{2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) & (\dot{\mathbb{G}}_t^{(\alpha)} (\xi_I))^{\top}\\ \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t^{(\alpha)} (\xi_I) & [\dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_I)] \end{array} \right] \in \mathbb{C}^{(1 + | I |) \times (1 + | I |)}\)

where \([\dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_I)] \in \mathbb{C}^{| I | \times | I |}\) is the usual covariance matrix of sine–Gordon, i.e. \([\dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_I)]_{j, k} := - \frac{\beta^2}{2} \sigma_j \sigma_k \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (x_j - x_k)\), and the off-diagonal vector \(\dot{\mathbb{G}}_t^{(\alpha)} (\xi_I)\), given by \(\)\([\dot{\mathbb{G}}_t^{(\alpha)} (\xi_I)]_j := - \frac{\alpha \beta}{2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t \sigma_j (x_j - y)\), encodes the interaction effect of perturbing the charge structure of sine–Gordon with the external vertex field \(\exp (i \alpha \phi (y))\). The strength of this perturbation depends on the relation \(\alpha \lessgtr \beta\) and we expect the two cases to require different ideas to control.

When \(\alpha > \beta\) we expect that adding \(\exp (i \alpha \phi (y))\) destroys the usual charge structure, so that the interaction is dominated by the off-diagonal perturbation term \(\dot{\mathbb{G}}_t^{(\alpha)} (\xi_I)\). On the other hand, when \(\alpha < \beta\), we expect the perturbation to be irrelevant and \(\dot{\mathbf{G}}_t (y, \xi_I)\) to behave similarly to the sine–Gordon potential, i.e. governed by the bulk covariance \([\dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_I)]\). This observation is leveraged in the following

Lemma 5.4. (external vertex screening) Let \(0 \leq \alpha, \beta\), suppose \(t \leq s\). Then denoting by \(\chi := \chi (\xi_{1 : \ell})\) the global charge, we have

\(\displaystyle \sup_{x_{1 : \ell}} e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : \ell})} \lesssim_{\ell} e^{- \frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)} {(\lambda_t \lambda_s^{- 1})^{K_{\ell}}} e^{\left( | \chi | + \frac{K_{\ell} - | \chi |}{2} \right) \alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)} \) (5.15)

with \(K_{\ell} := | \chi |\) if \(\alpha \leq \beta\) and \(K_{\ell} := \ell\) if \(\beta \leq \alpha\).

Proof. First suppose that the ensemble \(\xi_{1 : \ell}\) is purely charged, i.e. \(| \chi | = \ell\). By positivity of \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_u\)

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : \ell})\\ = & - \frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0) + \mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) - \alpha \beta \sum_{j \in 1 : \ell} \sigma_j \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_j - y)\\ = & - \frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0) - \frac{\beta^2}{2} \sum_{j \in 1 : \ell} \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0) - \frac{\beta^2}{2} \sum_{j \neq k \in 1 : \ell} \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_j - x_k) - \alpha \beta \sum_{j \in 1 : \ell} \sigma_j \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_j - y)\\ \leq & - \frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0) - \frac{\ell}{2} \beta^2 \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0) + \ell \alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0) . \end{array} \) (5.16)

yielding the statement. Hence from now on we assume that \(| \chi | \leq \ell - 1\) so that there exists at least one neutral pair; denote by \(k_+, k_-\) the index of a closest of such pairs, i.e. a pair \(\eta := \{ (+ 1, x_{k_+}), (- 1, x_{k_-}) \} \in (\{ - 1, 1 \} \times \mathbb{R}^2)^2\) such that

\(\displaystyle | x_{k_+} - x_{k_-} | = \min_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} j_+, j_- : \sigma_{j_+} \sigma_{j_-} = - 1 \end{array}$}}} | x_{j_+} - x_{j_-} | .\)

By possibly relabeling the points, we distinguish the purely charged core and the ensemble of neutral pairs with \(\eta\) being the last, i.e. we assume \(\sigma_1, \ldots, \sigma_{| \chi |} =\operatorname{sgn} (\chi)\) and \(\sigma_{| \chi | + j} = - \sigma_{| \chi | + j + 1}\) for all \(j \in 1, \ldots, \ell - | \chi | - 1\) and \((k_-, k_+) = (\ell - 1, \ell)\). Then split

\(\displaystyle \mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : \ell}) =\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 2)}) + 2 \mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 2)}, \eta) + \mathscr{C}_{t, s} (\eta, y) \) (5.17)

where

\(\displaystyle \mathscr{C}_{t, s} (\eta, y) := {\color{blue}{\beta^2 \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_{k_+} - x_{k_-}) - \beta^2 \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)}} + {\color{red}{\alpha \beta (\mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_{k_-} - y) - \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_{k_+} - y))}}\)

We show that the coloured term, governed by the triangle \(x_{k_+}, x_{k_-}, y\), is bounded uniformly by

\(\displaystyle \mathscr{C}_{t, s} (\eta, y) \lesssim \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} C & \operatorname{if} \alpha \leq \beta\\ \alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0) - \beta^2 \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0) & \operatorname{if} \beta \leq \alpha . \end{array} \right.\)

  1. If the edge \(x_{k_+} - y\) is a shortest, then the red term is nonpositive, and the blue term is nonpositive as usual.

  2. If the edge \(x_{k_+} - x_{k_-}\) is a shortest, then we can bound the red term with Lemma 3.5, while the blue term is nonpositive as usual.

  3. If the edge \(x_{k_-} - y\) is a shortest, then

    1. when \(\alpha \leq \beta\), since the red term in \(\mathscr{C}_{t, s}\) is nonnegative,

      \(\displaystyle \mathscr{C}_{t, s} (\eta, y) \leq \beta^2 ({\color{red}{\mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_{k_-} - y)}} - {\color{blue}{\mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)}}) + \beta^2 ({\color{blue}{\mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_{k_+} - x_{k_-})}} - {\color{red}{\mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_{k_+} - y)}})\)

      where now the first difference is nonpositive, and the second difference is estimated via Lemma 3.5 with the prescription \(z := x_{k_+}\), \(x_b := x_{k_-}\), \(x_g := y\).

    2. when \(\beta \leq \alpha\), then \(- \alpha \beta \leq - \beta^2\), so

      \(\displaystyle \mathscr{C}_{t, s} (\eta, y) \leq {\color{red}{\alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_{k_-} - y)}} - {\color{blue}{\beta^2 \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)}} + \beta^2 ({\color{blue}{\mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_{k_+} - x_{k_-})}} - {\color{red}{\mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_{k_+} - y)}})\)

      and the first difference is bounded by

      \(\displaystyle {\color{red}{\alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)}} - {\color{blue}{\beta^2 \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)}},\)

      while the second difference is estimated via Lemma 3.5 with the prescription \(z := x_{k_+}\), \(x_b := x_{k_-}\), \(x_g := y\).

The term \(\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 2)}, \eta)\) is treated as in the proof of Lemma 3.6: since by construction

\(\displaystyle | x_{k_+} - x_{k_-} | \leq | \mathbb{1}_{\sigma_j = 1} x_{k_-} + \mathbb{1}_{\sigma_j = - 1} x_{k_+} - x_j |\)

for all \(j \in 1 : (\ell - 2)\), we may apply Lemma 3.5 to estimate

\(\displaystyle 2 \mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 2)}, \eta) = - \beta^2 \sum_{j = 1}^{\ell - 2} \sigma_j (\mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_{k_+} - x_j) - \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_{k_-} - x_j)) \lesssim_{\ell} C.\)

Thus from (5.17) we obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : \ell})} & \lesssim_{\ell} & e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 2)}) + \mathbb{1}_{\beta \leq \alpha} (\alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0) - \beta^2 \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0))}\\ & = & e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 2)})} ((\lambda_t \lambda_s^{- 1})^2 e^{\alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)})^{\mathbb{1}_{\beta \leq \alpha}} \end{array}\)

and repeat the above extraction procedure inductively until only the purely charged core \(\tilde{\xi}_{1 : | \chi |}\) remains for which we have seen in (5.16) the bound

\(\displaystyle e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \tilde{\xi}_{1 : | \chi |})} \lesssim e^{- \frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)} (\lambda_t \lambda_s^{- 1})^{| \chi |} e^{| \chi | \alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)} .\)
\(\Box\)

Note that the above bounds are not the sharpest in the sense that only the “bad” negative charges \(\sigma_j = - 1\) contribute to divergence in the \(\alpha \beta \sigma_j \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_j - y)\) term; it remains bounded for the “good” positive ones \(\sigma_j = 1\).

In light of the previous sections it is now a standard exercise to derive the observable flow equation of the kernels of the ansatz (5.13).

Lemma 5.5. Let \(V^{[\ell]}\) be given by (3.31). Suppose that the kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\) satisfy the equations

\(\displaystyle \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) = e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, T} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \gamma^{[\ell]}_{T, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) + \int_t^T e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \mathrm{d} s \) (5.18)

where \(\dot{\mathbf{G}}_t\) is defined in (5.14) and

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} - & \mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y)\\ = & \sum_{I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell} C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |} \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = | I_2 | \end{array}$}}} (v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) i \alpha \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{I_2}, y) \gamma^{[| I_1 |]}_{s, T} (y, \xi_{I_1 - 1})\\ + & \sum_{I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell} C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |} \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = | I_2 | \end{array}$}}} (v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{I_2}, \xi_{I_1 - 1}) \gamma^{[| I_1 |]}_{s, T} (y, \xi_{I_1 - 1}) \end{array} \) (5.19)

for some positive combinatorial coefficients \(C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |}\). Then \(q_t^{[\ell], T}\), given by (5.13), satisfies the observable flow equation (2.19).

Proof. Straightforward; similar to Lemma 5.1.\(\Box\)

Imposing the boundary conditions

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{llll} \gamma^{[1]}_{0, T} & := & 1 & \\ \gamma^{[\ell]}_{T, T} & := & 0 & \operatorname{if} \ell \geq 2, \end{array} \) (5.20)

allows us to estimate inductively the germ kernels in the norms (5.7):

Proposition 5.6. Let \(\beta^2 < 6 \pi\) and \(0 \leq \alpha < 4 \pi / \beta\). Then there exist kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\) satisfying (5.18) with boundary conditions (5.20), and for any \(\eta \geq 0\) it holds that

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi}} (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \) (5.21)

for some \(c = c (\ell, \beta^2) \in \mathbb{R}_+\). Moreover, the \(\unicode{x2980} \cdot \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta}\)-limits

\(\displaystyle \gamma^{[\ell]}_t := \lim_{T \rightarrow \infty} \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\)

exist, are independent of \(\eta\) and satisfy the bounds (5.21).

Proof. The boundary condition (5.20) at \(\ell = 1\) computes Wick ordering, so that by Lemma A.1

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma_{t, T}^{[1]} (y) \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (1), \eta} = e^{\frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)} \sup_y \left| e^{\varsigma \sqrt{t} 0} \right| \asymp e^{\frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi} \log (1 \vee t)} \asymp \langle t \rangle^{\frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi}},\)

verifying (5.21). When \(\ell \geq 2\), to estimate the source term, we use the inductive hypothesis, the potential bound (3.29), and for nontrivial \(M^{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_{I_2})\) the technology of Lemma 3.12. Indeed, from (3.25) with \(c := 2 \cdot 4^{- \ell}\)

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \unicode{x2980}v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} (\xi_{I_2}) M^{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_{I_2}) \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{I_2}, \xi_{I_1 - 1}) \gamma^{[| I_1 |]}_{s, T} (\xi_{I_1 - 1}, y) \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- \ell}, \eta}\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980}v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 3 \cdot 4^{- \ell}, \eta} \langle s \rangle^{- 2 + \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{b})}{2}} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[| I_1 |]}_{s, T} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- \ell}, \eta}\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980}v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- (\ell - 1)}, \eta} \langle s \rangle^{- 2 + \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{b})}{2}} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[| I_1 |]}_{s, T} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- \ell}, \eta}\\ \lesssim & \lambda_s (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{| I_2 | - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \langle s \rangle^{- 2} \langle s \rangle^{\frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi}} (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{| I_1 | - 1} \end{array}\)

since \(| I_2 | \leq \ell - 1\) apart from the trivial case \(| I_2 | = \ell\) when \(| I_1 | = 0\) so that \(q^{[0]} \equiv 0\). Similarly, via a straightforward modification of (3.25) to \(\dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{1 : 2}, y)\)

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \unicode{x2980}v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} (\xi_{I_2}) M^{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_{I_2}) i \alpha \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{I_2}, y) \gamma^{[| I_1 |]}_{s, T} (\xi_{I_1 - 1}, y) \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- \ell}, \eta}\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980}v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- (\ell - 1)}, \eta} \langle s \rangle^{- 2 + \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{b})}{2}} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[| I_1 |]}_{s, T} \unicode{x2980}_{s, 4^{- \ell}, \eta}\\ \lesssim & \lambda_s (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{| I_2 | - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \langle s \rangle^{- 2} \langle s \rangle^{\frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi}} (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{| I_1 | - 1} . \end{array}\)

It follows from (5.19) that the source term satisfies

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta, \eta} \lesssim \langle s \rangle^{\frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi}} (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \langle s \rangle^{- 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c . \) (5.22)

Inserting this and the screening bound (5.15) into (5.18) with the boundary condition (5.20) and denoting by \(\chi := \chi (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})\) the global charge yields

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta}\\ \lesssim & \int_t^{\infty} \| e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \|_{L^{\infty}} \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta, \eta} \mathrm{d} s\\ \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi}} \int_t^{\infty} \langle s \rangle^{- \frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi}} {(\lambda_t \lambda_s^{- 1})^{K_{\ell - 1}}} e^{\left( | \chi | + \frac{K_{\ell - 1} - | \chi |}{2} \right) \alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)} \langle s \rangle^{\frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi}} (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \langle s \rangle^{- 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \mathrm{d} s\\ \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi}} (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \end{array}\)

provided the integral converges. To see that it does, we need to show (cf. Lemma A.1) that

\(\displaystyle \Sigma_{\sigma} := - (1 - \delta) K_{\ell - 1} + \left( | \chi | + \frac{K_{\ell - 1} - | \chi |}{2} \right) \frac{\alpha \beta}{4 \pi} - \delta (\ell - 1) < 0.\)

Now by Lemma 5.4, either \(K_{\ell - 1} = \ell - 1\), in which case \(| \chi | + \frac{K_{\ell - 1} - | \chi |}{2} \leq \ell - 1\), so by the Wick regime assumption \(\alpha \beta / 4 \pi < 1\)

\(\displaystyle \Sigma_{\sigma} < - (1 - \delta) (\ell - 1) + \ell - 1 - \delta (\ell - 1) = 0 ;\)

or \(K_{\ell - 1} = | \chi |\); if so, then \(| \chi | = 0\) is trivial, and otherwise

\(\displaystyle \Sigma_{\sigma} < - (1 - \delta) | \chi | + | \chi | - \delta (\ell - 1) = \delta (| \chi | - (\ell - 1)) \leq 0.\)

Taking the \(T \rightarrow \infty\) limit is done similarly to the proof of Proposition 5.2: it is seen inductively that the limits solve

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{llll} \gamma^{[1]}_t (y) & = & e^{\frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)} & \\ \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) & = & \int_t^{\infty} e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, \infty} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \mathrm{d} s & \operatorname{if} \ell \geq 2, \end{array} \) (5.23)

where \(\mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, \infty}\) are obtained by replacing the \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\) in (5.19) with the limits \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_t\), by propagating inductively the bound

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_t - \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi}} (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon}\)

for some \(\varepsilon > 0\) small enough.\(\Box\)

As we have mentioned, in the case \(\beta \leq \alpha\) the strong influence of the vertex field insertion destroys the usual charge structure of the covariance matrix. This observation allows for an alternative proof of the above proposition when the sine–Gordon potential is in its Wick regime \(\beta^2 < 4 \pi\), which does not rely on the external screening mechanism of Lemma 5.4 and operates only on a naive estimate on \(\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : \ell})\). This alternative proof is elaborated in the following side remark; we do not use the ideas presented there in the subsequent sections and hence invite the reader to skip it.

Remark 5.7. (Alternative construction when \(\beta^2 < 4 \pi\)) As promised, we will not use Lemma 5.4 here. Instead, denoting by

\(\displaystyle \sharp_- := \sum_{j \in I} \mathbb{1}_{\sigma_j = - 1}, \hspace{4em} \sharp_+ := \sum_{j \in I} \mathbb{1}_{\sigma_j = + 1},\)

from (5.14) we deduce the following naive estimate: when \(t \leq s\), and \(\alpha, \beta \geq 0\), we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \sup_{x_{1 : \ell}} e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : \ell})} & \leq & e^{- \frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)} (\sup_{x_{1 : \ell}} e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : \ell})}) \left( \sup_{x_{1 : \ell}} e^{- \alpha \beta \sum_{j = 1}^{\ell} \sigma_j \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_j - y)} \right)\\ & \lesssim & e^{- \frac{\alpha^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)} (\lambda_t \lambda_s^{- 1})^{| \chi (\xi_{1 : \ell}) |} e^{\sharp_- \alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)} ; \end{array} \) (5.24)

where the bound on the \(\mathbb{G}_{t, s}\) sector has been proven in (3.12), and for the bound on the last term note that as \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_u \geq 0\), we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{llllll} \sup_x \exp (- \alpha \beta \sigma \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x - y)) & = & \sup_x \exp (- \alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x - y)) & = & 1 & \qquad \operatorname{if} \sigma = 1\\ \sup_x \exp (- \alpha \beta \sigma \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x - y)) & = & \sup_x \exp (\alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x - y)) & = & {e^{\alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)}} & \qquad \operatorname{if} \sigma = - 1 \end{array}\)

so that only the terms with \(\sigma_j = - 1\) contribute. Inserting (5.24) into (5.18), denoting by \(\chi := \chi (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})\) the global charge and relying on the inductive estimates (5.22) on the source term derived in the proof of the above Proposition, we arrive at

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta} & \lesssim & \int_t^{\infty} \| e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \|_{L^{\infty}} \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta, \eta} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \lambda_t^{| \chi |} \langle t \rangle^{- \sharp_- \frac{\alpha \beta}{4 \pi}} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{\alpha^2}{8 \pi}} \int_t^{\infty} \lambda_s^{- | \chi |} \langle s \rangle^{\sharp_- \frac{\alpha \beta}{4 \pi}} (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \langle s \rangle^{- 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \mathrm{d} s. \end{array}\)

To finish the induction, it remains to show that this integral converges, for which we need

\(\displaystyle \Sigma_{\ell, \delta} := - (1 - \delta) | \chi | + \sharp_- \frac{\alpha \beta}{4 \pi} - \delta (\ell - 1) < 0\)

for all \(\ell \geq 2\). When \(\beta^2 < 4 \pi\), this in turn follows from simple charge algebra. Indeed, since \(\sharp_+ + \sharp_- = (\ell - 1)\) and \(\sharp_+ - \sharp_- = \chi\), we have

\(\displaystyle \sharp_- = (\ell - 1) - \sharp_+ = (\ell - 1) - \sharp_- - \chi,\)

so

\(\displaystyle 2 \sharp_- = (\ell - 1) - \chi \leq (\ell - 1) + | \chi | .\)

Then denoting by \(R := \frac{\alpha \beta}{4 \pi} < 1\),

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \Sigma_{\ell, \delta} & \leq & - (1 - \delta) | \chi | + R \frac{| \chi | + (\ell - 1)}{2} - \delta (\ell - 1)\\ & = & | \chi | \left( \frac{R}{2} - 1 + \delta \right) + (\ell - 1) \left( \frac{R}{2} - \delta \right)\\ & = & | \chi | \left( \frac{R}{2} - \frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{2} + \delta \right) + (\ell - 1) \left( \frac{R}{2} - \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{2} - \delta \right)\\ & = & ((\ell - 1) + | \chi |) \left( \frac{R - 1}{2} \right) + ((\ell - 1) - | \chi |) \left( \frac{1}{2} - \delta \right) \end{array}\)
