and the statement \(\Sigma_{\ell, \delta} < 0\) follows from \(\beta^2 < 4 \pi \Leftrightarrow \delta > 1 / 2\) and the fact that \((\ell - 1) - | \chi |\) and \((\ell - 1) + | \chi |\) cannot be both zero for \(\ell \geq 2\). This finishes the alternative construction.

As we have noted in the beginning of the section, it is straightforward to extend the construction from real \(\alpha\) to complex.

Corollary 5.8. Let \(\beta^2 < 6 \pi\) and \(\alpha \in \mathbb{C}\) with real part \(| \Re (\alpha) | < 4 \pi / \beta\). Then the analogue of Proposition 5.6 holds, namely we have

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} {\lesssim e^{\frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)}} {e^{- \frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)}} (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \) (5.25)

for some \(c = c (\ell, \beta^2) \in \mathbb{R}_+\).

Proof. For \(\alpha = \Re (\alpha) + i \Im (\alpha) \in \mathbb{C}\), we may write

\(\displaystyle \tilde{\psi}_y = e^{i \Re (\alpha) \phi (y)} e^{- \Im (\alpha) \phi (y)}\)

so that a simple computation gives that Wick ordering is now

\(\displaystyle \gamma_{t, T}^{[1]} {= e^{\frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)}} {e^{- \frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)}} e^{i \Re (\alpha) \Im (\alpha) \mathcal{G}_t (0)}\)

verifying the inductive bound (5.25) for \(\ell = 1\). The action of the functional Laplacian is now

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \dot{\mathbf{G}}_t^{\mathbb{C}} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) & = & \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) + \left( - \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) \right) + \left( - \Re (\alpha) \beta \sum_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \sigma_j \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x_j - y) \right)\\ & + & \frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) + \left( - i \Im (\alpha) \beta \sum_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \sigma_j \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x_j - y) \right) + (- i \Im (\alpha) \Re (\alpha) \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0)) \end{array} \) (5.26)

so that bounding trivially

\(\displaystyle \left| \exp \left( - i \Im (\alpha) \left( \beta \sum_j \sigma_j \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_j - y) + \Re (\alpha) \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) \right) \right) \right| \leq 1\)

we get

\(\displaystyle \| e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s}^{\mathbb{C}}} \|_{L^{\infty}} \leq \| e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s}} \|_{L^{\infty}} e^{\frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (0)}\)

and adapting the end of the proof of Proposition 5.6 propagates the inductive hypothesis (5.25) via the screening estimate (5.15) on \(e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s}}\) and source term bound obtained just as (5.22).\(\Box\)

Having constructed the UV-complete kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_t\), we may define

\(\displaystyle q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi) := \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \) (5.27)

Proposition 5.9. Suppose \(\beta^2 < 6 \pi\) and \(\alpha \in \mathbb{C}\) with real part \(| \Re (\alpha) | < \frac{4 \pi}{\beta}\). Then \(q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t := \sum_{\ell \leq \ell^{\ast}} q_t^{[\ell]}\), where \(q_t^{[\ell]}\) is given by (5.27), is in the space \(\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, i \alpha, y}^{\kappa, \sigma}\) with \(\kappa := \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2 - \Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi}\), \(\sigma := \ell^{\ast} \delta - \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2 - \Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi}\).

Proof. The boundedness of the complex exponential and \(1 \leq \omega_t^{\Theta} (x_I)\) yields

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} e^{- \langle i \alpha, \phi (y) \rangle} q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi) & = & \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) e^{- i \alpha \phi (y)} e^{i \alpha \phi (y)} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ & \leq & \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \end{array}\)

hence (5.25) yields that for all \(\ell \geq 1\) there exists a \(c = c (\ell, \beta^2) \in \mathbb{R}_+\) such that for any \(p \geq 1\)

\(\displaystyle \| e^{- \langle i \alpha, \varphi_t (y) \rangle} q_t^{[\ell]} (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{\Re (\alpha)^2 - \Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi}} (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c . \) (5.28)

Since the UV limiting kernels satisfy (5.23), it follows just as Lemma 5.5 that \(q^{[\ell]}\) satisfy the observable flow equation (2.19). Thus Lemma 2.19 applies, and computing

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & e^{- \langle i \alpha, \phi (y) \rangle} \langle \mathrm{D} q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi), \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \rangle\\ = & i \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \left( \alpha \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t (y) + \beta \sum_{j \in 1 : (\ell - 1)} \sigma_j \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t (x_j) \right) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \| \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \|_{L^{\infty}}, \end{array}\)

we obtain from the estimates (5.25) and (2.26) that

\(\displaystyle \| e^{- \langle i \alpha, \varphi_t (y) \rangle} \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{\Re (\alpha)^2 - \Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} - \ell^{\ast} \delta - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c . \) (5.29)

Combining (5.28) and (5.29) yields the statement.\(\Box\)

5.3Derivative fields

The polynomial local observables have very mild superficial degree of divergence, hence we could flow backwards at all levels \(\ell \geq 2\) in the representation (5.1). This is not the case for sufficiently high derivatives of the field anymore and new ideas are needed to solve the flow equation. To see why, let us compute the source term with the Wick ordered first-order germ \(q^{[1]}_t (\phi) := \partial^{\mu} \phi (y)\) with \(y \in \mathbb{R}^d\) and \(\mu \in \mathbb{N}_0^d\) multiindex. It is

\(\displaystyle \langle \mathrm{D} q^{[1]}_t, \dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} V^{[1]}_t \rangle = i \beta \int \sigma_1 \partial^{\mu}_y \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - x_1) v^{[1]}_t \rho (x_1) \psi_1 \mathrm{d} \xi_1 .\)

Then it is clear from the bound \(\)\(\| \partial^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t \|_{L^1_{\eta}} \lesssim \langle s \rangle^{- 2 + \frac{| \mu |}{2}}\) that repeating the strategy of the previous section does not work here for arbitrary multiindex \(\mu\), since we would have

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[2]}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta} \lesssim \int_t^{\infty} \| e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_1)} \|_{L^{\infty}} \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}^{[2]}_{s, T} \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta, \eta} \mathrm{d} s \lesssim \lambda_t \int_t^{\infty} \langle s \rangle^{- 1 + \frac{| \mu |}{2}} \langle s \rangle^{- 1} \mathrm{d} s\)

which does not converge when \(| \mu | \geq 2\).

The idea then is to change the representation of the germ: the field content in (2.28) will not be products of vertex fields \(\psi_{1 : \ell}\) as in (5.1); instead, we make a new ansatz. The goal is to shift the divergence caused by the derivative \(\partial^{\mu}\) from the kernels to the field content, which is not flown.

Given an index set \(I \subseteq 1 : \ell\), define the operator \(\partial_I\) by

\(\displaystyle \partial_I f := \sum_{j \in I} \partial_{x_j} f \left( x_1 {, \ldots, x_{\ell}} \right)\)

where \(x_j \in \mathbb{R}^d\). Then postulate

\(\displaystyle q^{[\ell], T}_t (\phi) = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \gamma^{[1]}_{t, T} (y) \partial^{\mu}_y \phi (y) & \operatorname{if} \ell = 1\\ \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \partial_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} & \operatorname{if} \ell \geq 2 \end{array} \right. \) (5.30)

We have to show that this ansatz is compatible with the structure of the equation (2.19). This is not immediately obvious, since the representation we obtained before for \(V^{[\ell]}\) involves the vertex fields \(\psi_{1 : \ell}\) without derivatives on them. However, the following lemma shows that translation invariance and exponential decay of the fluctuation free field covariance \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t\) ensures that the Picard scheme (2.19) propagates the original field insertion \(\partial^{\mu}_y \phi\) through the induction in such a way that is compatible with (5.30).

Lemma 5.10. Let \(q^{[\ell], T}\) be given by (5.30) and \(V^{[\ell]}\) by (3.31). Then for any \(I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell\) we have

\(\displaystyle \langle \mathrm{D} q^{[| I_1 |], T}_t, \dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} V^{[| I_2 |]}_t \rangle = \int \mathbf{B}^{[| I_1 |, | I_2 |]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \partial_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \) (5.31)

for some function \(\mathbf{B}^{[| I_1 |, | I_2 |]}_t\).

Proof. Since \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x_1 - x_2)\) is invariant under simultaneous translations, it is straightforward to see from the induction of Section 3.2 that the same is true for the potential kernels \(v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t\) (understood in the degenerate sense for the first-order kernel \(v^{[1]}_t := \lambda_t\) and the vacuum energy contribution \(v^{[2] (0)}_t\)), and also for the divergence-corrected kernels \(v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{a})}\) where the correction \(M^{(\boldsymbol{a})}\) was defined in (5.3). That is, for any \(z \in \mathbb{R}^d\) we have

\(\displaystyle (v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{a})}) (\sigma_{\boldsymbol{a}}, x_1 + z, \ldots, x_{\ell} + z) = (v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{a})}) (\sigma_{\boldsymbol{a}}, x_1, \ldots, x_{\ell}) .\)

When \(| I_1 | = 1\), compute

\(\displaystyle \langle \mathrm{D} q^{[1], T}_t, \dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} V^{[| I_2 |]}_t \rangle = C_{| I_2 |} i \beta \gamma^{[1]}_{t, T} (y) \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = | I_2 | \end{array}$}}} \int \sum_{j \in I_2} \sigma_j \partial_y^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - x_j) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{I_2} .\)

Fix a charge configuration \(\sigma_{I_2}\). For any \(a \in \mathbb{R}^d\), denote by \(\psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (x_{I_2} - a)\) the usual vertex field product evaluated at \(\phi (x_k - a)\), \(k \in I_2\). Consider

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} H (a) & := & \int \sum_{j \in I_2} \sigma_j \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - x_j) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (x_{I_2}) \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (x_{I_2} - a) \mathrm{d} x_{I_2}\\ & = & \int \sum_{j \in I_2} \sigma_j \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - a - z_j) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (z_{I_2}) \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (z_{I_2}) \mathrm{d} z_{I_2} \end{array}\)

where in the second line we used the unit Jacobian change of variables \(z_k := x_k - a\), \(k \in I_2\) and translation invariance of \(v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}\). We compute \(\partial_a^{\mu}\) in both of these representations. On one hand, since \(\partial_a^{\mu} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (x_{I_2} - a) = (- 1)^{| \mu |} \partial_{I_2}^{\mu} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (x_{I_2} - a)\), provided we can differentiate under the integral (which we postpone to the end of the proof),

\(\displaystyle \partial_a^{\mu} H = (- 1)^{| \mu |} \int \sum_{j \in I_2} \sigma_j \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - x_j) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (x_{I_2}) \partial_{I_2}^{\mu} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (x_{I_2} - a) \mathrm{d} x_{I_2} .\)

while on the other, again provided we can differentiate under the integral,

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \partial_a^{\mu} H & = & \int \sum_{j \in I_2} \sigma_j \partial_a^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - a - z_j) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (z_{I_2}) \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (z_{I_2}) \mathrm{d} z_{I_2}\\ & = & (- 1)^{| \mu |} \int \sum_{j \in I_2} \sigma_j \partial_y^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - a - z_j) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (z_{I_2}) \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (z_{I_2}) \mathrm{d} z_{I_2} . \end{array}\)

Evaluating at \(a = 0\), we obtain

\(\displaystyle \int \sum_{j \in I_2} \sigma_j \partial_y^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - z_j) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho}) (z_{I_2}) \mathrm{d} z_{I_2} = \int \sum_{j \in I_2} \sigma_j \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - x_j) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})} \partial_{I_2}^{\mu} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho}) (x_{I_2}) \mathrm{d} x_{I_2}\)

which means that \(\langle \mathrm{D} q^{[1], T}_t, \dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} V^{[| I_2 |]}_t \rangle\) is of the form (5.31), since \(| I_1 | = 1\) implies \(| I_2 | = \ell - 1\).

When \(| I_1 | \geq 2\), since

\(\displaystyle \mathrm{D} \partial_I^{\mu} \psi_I = \partial_I^{\mu} \mathrm{D} \psi_I = \partial_I^{\mu} \left( i \beta \sum_{j \in I} \sigma_j \delta_{x_j} \psi_I \right) = i \beta \sum_{j \in I} \sigma_j \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \partial_I^{\nu} \delta_{x_j} \partial_I^{\mu - \nu} \psi_I^{\rho}, \) (5.32)

writing \(\dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_j, \xi_k) := - \frac{\beta^2}{2} \sigma_j \sigma_k \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x_j - x_k)\) and \(I := 1 : (\ell - 1)\), we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \langle \mathrm{D} q^{[| I_1 |], T}_t, \dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} V^{[| I_2 |]}_t \rangle\\ = & C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |} \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = | I_2 | \end{array}$}}} \int \gamma^{[| I_1 |]}_{t, T} (y, \xi_{I_1 - 1})\\ \times & \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} j \in I_1 - 1\\ k \in I_2 \end{array}$}}} \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \partial_{I_1 - 1}^{\nu} \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_j, \xi_k) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} \partial_{I_1}^{\mu - \nu} \psi_{I_1}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_I \end{array}\)

By similar argument as above, for any \(j \in I_1 - 1\), for each \(x_j \in \mathbb{R}^d\), we obtain

\(\displaystyle \int \sum_{k \in I_2} \sigma_k \partial_{x_j}^{\nu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x_j - x_k) v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (x_{I_2}) \mathrm{d} x_{I_2} = \int \sum_{k \in I_2} \sigma_k \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x_j - x_k) v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})} \partial^{\nu}_{I_2} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (x_{I_2}) \mathrm{d} x_{I_2}\)

which, combined with the formula for \(\langle \mathrm{D} q^{[| I_1 |], T}_t, \dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} V^{[| I_2 |]}_t \rangle\) yields the statement, since

\(\displaystyle \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \partial_{I_2}^{\nu} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} \partial_{I_1}^{\mu - \nu} \psi_{I_1}^{\rho} = \partial_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho}\)

by the binomial theorem.

It then remains to show we can apply the Leibniz integral rule. Let \(j \in I_2\). For any fixed \(y \in \mathbb{R}^d\)

\(\displaystyle \int \left| \sum_{j \in I_2} \sigma_j \partial_y^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - z_j) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (z_{I_2}) \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (z_{I_2}) \right| \mathrm{d} z_{I_2} \lesssim \unicode{x2980}v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \varsigma, \eta} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu | + \varkappa (\boldsymbol{b})}{2} - 2}\)

for any \(\varsigma > 0\), where in the case \(\varkappa (\boldsymbol{b}) > 0\) so that \(M^{(\boldsymbol{b})} (z_{I_2}) = | z_1 - z_2 |^{- \varkappa (\boldsymbol{b})}\), we proceed as in Lemma 3.12, i.e. leverage neutrality and feed the divergence to \(\sum_{j \in I_2} \sigma_j \partial_y^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t\) via Lemma A.6.

For the other integral, the function that is needed to be dominated involves \(\partial_{I_2}^{\mu} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho}\), so that the boundedness of the complex exponential is not sufficient. To control the infinite volume limit, we need to rely on the Steiner weights in our kernels. Note that for a fixed \(j \in I_2\), we have

\(\displaystyle \prod_{k \in I_2} \langle x_k \rangle \lesssim \langle x_j \rangle \prod_{k \in I_2 \setminus \{ j \}} \langle x_k - x_j \rangle \langle x_j \rangle \lesssim \langle \operatorname{St} (x_{I_2}) \rangle^{| I_2 | - 1} \langle x_j - y \rangle^{| I_2 |} \langle y \rangle^{| I_2 |} .\)

Thus, when \(\varkappa (\boldsymbol{b}) = 0\), applying Faà di Bruno to the vertex fields, there exists polynomials \(P^{\mu}\) of the field and its derivatives such that

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \int \sum_{j \in I_2} \sigma_j \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - x_j) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (x_{I_2}) \partial_{I_2}^{\mu} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (x_{I_2} - a) \mathrm{d} x_{I_2}\\ \lesssim & \sum_{j \in I_2} \unicode{x2980}v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \varsigma, \eta} \| \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t \|_{L^1_{\eta}} \sup_{x_{I_2}} \left| \langle x_j - y \rangle^{- \eta} \langle \operatorname{St} (x_{I_2}) \rangle^{- \eta} \prod_{j \in I_2} P^{\mu} (\phi (x_j - a)) \right|\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980}v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \varsigma, \eta} \langle t \rangle^{- 2} \prod_{j \in I_2} \left\| \langle \cdot \rangle^{- \frac{\eta}{| I_2 |}} P^{\mu} (\phi (\cdot - a)) \right\|_{L^{\infty}}\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980}v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \varsigma, \eta} \langle t \rangle^{- 2} \| \phi \|_{\mathcal{H}}^c \end{array}\)

for some \(c\), where in the last line we used Besov embedding and chose \(\eta\) large enough. When \(\varkappa > 0\), we proceed similarly, the only difference being the use of Lemma A.6:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \int \frac{\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - x_1) - \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y - x_2)}{| x_1 - x_2 |^{\varkappa}} (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t) (x_{1 : 2}) \partial_{I_2}^{\mu} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} (x_{1 : 2} - a) \mathrm{d} x_{1 : 2}\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980}v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t | x_1 - x_2 |^{1 - \varkappa} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \varsigma, \eta} e^{- \frac{m^2}{t}} t^{- \frac{1}{2}} \int e^{- \varsigma \sqrt{t} | x_1 - y |} \langle x_1 - y \rangle^{\eta} \mathrm{d} x_1\\ \times & \sup_{x_{1 : 2}} \left| \langle x_1 - y \rangle^{- \eta} \langle \operatorname{St} (x_{1 : 2}) \rangle^{- \eta} \prod_{j \in 1 : 2} P^{\mu} (\phi (x_j - a)) \right| . \end{array}\)

The justification for the Leibniz integral rule when \(| I_1 | \geq 2\) follows verbatim.\(\Box\)

The point of the above lemma was not just to shift the divergence from the kernels to the field, but also to do it in such a way that the resulting basis of the representation consists solely of eigenfunctions of the functional Laplacian \(\Delta_{\dot{G}_t}\). Indeed, translation invariance of \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t\) ensures that there are no “trickle down” maps when evaluating \(\Delta_{\dot{G}_t}\) on the field content \(\partial_{1 : \ell}^{\mu} \psi_{1 : \ell}\) (as opposed to the case of the potential, cf. the map \(\mathbf{A} (v^{[2] (\varkappa)}_t)\) around (3.17)). This observation leads to

Lemma 5.11. Fix \(\mu \in \mathbb{N}^d_0\) multiindex, and let \(V^{[\ell]}\) be given by (3.31). Suppose that the kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\) satisfy the equations

\(\displaystyle \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) = e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \gamma^{[\ell]}_{T, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) + \int_t^T e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \mathrm{d} s \) (5.33)

where

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & - \mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y)\\ = & \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell\\ | I_1 | = 1 \end{array}$}}} C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |} \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = | I_2 | \end{array}$}}} \gamma^{[1]}_{s, T} (y) \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{I_2}, y) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_s M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2})\\ + & \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell\\ | I_1 | \geq 2 \end{array}$}}} C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |} \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = | I_2 | \end{array}$}}} \gamma^{[| I_1 |]}_{s, T} (y, \xi_{I_1 - 1}) \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{I_1 - 1}, \xi_{I_2}) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_s M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) \end{array} \) (5.34)

for some positive combinatorial coefficients \(C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |}\). Then \(q_t^{[\ell], T}\), given by (5.30), satisfies the observable flow equation (2.19).

Proof. Let \(I \subseteq \mathbb{N}\) be a finite index set. Compute

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\dot{G}_t} [\partial_I^{\mu} \psi_I^{\rho}]\\ = & - \frac{\beta^2}{2} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^2)^2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (z_1 - z_2) \partial_{x_I}^{\mu} \left( \sum_{j_1, j_2 \in I} \sigma_{j_1} \sigma_{j_2} \delta (x_{j_1} - z_1) \delta (x_{j_2} - z_2) \psi_I^{\rho} \right) \mathrm{d} z_{1 : 2}\\ = & - \frac{\beta^2}{2} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^2)^2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (z_1 - z_2) \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \partial_{x_I}^{\nu} \left( \sum_{j_1, j_2 \in I} \sigma_{j_1} \sigma_{j_2} \delta (x_{j_1} - z_1) \delta (x_{j_2} - z_2) \right) \partial^{\mu - \nu}_I \psi_I^{\rho} \mathrm{d} z_{1 : 2}\\ = & \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \partial_{x_I}^{\nu} \left( - \frac{\beta^2}{2} \sum_{j_1, j_2 \in I} \sigma_{j_1} \sigma_{j_2} \int \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (z_1 - z_2) \delta (x_{j_1} - z_1) \delta (x_{j_2} - z_2) \mathrm{d} z_{1 : 2} \right) \partial^{\mu - \nu}_I \psi_I^{\rho}\\ = & \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \partial_{x_I}^{\nu} \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_I) \partial^{\mu - \nu}_I \psi_I^{\rho}\\ = & \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_I) \partial^{\mu}_I \psi_I^{\rho} \end{array}\)

since for any nontrivial multiindex, the translation invariant covariance matrix \(\dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_I)\) is annihilated by the diagonal derivative \(\partial_I = \sum_{j \in I} \partial_{x_j}\). The statement then follows from Lemma 5.10.\(\Box\)

Imposing the boundary conditions

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{llll} \gamma^{[1]}_{0, T} & := & 1 & \\ \gamma^{[\ell]}_{T, T} & := & 0 & \operatorname{if} \ell \geq 2, \end{array} \) (5.35)

allows us to estimate inductively the germ kernels in the norms (5.7) and transfer these bounds onto the germ and its approximate generator.

Proposition 5.12. Let \(\beta^2 < 6 \pi\) and \(\mu \in \mathbb{N}^d_0\) multiindex. Then there exist kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\) satisfying (5.33) with boundary conditions (5.35), and for any \(\eta \geq 0\) it holds that

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \lesssim (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \) (5.36)

for some \(c = c (\ell, \beta^2) \in \mathbb{R}_+\). Moreover, the \(\unicode{x2980} \cdot \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta}\)-limits \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_t := \lim_{T \rightarrow \infty} \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\) exist, are independent of \(\eta\) and satisfy the bounds (5.36). Finally, defining

\(\displaystyle q^{[\ell]}_t (\phi) := \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \gamma^{[1]}_t (y) \partial^{\mu}_y \phi (y) & \operatorname{if} \ell = 1\\ \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \partial_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} & \operatorname{if} \ell \geq 2 \end{array} \right., \) (5.37)

it holds that \(q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t := \sum_{\ell \leq \ell^{\ast}} q_t^{[\ell]}\) is in the space \(\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, 0, y}^{\frac{| \mu |}{2}, \ell^{\ast} \delta - \frac{| \mu |}{2}}\).

Proof. The bound (5.36) is verified similarly to Proposition 5.2 by repeating standard estimates on the source term (5.34). The \(T \rightarrow \infty\) limit is also obtained via a straightforward modification: defining \(\mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, \infty}\) by replacing the \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\) in (5.34) with the limits \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_t\), it is seen inductively that the limits solve

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{llll} \gamma^{[1]}_t (y) & = & 1 & \\ \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) & = & \int_t^{\infty} e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, \infty} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \mathrm{d} s & \operatorname{if} \ell \geq 2, \end{array} \) (5.38)

by propagating the bound

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_t - \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \lesssim (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon}\)

for some \(\varepsilon > 0\) small enough.

We claim that for any \(\ell \geq 1\) there exists \(c = c (\ell, \beta^2) \in \mathbb{R}_+\) such that for every \(p \geq 1\)

\(\displaystyle \| q_t^{[\ell]} (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2}} (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c . \) (5.39)

Indeed, when \(\ell = 1\) simply apply (4.9). For \(\ell \geq 2\), first note that Faà di Bruno and (4.9) yields for any \(b \in \mathbb{R}\), \(x \in \mathbb{R}^2\) that

\(\displaystyle \| \partial^{\mu}_x e^{i b \varphi_t (x)} \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2}} \mathfrak{l}_t^c,\)

so that denoting by \(\psi_j (t) := e^{i \beta \sigma_j \varphi_t (x_j)}\), using Minkowski's integral inequality, \(1 \leq \omega_t^{\Theta}\) and the multinomial theorem we obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \| q_t^{[\ell]} (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)}\\ \leq & \int | \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) | \| \partial_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} (t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ \leq & \int | \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) | \sum_{\sum_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \mu^{(j)} = \mu} \binom{\mu}{\mu^{(1)}, \ldots, \mu^{(\ell - 1)}} \prod_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \| \partial^{\mu^{(j)}}_{x_j} \psi_j^{\rho} (t) \|_{L^{(\ell - 1) p} (\Omega)} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2}} \end{array}\)

and inserting (5.36) yields (5.39). On the other hand, we claim

\(\displaystyle \| \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2}} \langle t \rangle^{- \ell^{\ast} \delta - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c . \) (5.40)

Indeed, this follows in the case \(\ell = 1\) simply from (2.26); for \(\ell \geq 2\), we proceed similarly as above: compute with (5.32)

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \| \langle \mathrm{D} q_t^{[\ell]} (\varphi_t), \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \rangle \|_{L^p (\Omega)}\\ \lesssim & \int | \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) | \left\| \sum_{j \in 1 : (\ell - 1)} \sigma_j \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \partial^{\nu} \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t (x_j) \partial_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu - \nu} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ \lesssim & \int | \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) | \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \| \partial^{\nu} \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \|_{L^{\infty} (\Omega ; L^{\infty})} \| \partial_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu - \nu} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \nu |}{2} - 1 - \delta - (\ell^{\ast} - \ell) \delta} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu | - | \nu |}{2}} \end{array}\)

where in the last line we used (2.26) to estimate \(\partial^{\nu} \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t\) (more precisely, we estimate with (4.15), Young's inequality, (A.2) and (4.1)). It follows (5.38) that the proof of Lemma 5.11 applies and thus \(q^{[\ell]}\) satisfy the observable flow equation (2.19). Thus inserting (5.36) in the above estimate and using Lemma 2.19, we conclude (5.40). Combining (5.39) and (5.40) yields the statement.\(\Box\)

Similar considerations hold for the derivatives of the vertex field \(\tilde{\psi}_y := \exp (i \alpha \phi (y)) \) with \(\alpha\) as in the previous section. Make the ansatz

\(\displaystyle q^{[\ell], T}_t (\phi) = \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \partial_{y, 1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu} \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \) (5.41)

where the operator \(\partial_{y, I}\) is defined by

\(\displaystyle \partial_{y, I} f \left( y, x_1 {, \ldots, x_{\ell}} \right) := (\partial_y + \partial_I) f \left( y, x_1 {, \ldots, x_{\ell}} \right) .\)

Similar ideas to those in Lemma 5.10 lead us to the following

Lemma 5.13. Let \(q^{[\ell]}_t\) be given by (5.41) and \(V^{[\ell]}\) by (3.31). Then for all \(I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell\) we have

\(\displaystyle \langle \mathrm{D} q^{[| I_1 |]}_t, \dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} V^{[| I_2 |]}_t \rangle = \int \mathbf{B}^{[| I_1 |, | I_2 |]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \partial_{y, 1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu} \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \) (5.42)

for some function \(\mathbf{B}^{[| I_1 |, | I_2 |]}_t\).

Proof. Compute

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \mathrm{D}_{\phi} \partial_{y, I}^{\mu} \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_I^{\rho} & = & \partial_{y, I}^{\mu} \mathrm{D}_{\phi} \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_I^{\rho}\\ & = & \partial_{y, I}^{\mu} \left( \left( i \alpha \delta_y + i \beta \sum_{j \in I} \sigma_j \delta_{x_j} \right) \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_I^{\rho} \right)\\ & = & i \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \left( \alpha \partial_y^{\nu} \delta_y + \beta \sum_{j \in I} \sigma_j \partial_{x_j}^{\nu} \delta_{x_j} \right) \partial_{y, I}^{\mu - \nu} (\tilde{\psi}_y \psi_I^{\rho}) \end{array}\)

so that

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \langle \mathrm{D} q^{[| I_1 |]}_t, \dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} V^{[| I_2 |]}_t \rangle\\ = & C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |} \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = | I_2 | \end{array}$}}} \int \gamma^{[| I_1 |]}_t (y, \xi_{I_1 - 1}) \sum_{k \in I_2} \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \left( i \alpha \partial_y^{\nu} \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_t (y, \xi_k) + \sum_{j \in I_1 - 1} \partial_{x_j}^{\nu} \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_j, \xi_k) \right)\\ \times & (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} \partial_{y, I_1 - 1}^{\mu - \nu} (\tilde{\psi}_y \psi_{I_1 - 1}^{\rho}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \end{array}\)

Similarly to the proof of Lemma 5.10, we obtain

\(\displaystyle \int \sum_{k \in I_2} i \alpha \partial_y^{\nu} \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_t (y, \xi_k) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{I_2} = \int \sum_{k \in I_2} i \alpha \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_t (y, \xi_k) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) \partial_{I_2}^{\nu} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} x_{I_2}\)

and for each fixed \(x_j \in \mathbb{R}^d\), \(j \in I_1 - 1\),

\(\displaystyle \int \sum_{k \in I_2} \partial_{x_j}^{\nu} \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_j, \xi_k) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{I_2} = \int \sum_{k \in I_2} \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_j, \xi_k) (v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_t M^{(\boldsymbol{b})}) (\xi_{I_2}) \partial_{I_2}^{\nu} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} x_{I_2} .\)

The statement then follows from

\(\displaystyle \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \partial_{I_2}^{\nu} \psi_{I_2}^{\rho} \partial_{y, I_1 - 1}^{\mu - \nu} (\tilde{\psi}_y \psi_{I_1 - 1}^{\rho}) = \partial_{y, 1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu} \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} .\)
\(\Box\)

We then have the analogue of Lemma 5.5 and 5.11:

Lemma 5.14. Fix \(\mu \in \mathbb{N}^d_0\) multiindex, and let \(V_t^{[\ell]}\) be given by (3.31). Suppose that the kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\) satisfy the equations

\(\displaystyle \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) = e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, T}^{\mathbb{C}} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \gamma^{[\ell]}_{T, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) + \int_t^T e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s}^{\mathbb{C}} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \mathrm{d} s \) (5.43)

where \(\dot{\mathbf{G}}_t^{\mathbb{C}}\) is defined in (5.26) and \(\mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, T}\) in (5.19). Then \(q_t^{[\ell], T}\), given by (5.41), satisfies the observable flow equation (2.19).

Proof. Similarly to the proof of Lemma 5.11, we have for any \(I \subseteq \mathbb{N}\) finite index set

\(\displaystyle \frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\dot{G}_t} [\partial_{y, I}^{\mu} \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_I^{\rho}] = \dot{\mathbf{G}}_t^{\mathbb{C}} (y, \xi_I) \partial_{y, I}^{\mu} \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_I^{\rho} .\)

The statement then follows from the previous lemma.\(\Box\)

Imposing the boundary conditions

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{llll} \gamma^{[1]}_{0, T} & := & 1 & \\ \gamma^{[\ell]}_{T, T} & := & 0 & \operatorname{if} \ell \geq 2, \end{array} \) (5.44)

finishes the argument:

Proposition 5.15. Let \(\beta^2 < 6 \pi\), \(\mu \in \mathbb{N}^d_0\) multiindex and \(\alpha \in \mathbb{C}\) with real part \(| \Re (\alpha) | < 4 \pi / \beta\). Then there exist kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\) satisfying (5.43) with boundary conditions (5.44), and for any \(\eta \geq 0\) it holds that

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} {\lesssim e^{\frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)}} {e^{- \frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)}} (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \) (5.45)

for some \(c = c (\ell, \beta^2) \in \mathbb{R}_+\). Moreover, the \(\unicode{x2980} \cdot \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta, \eta}\)-limits \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_t := \lim_{T \rightarrow \infty} \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\) exist, are independent of \(\eta\) and satisfy the bounds (5.45). Finally, defining

\(\displaystyle q^{[\ell]}_t (\phi) := \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \partial_{y, 1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu} \tilde{\psi}_y \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, \) (5.46)

it holds that \(q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t := \sum_{\ell \leq \ell^{\ast}} q_t^{[\ell]}\) is in the space \(\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, i \alpha, y}^{\kappa, \sigma}\) with \(\kappa := \frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2 - \Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi}\), \(\sigma := \ell^{\ast} \delta - \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2 - \Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} - \frac{| \mu |}{2}\).

Proof. The inductive bound (5.45) follows from comparing Lemma 5.14 with Lemma 5.5 and applying Proposition 5.6 and Corollary 5.8. Sending \(T \rightarrow \infty\) is again routine: defining \(\mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, \infty}\) by replacing the \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T}\) in (5.19) with the limits \(\gamma^{[\ell]}_t\), it is seen inductively that the limits solve

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{llll} \gamma^{[1]}_t (y) & = & {e^{\frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)}} {e^{- \frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)}} e^{i \Re (\alpha) \Im (\alpha) \mathcal{G}_t (0)} & \\ \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) & = & \int_t^{\infty} e^{\mathbf{G}_{t, s}^{\mathbb{C}} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})} \mathscr{S}^{[\ell]}_{s, \infty} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \mathrm{d} s & \operatorname{if} \ell \geq 2, \end{array} \) (5.47)

by propagating the bound

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_t - \gamma^{[\ell]}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} {\lesssim e^{\frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)}} {e^{- \frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_t (0)}} (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon}\)

for some \(\varepsilon > 0\) small enough.

By the multinomial theorem and Faà di Bruno,

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & e^{- \langle i \alpha, \phi (y) \rangle} q_t^{[\ell]}\\ = & e^{- i \alpha \phi (y)} \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \partial_{y, 1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu} e^{i \alpha \phi (y)} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ = & e^{- i \alpha \phi (y)} \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \sum_{\sum_{j = 0}^{\ell - 1} \mu^{(j)} = \mu} \binom{\mu}{\mu^{(0)}, \ldots, \mu^{(\ell - 1)}} \partial_y^{\mu^{(0)}} e^{i \alpha \phi (y)} \prod_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \partial^{\mu^{(j)}}_{x_j} \psi_j^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ = & \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \sum_{\sum_{j = 0}^{\ell - 1} \mu^{(j)} = \mu} \binom{\mu}{\mu^{(0)}, \ldots, \mu^{(\ell - 1)}} P_{\mu^{(0)}} (\{ \partial^{\nu} \phi (y) \}_{\nu \leq \mu^{(0)}}) \prod_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \partial^{\mu^{(j)}}_{x_j} \psi_j^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \end{array}\)

for polynomials \(P_{\mu^{(0)}}\) with total scaling degree \(| \mu^{(0)} |\), and consequently by Minkowski's integral inequality, Hölder, (4.9), \(1 \leq \omega_t^{\Theta}\), and (5.45) we obtain

\(\displaystyle \| e^{- \langle i \alpha, \varphi_t (y) \rangle} q_t^{[\ell]} (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2 - \Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi}} (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c . \) (5.48)

On the other hand, computing

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & e^{- \langle i \alpha, \phi (y) \rangle} \langle \mathrm{D} q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi), \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \rangle\\ = & i \int \gamma^{[\ell]}_t (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \binom{\mu}{\nu} \partial_{y, 1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\nu} \left( \alpha \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t (y) + \beta \sum_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \sigma_j \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t (x_j) \right)\\ \times & e^{- i \alpha \phi (y)} \partial_{y, 1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\mu - \nu} e^{i \alpha \phi (y)} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \end{array}\)

and proceeding similarly as above, by (2.26) (more precisely, by (4.15), Young's inequality, (A.2) and (4.1)), we get

\(\displaystyle \| e^{- \langle i \alpha, \varphi_t (y) \rangle} \langle \mathrm{D} q_t^{[\ell]} (\varphi_t), \dot{G} R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \rangle \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \sum_{\nu \leq \mu} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \nu |}{2} - 1 - \delta - (\ell^{\ast} - \ell) \delta} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu | - | \nu |}{2}} .\)

It follows from (5.47) that the proof of Lemma 5.1 applies and thus \(q^{[\ell]}\) satisfy the observable flow equation (2.19). Thus inserting (5.45) in the above estimate and using Lemma 2.19, we obtain

\(\displaystyle \| e^{- \langle i \alpha, \varphi_t (y) \rangle} \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2 - \Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi}} \langle t \rangle^{- \ell^{\ast} \delta - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c . \) (5.49)

The statement follows from combining (5.48) and (5.49).\(\Box\)

6Observable products, prefactorization algebra property

In this section we assemble the ideas developed throughout the paper to finish the proof of Theorems 1.1 and 1.2.

We begin with unifying the notation of the previous section: given a \(y \in \mathbb{R}^d\) where the germ is localized, \(\mu \in \mathbb{N}^d_0\), \(\alpha \in \mathbb{C}\), we write

\(\displaystyle q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi) = \int \sum_{k = 0}^n \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \partial^{\mu}_{y, 1 : (\ell - 1)} (\phi^k (y) \tilde{\psi}_y^{(\alpha)} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \) (6.1)

where \(\tilde{\psi}_y^{(\alpha)} := e^{i \alpha \phi (y)}\). This ansatz includes everything we have constructed in the previous section as special cases, cf. (5.10), (5.27), (5.37) and (5.46).

Recall that to prove existence of observables, in light of the Ito reconstruction (Lemma 2.6), we need to construct coherent germs. It is intuitively clear that the conditions 1 and 2 in Definition 2.5 are only technical, and the actually constraining part is the coherence estimate (2.10). In the lemma below we verify these conditions to the ansatz (6.1).

Lemma 6.1. Let \(q^{\langle 1 \rangle}\) and \(q^{\langle 2 \rangle}\) be a sum of functionals of the form (6.1). Then \(\alpha q^{\langle 1 \rangle} + q^{\langle 2 \rangle}\) and \(\alpha q^{\langle 1 \rangle} q^{\langle 2 \rangle}\), \(\alpha \in \mathbb{R}\) satisfy Conditions \(1\) and \(2\) of Definition 2.5.

Proof. Let \(m > d / 2\). Fix a \(T \in \mathbb{R}_+\) arbitrary, and consider the restriction of \(q^{\langle j \rangle}\) to \([0, T]\). Take \(\mathcal{H} := B^s_{2, 2} (\langle x \rangle^{- m})\) with \(s > | \mu | + \frac{d}{2}\), so that Morrey's inequality yields that \(\partial^{\mu} \delta_x\) are continuous functionals on \(\mathcal{H}\). In particular, given a sequence \(\phi_r \xrightarrow{\| \cdot \|_{\mathcal{H}}} \phi\), we have for every \(x \in \mathbb{R}^d\) and \(\zeta \in \mathbb{C}\) that

\(\displaystyle (\partial^{\mu} \phi_r (x))^n e^{\zeta \phi_r (x)} \rightarrow (\partial^{\mu} \phi (x))^n e^{\zeta \phi (x)}\)

and similarly for all \(k \leq n\) and \(\nu\) multiindex with \(| \nu | \leq | \mu |\).

As \(| x_j | \leq | y | + | x_j - y | \leq | y | +\operatorname{St} (x_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y)\), we obtain

\(\displaystyle \prod_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \langle x_j \rangle \lesssim \langle y \rangle^{(\ell - 1)} \langle \operatorname{St} (x_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \rangle^{(\ell - 1)} .\)

Applying Faà di Bruno to the vertex fields in (6.1) we get \(P^{\mu}_k\) polynomials \(\phi (x_j)\) and their derivatives. Then choosing \(\eta\) large enough, by Besov embedding we have,

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} | q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi) | & \lesssim & \sum_{k = 0}^n \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t \unicode{x2980}_{0, \Theta, \eta} \sup_{x_{1 : (\ell - 1)}} \left| \langle \operatorname{St} (x_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \rangle^{- \eta} P_k^{\mu} (\phi (y)) \prod_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} P_k^{\mu} (\phi (x_j)) {e^{- \Im (\alpha) \phi (y)}} \right|\\ & \lesssim & \sum_{k = 0}^n \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t \unicode{x2980}_{0, \Theta, \eta} \left| \langle y \rangle^{\eta} P_k^{\mu} (\phi (y)) {e^{- \Im (\alpha) \phi (y)}} \right| \prod_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \left\| \langle \cdot \rangle^{- \frac{\eta}{(\ell - 1)}} P_k^{\mu} (\phi (\cdot)) \right\|_{L^{\infty}}\\ & \lesssim & \sum_{k = 0}^n \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t \unicode{x2980}_{0, \Theta, \eta} \left| \langle y \rangle^{\eta} P_k^{\mu} (\phi (y)) {e^{- \Im (\alpha) \phi (y)}} \right| \| \phi \|_{\mathcal{H}}^c \end{array}\)

for some \(c\). Combining this with the pointwise convergence above, by dominated convergence we obtain \(q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi_r) \rightarrow q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi)\).

On the other hand, we see from the induction on the kernels that \(t \mapsto \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t\) and \(t \mapsto \partial_t \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t\) are continuous in \(\unicode{x2980} \cdot \unicode{x2980}_{0, \Theta, \eta}\), since \(\gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t\) satisfies classically a differential equation with continuous coefficients. Indeed, for relevant kernels continuity is trivial, and for irrelevant, note that

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t - \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_{t_r} \unicode{x2980}_{0, \Theta} \lesssim \int_{t \wedge t_r}^{t \vee t_r} \left\| e^{\mathbb{U}_{t \wedge t_r, s}} \right\|_{L^{\infty}} \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}_s \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta} \mathrm{d} s + \int_{t \vee t_r}^{\infty} \left\| e^{\mathbb{U}_{t, s}} - e^{\mathbb{U}_{t_r, s}} \right\|_{L^{\infty}} \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}_s \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta} \mathrm{d} s\)

for some \(\mathbb{U}_{\cdot, s} = \int_{\cdot}^s \dot{\mathbb{U}}_v \mathrm{d} v\) with \(\dot{\mathbb{U}}_v\) a linear combination of \(\mathcal{G}_{\cdot, s}\) (see the preceding section), hence continuous in \(L^{\infty}\). The case of time derivatives follow by induction, as

\(\displaystyle \partial_t \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t = \dot{\mathbb{U}}_t \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t + \mathscr{S}_t\)

and we have \(\| \dot{\mathbb{U}}_t \|_{L^{\infty}} \lesssim 1\).

Combined with the above argument for the bound on \(| q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi) |\), we obtain joint continuity on \([0, T] \times \mathcal{H}\) of \(q^{[\ell]}\) and \(\partial_t q^{[\ell]}\). Boundedness and continuity of \(\mathrm{D}_{\phi} q^{[\ell]}\) and \(\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^2 q^{[\ell]}\) on bounded subsets of \([0, T] \times \mathcal{H}\) follow along similar lines by noting that (6.1) ensures that they all depend on \(\phi\) through smooth gradients and Hessians applied to the point evaluations \(\partial^{\mu} \delta_x\).

For condition 2, note that similarly to the above argument for the bound on \(| q_t^{[\ell]} (\phi) |\), there exists an \(N \in \mathbb{N}\) and polynomial \(P\) of \(\phi (y)\) and its derivatives such that

\(\displaystyle \| \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell]} |^{\sharp}_{\varphi_s} \|_{\mathcal{H}} = \sup_{\| v \|_{\mathcal{H}} \leq 1} | \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell]} |_{\varphi_s} (v) | \lesssim \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t \unicode{x2980}_{0, \Theta, \eta} \left| P (\varphi_s (y)) {e^{- \Im (\alpha) \varphi_s (y)}} \right| \| \varphi_s \|_{\mathcal{H}}^N .\)

Then plugging in the bounds on the kernels \(\unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)} \unicode{x2980}\) of the previous section, (4.12) and Lemmas 4.6 and A.9, we obtain that there exists a \(\tilde{N} \in \mathbb{Z}\) such that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} \int_0^T \| Q^{\ast}_s \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell]} |_{\varphi_s}^{\sharp} \|^2_{L^2} \mathrm{d} s \leq \int_0^T \| Q^{\ast}_s \|^2_{\mathcal{L}^2 (\mathcal{H} ; L^2)} \mathbb{E} \| \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell]} |_{\varphi_s}^{\sharp} \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \mathrm{d} s \lesssim \int_0^T \langle s \rangle^{\tilde{N}} \mathrm{d} s < \infty .\)

We thus have proven that \(\alpha q^{\langle 1 \rangle} + q^{\langle 2 \rangle}\) satisfy Conditions \(1\) and \(2\) of Definition 2.5. Extension to \(\alpha q^{\langle 1 \rangle} q^{\langle 2 \rangle}\) is straightforward.\(\Box\)

Proof. (of Theorem 1.1) Consider the germs \(q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t\) constructed in the previous section. As we have seen in Propositions 5.3, 5.9, 5.12 and 5.15, they are in the spaces \(\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, i \alpha, y}^{\kappa, \sigma}\) for \(\kappa := \frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2 - \Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi}\) and \(\sigma := \ell^{\ast} \delta - \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2 - \Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} - \frac{| \mu |}{2}\). Pick \(p > 1\) arbitrary. Then (4.10) yields

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} & \leq & \| e^{i \alpha \phi (y)} \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \| e^{- i \alpha \varphi_t (y)} \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^{p'} (\Omega)}\\ & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} + (p - 1) \frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} - \ell^{\ast} \delta - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \| q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]} \|_{\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, i \alpha, y}^{\kappa, \sigma}} \end{array}\)

so as \(\delta > 0\), choosing

\(\displaystyle \ell^{\ast} := \left\lceil \frac{\frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} + (p - 1) \frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} + 1}{\delta} \right\rceil < \infty \) (6.2)

ensures that the germs \(q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t\) satisfy (2.10). Coupled with the previous lemma, this means that they are coherent; the statement about existence then follows from Lemma 2.6. The existence of \(\varphi\) and the convergence of measures is the content of Theorem 4.4 and Corollary 4.5.\(\Box\)

A couple of remarks are in order.

Remark 6.2. (Sharp estimates) Obviously the Hölder inequality we use in the above proof does not give the optimal scaling for exponentials. Instead, recalling the proofs of Propositions 5.9 and 5.15, we see that the field content of \(q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\phi)\) and \(\mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\phi)\) is of the form \(e^{i \alpha \phi (y)} P_{\mu} (\phi)\) with \(P_{\mu}\) polynomial in \(\phi (y), \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\) and their derivatives. Thus proceeding similarly to the proof of (4.11), the sharp estimates

\(\displaystyle \| q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi}} \mathfrak{l}_t^c, \hspace{4em} \| \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} - \ell^{\ast} \delta - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c\)

follow. We chose to include the suboptimal bounds in the above proof and the subsequent results, since they suffice for our purposes while having simpler presentation.

Remark 6.3. (Required order of expansion) Clearly, the +1 in (6.2) is not necessary apart from the edge cases where the fraction, without it, would be an integer. Then using the sharp estimates in the above remark, recalling that \(\delta := 1 - \frac{\beta^2}{8 \pi}\), \(\beta^2 \in (0, 8 \pi)\) and plugging in the saturated optimal condition \(| \Re (\alpha) | \beta = 4 \pi\) into (6.2), we obtain that the existence of the observable \(\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} \partial^{\mu} e^{i \alpha \phi (y)} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}\) in the optimal regime requires the flow equation to be solved up to iteration

\(\displaystyle \ell^{\ast} (\beta^2, \mu) = \left\lceil \frac{4 \pi (\beta^2 | \mu | + 4 \pi)}{\beta^2 (8 \pi - \beta^2)} \right\rceil . \) (6.3)

This blows up as approaching criticality, \(\beta^2 \rightarrow 8 \pi\), indicating the breakdown of perturbation theory, and also as \(\beta^2 \rightarrow 0\) due to the allowed superficial degree of divergence blowing up along the saturation of the condition \(| \Re (\alpha) | < \frac{4 \pi}{\beta}\).

The function (in \(\beta^2\)) inside the ceiling in (6.3) has global minimum at

\(\displaystyle \beta^2_{\min} = \frac{8 \pi}{1 + \sqrt{1 + 2 | \mu |}} \qquad \operatorname{with}\operatorname{value} \quad \frac{\left( 1 + \sqrt{1 + 2 | \mu |} \right)^2}{4}\)

When \(| \mu | = 0\), i.e. in the case of the observable \(\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} e^{i \alpha \phi (y)} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}\), the function \(\beta^2 \mapsto \frac{16 \pi^2}{\beta^2 (8 \pi - \beta^2)}\) is symmetric around its global minimum at the free fermion point, \(\beta^2_{\min} = 4 \pi\), with value \(1\). That is, exactly at the free fermion point, even first-order perturbation theory is sufficient to construct the observable in the full optimal regime \(| \Re (\alpha) | < \frac{4 \pi}{\beta}\), and none of the screening theory developed in Section 5.2 is required.

As we will soon see, the above considerations are true not only for the 1-point function \(\langle \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} e^{i \alpha \phi (y)} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \rangle\), but also for general \(n\)-point functions \(\langle \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} e^{i \alpha \phi (y)} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \star \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} \phi^{k_1} (y_1) \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \star \ldots \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} \phi^{k_n} (y_n) \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \rangle\) with \(y \neq y_1 \ldots \neq y_n\), since polynomials of the field have \(\kappa = 0\) superficial degree of divergence owing to their log-blowup. For products with other vertex fields or derivatives, higher \(\ell^{\ast}\) is needed, so that the decay \(\langle t \rangle^{- \sigma}\) coming from the accuracy \(\sigma\) of the germ can offset the other germs' divergence on top of its own.

Remark 6.4. (Independence of order of expansion) The previous remark suggests the need for discussion on whether the observables we construct depend on the order of expansion. Suppose there exists an \(\ell^{\ast}\) (not necessarily given by (6.2)) for which the construction of Theorem 1.1 goes through, i.e. such that

\(\displaystyle \frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} + (p - 1) \frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} - \ell^{\ast} \delta < 0 \) (6.4)

for some \(p > 1\). Then for any \(\tilde{\ell} > \ell^{\ast}\), by the proofs of the previous section, in particular (5.11), (5.28), (5.39), (5.48), we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| q^{[\leq \tilde{\ell}]}_t (\varphi_t) - q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} & \leq & \sum_{\ell = \ell^{\ast} + 1}^{\tilde{\ell}} \| e^{\langle i \alpha, \varphi_t (y) \rangle} \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \| e^{- \langle i \alpha, \varphi_t (y) \rangle} q_t^{[\ell]} (\varphi_t) \|_{L^{p'} (\Omega)}\\ & \lesssim & \sum_{\ell = \ell^{\ast} + 1}^{\tilde{\ell}} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} + (p - 1) \frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi}} (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c\\ & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} + (p - 1) \frac{\Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi} - \ell^{\ast} \delta} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \end{array}\)

which goes to 0 as \(t \rightarrow \infty\) by (6.4). Thus \(q^{[\leq \tilde{\ell}]} \sim_{\varphi} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}\), and hence by (2.5) the germ \(q^{[\leq \tilde{\ell}]}\) reconstructs the same martingale as \(q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}\). In particular, the correlation functions we constructed are independent of the order of perturbative approximation.

To construct the observable product, we exploit that the germs obtained as approximate solution on the observable flow equation (2.18) localize deep in the UV. This is the content of the following

Lemma 6.5. Let \(q^{[\ell_j] \langle j \rangle}_t\), \(j \in \{ 1, 2 \}\) be given by (6.1), localized at \(y_1 \neq y_2 \in \mathbb{R}^2\), respectively. Then there exists a \(K = K (\mu_j, n_j, \alpha_j)\) and \(C = C (\ell_1, \ell_2)\) such that for all \(p \in [1, \infty)\),

\(\displaystyle \| e^{- i \langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}, \varphi_t (\boldsymbol{y}) \rangle} \operatorname{Tr} [\dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell_1] \langle 1 \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell_2] \langle 2 \rangle}_t (\varphi_t)] \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^K e^{- \frac{\sqrt{t}}{C} | y_1 - y_2 |} \prod_{j \in 1 : 2} \sum_{k_j \leq n_j} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell_j] (\alpha_j, k_j)}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell_j)}\)

with \(\boldsymbol{\alpha} := \alpha_1 \veebar \alpha_2 := (\alpha_1, \alpha_2) \in \mathbb{C}^2\) and \(\boldsymbol{y} := y_1 \veebar y_2 := (y_1, y_2) \in (\mathbb{R}^d)^2\).

Proof. Evaluating the functional derivative on (6.1), we see that after contraction, \(\operatorname{Tr} [\mathrm{D} q^{[\ell_1] \langle 1 \rangle}_t (\phi) \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell_2] \langle 2 \rangle}_t (\phi)]\) are linear combination of terms of the form

\(\displaystyle \int \int \prod_{j \in 1 : 2} \gamma^{[\ell_j] (\alpha_j, k_j)}_t (\xi_{1 : (\ell_j - 1)}, y_j) \mathfrak{G} (\boldsymbol{\xi}, \boldsymbol{y}) P_{k_j, \alpha_j}^{\mu} (\phi) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell_1 - 1)} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell_2 - 1)} \) (6.5)

for some \(k_j \leq n_j\), where \(P_{k_j, \alpha_j}^{\mu} (\phi)\) are polynomials of \(\phi (y_j), \psi_{y_j}^{(\alpha_j)} {, \psi_{1 : (\ell_j - 1)}} \) and their derivatives and where \(\mathfrak{G} (\boldsymbol{\xi}, \boldsymbol{y})\) is linear combination of \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}\) and its derivatives, contracting between the sets \((x_{1 : (\ell_1 - 1)}, y_1)\) and \((x_{1 : (\ell_2 - 1)}, y_2)\). Inserting

\(\displaystyle 1 = \prod_{j \in 1 : 2} \omega_t^{\Theta} (y_j, \xi_{1 : (\ell_j - 1)}) \omega_{- t}^{\Theta} (y_j, \xi_{1 : (\ell_j - 1)})\)

with weight \(\omega_t^{\Theta}\) defined in (3.4), for any \(k_j \in 1 : (\ell_j - 1)\) and multiindices \(\nu_1, \nu_2\), bound

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & e^{- \Theta (\ell_1) \sqrt{t} \operatorname{St} (x_{1 : (\ell_j - 1)}, y_j)} \partial^{\nu_1}_{x_{k_1}} \partial^{\nu_2}_{x_{k_2}} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t \left( x_{k_1} {- x_{k_2}} \right) e^{- \Theta (\ell_2) \sqrt{t} \operatorname{St} (x_{1 : (\ell_j - 1)}, y_j)}\\ \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \nu_1 | + | \nu_2 |}{2} - 1} e^{- \frac{\sqrt{t} }{4^{\ell_1}} | x_{k_1} - y_1 |} e^{- \frac{t}{8} | x_{k_1} - x_{k_2} |^2} e^{- \frac{\sqrt{t} }{4^{\ell_2}} | x_{k_2} - y_2 |}\\ \leq & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \nu_1 | + | \nu_2 |}{2} - 1} \exp \left( - \frac{\sqrt{t}}{4^{\ell_1 \vee \ell_2}} | y_1 - y_2 | \right) e^{\frac{2}{16^{\ell_1 \vee \ell_2}}} \end{array}\)

where in the second line we absorbed into the Gaussian the polynomial obtained from differentiation, and the last inequality follows, with \(C := 4^{\ell_1 \vee \ell_2}\), from

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \frac{\sqrt{t}}{C} | y_1 - y_2 | - \frac{2}{C^2} & \leq & \frac{1}{8} \left( \sqrt{t} | x_{k_1} - x_{k_2} | - \frac{4}{C} \right)^2 + \frac{\sqrt{t}}{C} | y_1 - y_2 | - \frac{2}{C^2}\\ & = & \frac{t}{8} | x_{k_1} - x_{k_2} |^2 + \frac{\sqrt{t}}{C} (| y_1 - y_2 | - | x_{k_1} - x_{k_2} |) . \end{array}\)

Similar estimates hold when the contraction is \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x_{k_1} - y_2)\) and \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (y_1 - y_2)\), with the corresponding weights \(\omega_{- t}^{\Theta}\) estimated trivially by \(1\). The statement then follows from Minkowski's integral inequality, the multinomial theorem, Faà di Bruno, Hölder and Lemma 4.9.\(\Box\)

Proof. (of Theorem 1.2) Given \(\mathfrak{Q}^{(j)} \in \mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{loc}}_W\), by results of the above section we may pick germs \(q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle} \in \widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, i \alpha_j, y_j}^{\kappa_j, \sigma_j}\) with \(\kappa_j := \frac{| \mu_j |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha_j)^2 - \Im (\alpha_j)^2}{8 \pi}\), \(\sigma_j := \ell^{\ast}_j \delta - \kappa_j\). Choose

\(\displaystyle \ell^{\ast}_j := \left\lceil \frac{1 + \sum_{m = 1}^n \kappa_m + \frac{n}{8 \pi} \sum_{m = 1}^n \Im (\alpha_m)^2}{\delta} \right\rceil \) (6.6)

for any \(j \in 1 : n\), so that

\(\displaystyle \sigma_{1 : n} := \bigwedge_{j \in 1 : n} \left( \sigma_j - \sum_{m \in (1 : n) \setminus \{ j \}} \kappa_m \right) = \bigwedge_{j \in 1 : n} \left( \ell^{\ast}_j \delta - \sum_{m = 1}^n \kappa_m \right) \geq \frac{n}{8 \pi} \sum_{m = 1}^n \Im (\alpha_m)^2 + 1. \) (6.7)

Combining Lemma 6.5 with estimates on the kernels \(\unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell_j] (\alpha_j, k_j)}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell_j)}\), of the previous section and the fact that \(q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle} \in \widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, i \alpha_j, y_j}^{\kappa_j, \sigma_j}\), for any \(p \in [1, \infty)\) we obtain that for any \(j_1 \neq j_2 \in 1 : n\),

\(\displaystyle \left\| e^{- i \langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1 : n}, \varphi_t (\boldsymbol{y}_{1 : n}) \rangle} \operatorname{Tr} \left[ \dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_{j_1}] \langle j_1 \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \mathrm{D} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_{j_2}] \langle j_2 \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \right] \prod_{j \in 1 : n \setminus \{ j_1, j_2 \}} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\tilde{K}} e^{- C \sqrt{t}}\)

with \(\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1 : n} := \alpha_1 \veebar \ldots \veebar \alpha_n\), \(\boldsymbol{y}_{1 : n} := y_1 \veebar \ldots \veebar y_n\) for some \(\tilde{K}\) and \(C = C (\ell_{j_1}^{\ast}, \ell_{j_2}^{\ast}, y_{j_1}, y_{j_2})\). Hence the conditions of Lemma 2.17 are satisfied and thus we have that the product germ is

\(\displaystyle q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_{1 : n}^{\ast}] \langle 1 : n \rangle} := \prod_{j \in 1 : n} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle} \in \widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, i \boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1 : n}, \boldsymbol{y}_{1 : n}}^{\kappa_{1 : n}, \sigma_{1 : n}}\)

for \(\kappa_{1 : n} := \sum_{j = 1}^n \kappa_j\) and \(\sigma_{1 : n}\) defined above in (6.7). It is a consequence of (4.10) and \(| \mathcal{G}_{\infty} (x) | \lesssim 1\) for all \(x \neq 0\) that for any \(p \in [1, \infty)\), \(\boldsymbol{\alpha} \in \mathbb{C}^n\), and \(\boldsymbol{y} \in (\mathbb{R}^2)^n\) pairwise distinct, we have

\(\displaystyle \| e^{i \langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}, \varphi_t (\boldsymbol{y}) \rangle} \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim e^{\frac{p}{2} \sum_{j = 1}^n \Im (\alpha_j)^2 \mathcal{G}_t (0)} .\)

Indeed, \(\langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}, \varphi_t (\boldsymbol{y}) \rangle = \langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}, B_t (\boldsymbol{y}) \rangle + \langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}, Z_t (\boldsymbol{y}) \rangle\), \(Z_t\) is bounded in \(L^{\infty}\), and \(\langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}, B_t (\boldsymbol{y}) \rangle\) is a centered Gaussian process with variance \(\sum_{j, k = 1}^m \alpha_j \alpha_k \mathcal{G}_t (y_j - y_k)\).

Thus choosing \(p > 1\) small enough, by Hölder the germ \(q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_{1 : n}^{\ast}] \langle 1 : n \rangle}\) satisfies (2.10). Indeed, by (6.7)

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_{1 : n}^{\ast}] \langle 1 : n \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} & \leq & \| e^{i \langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1 : n}, \varphi_t (\boldsymbol{y}_{1 : n}) \rangle} \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \| e^{- i \langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1 : n}, \varphi_t (\boldsymbol{y}_{1 : n}) \rangle} \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_{1 : n}^{\ast}] \langle 1 : n \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^{p'} (\Omega)}\\ & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{p}{8 \pi} \sum_{j = 1}^n \Im (\alpha_j)^2} \langle t \rangle^{- \sigma_{1 : n} - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \| q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_{1 : n}^{\ast}] \langle 1 : n \rangle} \|_{\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, i \boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1 : n}, \boldsymbol{y}_{1 : n}}^{\kappa_{1 : n}, \sigma_{1 : n}}}\\ & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{p - n}{8 \pi} \sum_{j = 1}^n \Im (\alpha_j)^2 - 2} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \end{array}\)

and the statement follows. Coupled with Lemma 6.1, this shows that \(q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_{1 : n}^{\ast}] \langle 1 : n \rangle}\) is coherent.

For the existence of the observable product, it then remains to show that (2.13) holds. Denoting by \(r^{\langle j \rangle}\) the canonical Ito remainders (cf. (2.11)) corresponding to the germ \(q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle}\), and by \(r^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}\) the remainder corresponding to that of the product, we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \left\| \mathfrak{R} \left( \prod_{j \in 1 : n} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle} \right)_t - \prod_{j \in 1 : n} \mathfrak{Q}^{(j)}_t \right\|_{L^1 (\Omega)}\\ = & \left\| \prod_{j \in 1 : n} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) + r^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_{t, \infty} - \prod_{j \in 1 : n} (q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) + r^{\langle j \rangle}_{t, \infty}) \right\|_{L^1 (\Omega)}\\ \leq & \| r^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_{t, \infty} \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} + \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : n\\ I_2 \neq \emptyset \end{array}$}}} \left\| \prod_{j_1 \in I_1} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_{j_1}] \langle j_1 \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \prod_{j_2 \in I_2} r^{\langle j_2 \rangle}_{t, \infty} \right\|_{L^1 (\Omega)} \end{array}\)

where each term in the sum has at least one remainder \(r^{\langle j_2 \rangle}_{t, \infty}\) due to \(\prod_{j \in 1 : n} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle}\) being cancelled. The fact that \(\| r^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_{t, \infty} \|_{L^1 (\Omega)}\) vanishes as \(t \rightarrow \infty\) is clear from (6.7):

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \| r^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_{t, \infty} \|_{L^1 (\Omega)}\\ \leq & \int_t^{\infty} \langle s \rangle^{- \sigma_{1 : n} - 1} \| e^{i \langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1 : n}, \varphi_s (\boldsymbol{y}_{1 : n}) \rangle} \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \langle s \rangle^{\sigma_{1 : n} + 1} \| e^{- i \langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1 : n}, \varphi_s (\boldsymbol{y}_{1 : n}) \rangle} \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_{1 : n}^{\ast}] \langle 1 : n \rangle}_s (\varphi_s) \|_{L^{p'} (\Omega)} \mathrm{d} s\\ \lesssim & \int_t^{\infty} \langle s \rangle^{\frac{p - n}{8 \pi} \sum_{j = 1}^n \Im (\alpha_j)^2 - 2} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \mathrm{d} s \| q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_{1 : n}^{\ast}] \langle 1 : n \rangle} \|_{\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, i \boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1 : n}, \boldsymbol{y}_{1 : n}}^{\kappa_{1 : n}, \sigma_{1 : n}}} \end{array}\)

and one must choose \(p > 1\) small enough. Now let \(\varepsilon > 0\) and denote by \(q \geq 0\) for which \((n + \varepsilon)^{- 1} + q^{- 1} = n^{- 1}\). For any \(I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : n\) with \(I_2 \neq \emptyset\), by (6.6)

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \left\| \prod_{j_1 \in I_1} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_{j_1}] \langle j_1 \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \prod_{j_2 \in I_2} r^{\langle j_2 \rangle}_{t, \infty} \right\|_{L^1 (\Omega)}\\ \leq & \prod_{j_1 \in I_1} \left\| q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_{j_1}] \langle j_1 \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \right\|_{L^n (\Omega)} \prod_{j_2 \in I_2} \| r^{\langle j_2 \rangle}_{t, \infty} \|_{L^n (\Omega)}\\ \leq & \langle t \rangle^{\sum_{j_1 \in I_1} \kappa_{j_1}} \prod_{j_1 \in I_1} \left\| e^{i \langle \alpha_{j_1}, \varphi_t (y_{j_1}) \rangle} \right\|_{L^{n + \varepsilon} (\Omega)} \langle t \rangle^{- \kappa_{j_1}} \left\| e^{- i \langle \alpha_{j_1}, \varphi_t (y_{j_1}) \rangle} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \right\|_{L^q (\Omega)}\\ \times & \prod_{j_2 \in I_2} \int_t^{\infty} \langle s \rangle^{- \sigma_{j_2} - 1} \left\| e^{- \langle \alpha_{j_2}, \varphi_s (y_{j_2}) \rangle} \right\|_{L^{n + \varepsilon} (\Omega)} \langle s \rangle^{\sigma_{j_2} + 1} \left\| e^{- \langle \alpha_{j_2}, \varphi_s (y_{j_2}) \rangle} \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_{j_2}] \langle j_2 \rangle}_s (\varphi_s) \right\|_{L^q (\Omega)} \mathrm{d} s\\ \lesssim & \prod_{j \in 1 : n} \| q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] (j)} \|_{\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, \alpha_j, y_j}^{\kappa_j, \sigma_j}} \langle t \rangle^{\sum_{j_1 \in I_1} \kappa_{j_1} + \frac{n + \varepsilon}{8 \pi} \Im (\alpha_{j_1})^2} \prod_{j_2 \in I_2} \int_t^{\infty} \langle s \rangle^{\frac{n + \varepsilon}{8 \pi} \Im (\alpha_{j_2})^2 - \sigma_{j_2} - 1} \mathrm{d} s\\ \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{\sum_{j \in 1 : n} \frac{n + \varepsilon}{8 \pi} \Im (\alpha_j)^2 + \sum_{j_1 \in I_1} \kappa_j - \sum_{j_2 \in I_2} \sigma_{j_2}}\\ = & \langle t \rangle^{\sum_{j \in 1 : n} \kappa_j + \frac{n + \varepsilon}{8 \pi} \Im (\alpha_j)^2 - \sum_{j_2 \in I_2} \ell^{\ast}_{j_2} \delta}\\ \leq & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{\varepsilon}{8 \pi} \sum_{j \in 1 : n} \Im (\alpha_j)^2 - 1} . \end{array}\)

and choosing \(\varepsilon > 0\) small enough we obtain that the germs \(\{ q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle} \}_{j = 1}^n\) are compatible.

It remains to prove associativity, which, in light of Proposition 2.14, entails proving the existence of product with germs grouped together. Let \(P\) be an arbitrary partition of \(1 : n\). For any \(J \in P\), repeating the argument in the beginning of the proof, we obtain

\(\displaystyle q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_J^{\ast}] \langle J \rangle} := \prod_{j \in J} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle} \in \widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, i \boldsymbol{\alpha}_J, \boldsymbol{y}_J}^{\kappa_J, \sigma_J}\)

for \(\kappa_J := \sum_{j \in J} \kappa_j\), \(\boldsymbol{\alpha}_J := \veebar_{j \in J} \alpha_j\), \(\boldsymbol{y}_{1 : n} := \veebar_{j \in J} y_j\), \(\sigma_j := \ell^{\ast}_j \delta - \kappa_j\)

\(\displaystyle \sigma_J := \bigwedge_{j \in J} \left( \sigma_j - \sum_{m \in J \setminus \{ j \}} \kappa_m \right) = \bigwedge_{j \in J} \left( \ell^{\ast}_j \delta - \sum_{m \in J} \kappa_m \right) \geq \frac{n}{8 \pi} \sum_{m = 1}^n \Im (\alpha_m)^2 + \sum_{m \in (1 : n) \setminus J} \kappa_m + 1, \) (6.8)

and that the product \(\mathfrak{Q}^{(J)} := \bigstar_{j \in J} \mathfrak{Q}^{(j)} := \mathfrak{R} (q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_J^{\ast}] \langle J \rangle})\) exist. Denoting by \(r^{\langle J \rangle}\) the corresponding Ito remainders,

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \left\| \mathfrak{R} \left( \prod_{J \in P} q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_J^{\ast}] \langle J \rangle} \right)_t - \prod_{J \in P} \mathfrak{R} (q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_J^{\ast}] \langle J \rangle})_t \right\|_{L^1 (\Omega)}\\ = & \left\| \prod_{j \in 1 : n} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) + r^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_{t, \infty} - \prod_{J \in P} (q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_J^{\ast}] \langle J \rangle} (\varphi_t) + r^{\langle J \rangle}_{t, \infty}) \right\|_{L^1 (\Omega)}\\ \leq & \| r^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_{t, \infty} \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} + \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \mathscr{I}_1 \uplus \mathscr{I}_2 = P\\ \mathscr{I}_2 \neq \emptyset \end{array}$}}} \left\| \prod_{J_1 \in \mathscr{I}_1} q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_{J_1}^{\ast}] \langle J_1 \rangle} (\varphi_t) \prod_{J_2 \in \mathscr{I}_2} r^{\langle J_2 \rangle}_{t, \infty} \right\|_{L^1 (\Omega)} \end{array}\)

again \(\mathscr{I}_2\) nonempty because of the cancellation of \(\prod_{J \in P} q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_J^{\ast}] \langle J \rangle}\). Denoting by \(\Im (\boldsymbol{\alpha}_J)^2 := \sum_{j \in J} \Im (\alpha_j)^2\) and adapting the argument above, for all \(\mathscr{I}_1 \uplus \mathscr{I}_2 = P\) with \(\mathscr{I}_2 \neq \emptyset\) we obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \left\| \prod_{J_1 \in \mathscr{I}_1} q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_{J_1}^{\ast}] \langle J_1 \rangle} (\varphi_t) \prod_{J_2 \in \mathscr{I}_2} r^{\langle J_2 \rangle}_{t, \infty} \right\|_{L^1 (\Omega)}\\ \leq & \prod_{J_1 \in \mathscr{I}_1} \left\| q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_{J_1}^{\ast}] \langle J_1 \rangle} (\varphi_t) \right\|_{L^{| P |} (\Omega)} \prod_{J_2 \in \mathscr{I}_2} \| r^{\langle J_2 \rangle}_{t, \infty} \|_{L^{| P |} (\Omega)}\\ \lesssim & \prod_{J \in P} \| q^{[\leq \boldsymbol{\ell}_J^{\ast}] \langle J \rangle} \|_{\widehat{\mathscr{O}}_{\infty -, i \boldsymbol{\alpha}_J, \boldsymbol{y}_J}^{\kappa_J, \sigma_J}} \langle t \rangle^{\sum_{J_1 \in \mathscr{I}_1} \kappa_{J_1} + \frac{| P | + \varepsilon}{8 \pi} \Im (\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{J_1})^2} \prod_{J_2 \in \mathscr{I}_2} \int_t^{\infty} \langle s \rangle^{\frac{| P | + \varepsilon}{8 \pi} \Im (\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{J_2})^2 - \sigma_{J_2} - 1} \mathrm{d} s\\ \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| P | + \varepsilon}{8 \pi} \sum_{J \in P} \Im (\boldsymbol{\alpha}_J)^2 + \sum_{J_1 \in \mathscr{I}_1} \kappa_{J_1} - {\sum_{J_2 \in \mathscr{I}_2}} \sigma_{J_2}}\\ \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| P | - n + \varepsilon}{8 \pi} \Im (\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1 : n})^2 - 1} \end{array}\)

where the last line follows from (6.8) and \(\mathscr{I}_1 \cap \mathscr{I}_2 = \emptyset\). Since \(| P | \leq n\), choosing \(\varepsilon > 0\) small enough we obtain that the product \(\bigstar_{J \in P} \mathfrak{Q}^{(J)}\) exist; it then follows from Proposition 2.14 that

\(\displaystyle \bigstar_{j \in 1 : n} \mathfrak{Q}^{(j)} = \bigstar_{J \in P} (\bigstar_{j \in J} \mathfrak{Q}^{(j)})\)

for any partition \(P\) of \(1 : n\), as requested.

The claimed algebraic properties of the product then follow from Proposition 2.14.\(\Box\)

Remark 6.6. Combining the compatibility of the germs with Remark 6.4, it follows from Proposition 2.13 that the observable products do not depend on the order of perturbative approximation.

Corollary 6.7. The space of multilocal observables

\(\displaystyle \mathscr{O}_{\operatorname{mloc}}^W := \operatorname{span} \{ \bigstar_{j \in 1 : n} \mathfrak{Q}^{(j)} (y_j) : n \in \mathbb{N}, \mathfrak{Q}^{(j)} \in \mathscr{O}_{\operatorname{loc}}^W, y_i \neq y_j \operatorname{if}i \neq j \}\)

is well-defined.

7Smoothness of correlation functions

This section is concerned with the proof of Theorem 1.3.

In the above section we saw that there exist \(q^{\langle j \rangle} := q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_j] \langle j \rangle}\) coherent germs for \(\mathfrak{Q}^{(j)}\), given by the ansatz (6.1), such that \((q^{\langle 1 \rangle}, \ldots, q^{\langle n \rangle})\) are compatible (cf. Theorem 1.2). By Ito reconstruction, we have

\(\displaystyle \langle \mathfrak{Q}^{(1)} (y_1) \star \ldots \star \mathfrak{Q}^{(n)} (y_n) \rangle = q^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_0 (\varphi_0) + \mathbb{E} \int_0^{\infty} \mathscr{L}^V q^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_s (\varphi_s) \mathrm{d} s \) (7.1)

with \(q^{\langle 1 : n \rangle} := \prod_{j \in 1 : n} q^{\langle j \rangle}\) the product germ.

Both of the above terms contain reference to the germ kernels \(\gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t\). The difficulty of the proof is that such kernels are not smooth in the localization point \(y\). Indeed, differentiating the kernels in \(y\) may destroy the inductions of Section 5: looking e.g. at (5.18), one obtains a term with \(\partial_y^{\nu} \mathbf{G}_{t, s} (y, \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})\). The following simple example shows that there indeed exist non-smooth germ kernels.

Example 7.1. Let \(\alpha > 0\) real and consider the kernel \(\gamma_t := \gamma^{[2]}_t (\xi_1, y)\) for the vertex field with \(\sigma_1 := - 1\). With \(r := x_1 - y\), the covariance is

\(\displaystyle \dot{\mathbf{G}}_t (y, \xi_1) = - \left( \frac{\alpha^2}{2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) + \frac{\beta^2}{2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) - \alpha \beta \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (r) \right) .\)

Referring back to Lemma 5.5 and (5.20), we have, for a nonzero constant \(C (t)\) depending on \(t\),

\(\displaystyle \gamma_t (r) = C (t) \alpha \beta \int_t^{\infty} e^{\alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (r)} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (r) \mathrm{d} s = C (t) (e^{\alpha \beta \mathcal{G}_{t, \infty} (r)} - 1) .\)

As \(| r | \rightarrow 0\), we have

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{G}_{t, \infty} (r) = \frac{1}{4 \pi} \int_t^{\infty} s^{- 1} e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}} e^{- \frac{s}{4} | r |^2} \mathrm{d} s = \frac{1}{4 \pi} \log (| r |^{- 2}) + g_t (r)\)

for \(g_t (\cdot)\) uniformly bounded. Hence \(\gamma_t (r) \asymp_t | r |^{- \frac{\alpha \beta}{2 \pi}}\).

To circumvent this problem, we have to shift all derivatives away from the germ kernels. This is possible by the following simple but crucial

Lemma 7.2. Let \(\gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t\) be a kernel obtained in Section 5. Then for any \(h \in \mathbb{R}^2\),

\(\displaystyle \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t (\sigma_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, x_1, \ldots, x_{\ell - 1}, y) = \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t (\sigma_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, x_1 + h, \ldots, x_{\ell - 1} + h, y + h) .\)

Proof. It is straightforward to see inductively from the flow equations for the germ kernels (Lemmas 5.1 5.5 5.11, 5.14) that they inherit translation invariance from the fluctuation covariance \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t\) and the potential kernels \(v^{[\ell]}_t\).\(\Box\)

Proof. (of Theorem 1.3)

To simplify notation, write \(I := 1 : (\ell - 1)\).

Smoothness of \(q^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_0 (\varphi_0)\)

Fix an arbitrary charge configuration \(\sigma_I\). Denoting by

\(\displaystyle \Phi (y, x_I) := \phi^k (y) e^{i \alpha \phi (y)} \prod_{j \in I} \rho (x_j) e^{i \beta \sigma_j \phi (x_j)},\)

the field content, by translation invariance

\(\displaystyle \int \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t (x_I, y) \partial^{\mu}_{y, I} \Phi (y, x_I) \mathrm{d} x_I = \int \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t (z_I, 0) [\partial^{\mu}_{y, I} \Phi (y, x_I)]_{x_I = y + z_I} \mathrm{d} z_I .\)

Since \(\varphi_0 = 0\), we have that \(q_0 (\varphi_0)\) is a sum of terms of the form

\(\displaystyle \int \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, 0)}_0 (z_I, 0) [\partial_I^{\mu} \rho (x_I)]_{x_I = y + z_I} \mathrm{d} z_I .\)

Since \(\rho \in \mathcal{C}^{\infty}_b\) and the kernel estimates imply \(\gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_0 (\cdot, 0) \in L^1\), by the Leibniz integral rule we obtain that \(y \mapsto q^{\langle j \rangle}_0 (\varphi_0)\) is smooth.

Smoothness of \(\mathbb{E} \int_0^{\infty} \mathscr{L}^V q^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_s (\varphi_s) \mathrm{d} s\)

The generator of the product is

\(\displaystyle \mathscr{L}^V q^{\langle 1 : n \rangle} = \sum_j \mathscr{L}^V q^{\langle j \rangle} \prod_{k \neq j} q^{\langle k \rangle} + \sum_{j < k} \operatorname{Tr} [\dot{G} \mathrm{D} q^{\langle j \rangle} \mathrm{D} q^{\langle k \rangle}] \prod_{r \neq j, k} q^{\langle r \rangle} .\)

Recall from the proofs of Section 5 the formulae for \(\langle \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell]}_t (\phi), \dot{G}_t R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \rangle\): we have

\(\displaystyle \langle \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell]}_t (\phi), \dot{G}_t R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \rangle = \sum \int \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t (\xi_I, y) \Upsilon_t (\phi, \xi_I, y) \mathrm{d} \xi_I\)

with \(\Upsilon_t\) functions depending on point evaluations of \(\dot{G}_t R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t\). By translation invariance,

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \int \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t (x_I, y) \Upsilon_t (\phi, x_I, y) \mathrm{d} x_I & = & \int \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t (z_I, 0) \Upsilon_t (\phi, z_I + y, y) \mathrm{d} z_I . \end{array}\)

Hence we have, for any \(\nu\) multiindex, that

\(\displaystyle \partial^{\nu}_y \langle \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell]}_t (\phi), \dot{G}_t R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \rangle = \sum \int \gamma^{[\ell] (\alpha, k)}_t (\xi_I, 0) \partial^{\nu}_y \Upsilon_t (\phi, \xi_I + y, y) \mathrm{d} \xi_I\)

where the validity of the Leibniz integral rule is checked similarly to the end of the proof of Lemma 5.10. Then

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \sup_y \| e^{- i \alpha \varphi_t (y)} \partial^{\nu}_y \mathscr{L}^V q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} & \leq & \sup_y \sum_{\ell = 1}^{\ell^{\ast}} \| e^{- i \alpha \varphi_t (y)} \partial^{\nu}_y \langle \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell]}_t, \dot{G}_t R^{[> (\ell^{\ast} - \ell)]}_t \rangle \|_{L^p (\Omega)}\\ & \lesssim & \sup_y \sum_{\ell = 1}^{\ell^{\ast}} \unicode{x2980} \gamma^{[\ell]}_t \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell)} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \mu | + | \nu |}{2} - 1 - \delta - (\ell^{\ast} - \ell) \delta} \mathfrak{l}_t^c\\ & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \nu |}{2} + \kappa - \ell^{\ast} \delta - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \end{array} \) (7.2)

for any \(p \geq 1\) with the standard \(\kappa\) of the previous section (i.e. \(\kappa := \frac{| \mu |}{2} + \frac{\Re (\alpha)^2 - \Im (\alpha)^2}{8 \pi}\)) with constants independent of \(y\).

Similarly, we may shift the derivatives away from the kernels in (6.1) to the field content to obtain

\(\displaystyle \sup_y \| e^{- i \alpha \varphi_t (y)} \partial^{\nu}_y q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}]}_t (\varphi_t) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \langle t \rangle^{\frac{| \nu |}{2} + \kappa} \mathfrak{l}_t^c . \) (7.3)

Fix a compact \(K \subseteq (\mathbb{R}^2)^n \setminus \operatorname{Diag}_n\) and let \(j_1 \neq j_2 \in 1 : n\). For any multiindices \(\nu_{j_1}, \nu_{j_2}\), we may shift the derivatives away from the kernels in \(\partial^{\nu_{j_1}}_{y_{j_1}} \partial^{\nu_{j_2}}_{y_{j_2}} \operatorname{Tr} \left[ \dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_{j_1}] \langle j_1 \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \mathrm{D} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_{j_2}] \langle j_2 \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \right]\) to the field content and the heat kernel contraction \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t\) (see e.g. (6.5)). Thus denoting by \(\boldsymbol{\alpha} := \alpha_{j_1} \veebar \alpha_{j_2}\), \(\boldsymbol{y} := y_{j_1} \veebar y_{j_2}\), the proof of Lemma 6.5 goes through and we obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \sup_{y_{1 : n} \in K} \left\| e^{- i \langle \boldsymbol{\alpha}, \varphi_t (\boldsymbol{y}) \rangle} \partial^{\nu_{j_1}}_{y_{j_1}} \partial^{\nu_{j_2}}_{y_{j_2}} \operatorname{Tr} \left[ \dot{G}_t \mathrm{D} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_{j_1}] \langle j_1 \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \mathrm{D} q^{[\leq \ell^{\ast}_{j_2}] \langle j_2 \rangle}_t (\varphi_t) \right] \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)}\\ \lesssim & \sup_{y_{1 : n} \in K} \langle t \rangle^M e^{- C \sqrt{t} | y_{j_1} - y_{j_2} |}\\ \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^M e^{- \tilde{C} \sqrt{t} d_K} \end{array} \) (7.4)

for some \(M\) depending on \(\nu_{j_1}, \nu_{j_2}\), where

\(\displaystyle d_K := \inf_{y_{1 : n} \in K} \min_{j < k} | y_j - y_k | > 0\)

by the assumption \(K \subseteq (\mathbb{R}^2)^n \setminus \operatorname{Diag}_n\).
