Fix \(m \in \mathbb{N}\). By choosing \(\ell^{\ast}_j = \ell^{\ast}_j (m)\) large enough, (7.2), (7.3), (7.4) and Lemma 4.6 yields that for any multiindindex tuple \(\boldsymbol{\nu} = \nu_1, \ldots, \nu_n\) with \(| \boldsymbol{\nu} | \leq m\), we have

\(\displaystyle \int_0^{\infty} \sup_{y_{1 : n} \in K} \| \partial^{\nu_1}_{y_1} \ldots \partial^{\nu_n}_{y_n} \mathscr{L}^V q^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_s (\varphi_s) \|_{L^1 (\Omega)} \mathrm{d} s < \infty\)

(cf. the proof of Theorem 1.2; see also Remark 6.4). For each fixed \(t > 0\), the bounds above also imply continuity of \((y_1, \ldots, y_n) \mapsto \partial^{\nu_1}_{y_1} \ldots \partial^{\nu_n}_{y_n} \mathscr{L}^V q^{\langle 1 : n \rangle}_s (\varphi_s)\) in \(L^1 (\Omega)\). Thus we may differentiate under the integrals in (7.1), and the statement follows from dominated convergence.\(\Box\)

AHeat kernel interpolation

A.1Heat kernel estimates

Here we collect some standard heat kernel estimates. Recall that \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_s\) and \(k^Q_s\) denote the \(L^2\) integral kernels of \(\dot{G}_s\) and \(Q_s\), respectively, cf. (2.7). Since the bounds are needed mostly for the part of the paper concerned with the special case of the sine–Gordon model, we take \(d = 2\) unless explicitly stated otherwise.

Lemma A.1. There is a uniformly bounded function \(h\) such that

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{G}_t (0) = \frac{1}{4 \pi} \log (1 \vee t) + h (t)\)

Proof. Simply split

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \mathcal{G}_t (0) & = & \frac{1}{4 \pi} \int_0^{1 \wedge t} s^{- 1} e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}} \mathrm{d} s + \frac{\mathbb{1}_{t > 1}}{4 \pi} \int_1^t s^{- 1} e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}} \mathrm{d} s\\ & = & \frac{1}{4 \pi} \int_0^{1 \wedge t} s^{- 1} e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}} \mathrm{d} s + \frac{\mathbb{1}_{t > 1}}{4 \pi} \int_1^t s^{- 1} \left( e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}} - 1 \right) \mathrm{d} s + \frac{\mathbb{1}_{t > 1}}{4 \pi} \log (t)\\ & =: & h (t) + \frac{1}{4 \pi} \log (1 \vee t) \end{array}\)
\(\Box\)

Lemma A.2. For any \(\varsigma, \eta, \alpha \geq 0\), we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x) | \cdot |^{\alpha} e^{\varsigma \sqrt{t} | x |} \langle x \rangle^{\eta} \mathrm{d} x & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{- 2 - \frac{\alpha}{2}} \end{array}\)

Proof. Indeed, since \(\varsigma \geq 0\), going to spherical coordinates yields

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x) e^{\varsigma \sqrt{t} | x |} | \cdot |^{\alpha} \langle x \rangle^{\eta} \mathrm{d} x & \lesssim & t^{- 1} e^{- \frac{m^2}{t}} \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} e^{- \frac{t}{4} | x |^2 + \varsigma \sqrt{t} | x |} | \cdot |^{\alpha} \langle x \rangle^{\eta} \mathrm{d} x\\ & \lesssim & \langle t \rangle^{- 2 - \frac{\alpha}{2}} . \end{array}\)
\(\Box\)

Lemma A.3. For any \(\varkappa \in [0, 2]\) we have

\(\displaystyle \sup_{x \in \mathbb{R}^2} \left| \frac{\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) - \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x)}{| x |^{\varkappa}} \right| \lesssim {\langle t \rangle^{\frac{\varkappa}{2} - 1}} \)

Proof. Using \(1 - e^{- u} \leq 1 \wedge u\) for \(u \geq 0\), we have

\(\displaystyle \frac{\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) - \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x)}{| x |^{\varkappa}} \asymp t^{- 1} e^{- \frac{m^2}{t}} \left( \frac{1 - e^{- \frac{t}{4} | x |^2}}{| x |^{\varkappa}} \right) \leq t^{- 1} e^{- \frac{m^2}{t}} \left( | x |^{- \varkappa} \wedge \frac{t}{4} | x |^{2 - \varkappa} \right)\)

and the statement follows from splitting the cases \(| x | \leq 2 / \sqrt{t}\) and \(| x | \geq 2 / \sqrt{t}\).\(\Box\)

Lemma A.4. Fix an arbitrary \(\alpha \in (0, 1]\). For any \(x \in \mathbb{R}^2\) and any \(t \in \mathbb{R}_+\) we have

\(\displaystyle | \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x) - \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (0) | \lesssim | x |^{\alpha} \langle t \rangle^{\frac{\alpha}{2} - 1} . \) (A.1)

Proof. Let \(f^{\alpha}_t : \mathbb{R}_+ \rightarrow \mathbb{R}\) be given by \(f^{\alpha}_t (y) := t^{- 1} \exp \left( - \frac{m^2}{t} - \frac{t}{4} y^{2 / \alpha} \right)\). Then

\(\displaystyle | \nabla_y f_t^{\alpha} | \asymp y^{\frac{2}{\alpha} - 1} e^{- \frac{m^2}{t}} e^{- \frac{t}{4} y^{2 / \alpha}}\)

attains its maximum at \(y^{\ast} = C t^{- \alpha / 2}\), so for all \(y \in \mathbb{R}_+\)

\(\displaystyle | f^{\alpha}_t (y) - f^{\alpha}_t (0) | \lesssim y \sup_{y \in \mathbb{R}_+} | \nabla_y f_t^{\alpha} | \lesssim y e^{- \frac{m^2}{t}} t^{\frac{\alpha}{2} - 1} .\)

We then conclude from \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t\) being radial and noting that with \(r = | x |\) we have \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x) = f^{\alpha}_t (r^{\alpha})\).\(\Box\)

Lemma A.5. For any multiindex \(\mu \in \mathbb{N}_0^2\) we have

\(\displaystyle \| \partial^{\mu} k^Q_s \|_{L^1} \lesssim \langle s \rangle^{\frac{| \mu |}{2} - 1} \) (A.2)

Proof. Denote \(g_s := e^{- \frac{s}{2} | \cdot |^2}\). Note that \(g_s = g_1 \circ f\) where \(f (x) := \sqrt{s} x\), thus by chain rule

\(\displaystyle \partial^{\mu} g_s (x) = s^{\frac{| \mu |}{2}} (\partial^{\mu} g_1) \left( \sqrt{s} x \right) . \) (A.3)

Hence

\(\displaystyle \| \partial^{\mu} g_s \|_{L^1} = \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} | \partial^{\mu} g_s (x) | \mathrm{d} x = s^{\frac{| \mu |}{2}} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \left| (\partial^{\mu} g_1) \left( \sqrt{s} x \right) \right| \mathrm{d} x = s^{\frac{| \mu | - d}{2}} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} | (\partial^{\mu} g_1) (\tilde{x}) | \mathrm{d} \tilde{x} \lesssim s^{\frac{| \mu | - d}{2}} .\)
\(\Box\)

Lemma A.6. Let \(\mu \in \mathbb{N}_0^2\) multiindex. We have for any \(s \in \mathbb{R}_+\), \(c \geq 0\) that

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} | \partial^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (x_1 - y) - \partial^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (x_2 - y) | & \lesssim & | x_1 - x_2 | e^{- c \sqrt{s} | x_1 - y |} e^{c \sqrt{s} | x_1 - x_2 |} e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}} s^{\frac{| \mu | - 1}{2}}\\ | \partial^{\mu} k^Q_s (x_1 - y) - \partial^{\mu} k^Q_s (x_2 - y) | & \lesssim & | x_1 - x_2 | e^{- c \sqrt{s} | x_1 - y |} e^{c \sqrt{s} | x_1 - x_2 |} e^{- \frac{m^2}{2 s}} s^{\frac{| \mu | + 1}{2}} \end{array}\)

Proof. Denote by \(z_i := x_i - y\). Taylor expansion yields with \(z (\vartheta) := z_1 - \vartheta (z_1 - z_2)\), \(\vartheta \in [0, 1]\)

\(\displaystyle | \partial^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (z_1) - \partial^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (z_2) | \leq | z_1 - z_2 | \int_0^1 | \nabla \partial^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (z (\vartheta)) | \mathrm{d} \vartheta .\)

We have \(| z_1 | = | z_1 - \vartheta (z_1 - z_2) + \vartheta (z_1 - z_2) | \leq | z_1 - \vartheta (z_1 - z_2) | + | z_1 - z_2 |\), so as \(\vartheta \in [0, 1]\)

\(\displaystyle - | z_1 - \vartheta (z_1 - z_2) | \leq | z_1 - z_2 | - | z_1 | . \) (A.4)

On the other hand, since for all \(b > 0,\) \(r \in \mathbb{R}\) it holds that \(\exp (- b r^2) \leq \exp (c^2 / 4 b) \exp (- c r)\), choosing \(r := \sqrt{s} | \xi |\), \(b := 1 / 8\) we have for all \(\xi\) that

\(\displaystyle | \nabla \partial^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (\xi) | \lesssim_{\mu} s^{\frac{| \mu | + 1}{2}} e^{- \frac{s}{8} | \xi |^2} e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}} s^{- 1} \lesssim_c s^{\frac{| \mu | - 1}{2}} e^{- c \sqrt{s} | \xi |} e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}}\)

Combining this with (A.4), taking \(\xi := z_1 - \vartheta (z_1 - z_2)\) we obtain

\(\displaystyle | \nabla \partial^{\mu} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (z (\vartheta)) | \lesssim s^{\frac{| \mu | - 1}{2}} e^{c \sqrt{s} | z_1 - z_2 |} e^{- c \sqrt{s} | z_1 |} e^{- \frac{m^2}{s}}\)

proving the estimate for \(\dot{\mathcal{G}}_s\). For \(k^Q_s\), we proceed the same way.\(\Box\)

A.2Proof of Lemma 3.5

Proof. (of Lemma 3.5) Recall that we want to prove the following: given \(t \leq s\), there exists a constant \(C\) such that for all \(x_1, x_2, z \in \mathbb{R}^2\) with \(| x_g - x_b | \leq | x_b - z |\) we have

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_b - z) - \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_g - z) \leq C.\)

If \(| x_g - z | \leq | x_b - z |\), then

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_b - z) - \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_g - z) \leq 0.\)

Thus from now on assume \(| x_b - z | \leq | x_g - z |\). Denoting by \(r := | x_g - x_b |\), if \(r = 0\), then

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_b - z) - \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_g - z) = 0\)

so that we may assume \(r > 0\). Then we split the scales \([t, s]\) according to \(r\):

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_b - z) - \mathcal{G}_{t, s} (x_g - z)\\ = & \int_t^s \mathbb{1}_{\{ u \leq r^{- 2} \}} (\dot{\mathcal{G}}_u (x_b - z) - \dot{\mathcal{G}}_u (x_g - z)) \mathrm{d} u + \int_t^s \mathbb{1}_{\{ u \geq r^{- 2} \}} (\dot{\mathcal{G}}_u (x_b - z) - \dot{\mathcal{G}}_u (x_g - z)) \mathrm{d} u\\ = & \mathcal{I}_{\operatorname{low}} + \mathcal{I}_{\operatorname{high}} . \end{array}\)

In the low region, the Gaussian is flat and the mean value theorem is a good approximation: using (A.1)

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} | \mathcal{I}_{\operatorname{low}} | & \lesssim & r \int_t^s \mathbb{1}_{\{ u \leq r^{- 2} \}} \langle u \rangle^{- \frac{1}{2}} \mathrm{d} u\\ & \lesssim & r (s \wedge r^{- 2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ & \lesssim & 1. \end{array}\)

In the high region we exploit the exponential decay of the Gaussian: since \(r \leq | x_g - z |, | x_b - z |\)

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} | \mathcal{I}_{\operatorname{high}} | & \leq & 2 \int_t^s \mathbb{1}_{\{ u \geq r^{- 2} \}} | \dot{\mathcal{G}}_u (r) | \mathrm{d} u\\ & \lesssim & \int_{r^{- 2}}^{\infty} u^{- 1} e^{- \frac{m^2}{u}} e^{- u \frac{r^2}{4}} \mathrm{d} u\\ & \lesssim & 1 + \int_{r^{- 2} \vee 1}^{\infty} u^{- 1} e^{- u \frac{r^2}{4}} \mathrm{d} u\\ & \lesssim & 1 + \int_{1 / 4}^{\infty} w^{- 1} e^{- w} \mathrm{d} w\\ & = & 1 + E_1 (1 / 4) \end{array}\)

yielding the statement.\(\Box\)

A.3Scale decomposition of the free field

In this section we prove some standard technical results about the heat kernel decomposition \(B_t := \int_0^t Q_s \mathrm{d} W_s\) of the Gaussian free field.

For the Wiener integral \(\int_0^t Q_s \mathrm{d} W_s\) to be well-defined, by standard infinite-dimensional stochastic integration theory [20] we have to show the following three properties:

  1. \(Q_t \in \mathcal{L}_2 (L^2 ; \mathcal{H})\) for every \(t\), i.e. \(Q_t\) is Hilbert–Schmidt when viewed as an operator from \(L^2\) to H.

  2. The process \((Q_t)_{t \in \mathbb{R}_+}\) is predictable with respect to the canonical filtration of \(W\).

  3. For every \(T \in \mathbb{R}_+\), it has to hold that

    \(\displaystyle \mathbb{P} \left[ \int_0^T \| Q_t \|^2_{\mathcal{L}_2 (L^2 ; \mathcal{H})} \mathrm{d} t < \infty \right] = 1.\)

We take \(\mathcal{H}\) to be the weighted Sobolev space \(\mathcal{H} := B^k_{2, 2} (\langle x \rangle^{- \ell})\) with \(k, \ell > 0\).

Lemma A.7. Suppose \(\ell > d / 2\). Then \(Q_t \in \mathcal{L}_2 (L^2 ; \mathcal{H})\).

Proof. By the Fourier multiplier property of the weighted Sobolev spaces (cf. subsequent remark), for an ONB \((e_n)_{n \in \mathbb{N}}\) of \(L^2\),

\(\displaystyle \| Q_t \|_{\mathcal{L}_2 (L^2 ; \mathcal{H})}^2 = \sum_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \| Q_t e_n \|^2_{\mathcal{H}} \asymp \sum_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \| \langle \cdot \rangle^{- \ell} (- \Delta + 1)^{k / 2} Q_t e_n \|^2_{L^2} = \| \langle \cdot \rangle^{- \ell} (- \Delta + 1)^{k / 2} Q_t \|_{\mathcal{L}_2 (L^2 ; L^2)}^2\)

That is, \(Q_t\) is Hilbert–Schmidt as an operator viewed as \(L^2 \rightarrow \mathcal{H}\) if and only if \(\langle \cdot \rangle^{- \ell} (- \Delta + 1)^{k / 2} Q_t\), viewed as \(L^2 \rightarrow L^2\), is Hilbert–Schmidt. Now consider the kernel

\(\displaystyle K_t (x, y) := \frac{\langle x \rangle^{- \ell}}{(2 \pi)^d} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \underbrace{(| \xi |^2 + 1)^{k / 2} \left( \frac{1}{t^2} e^{\frac{- | \xi |^2 - m^2}{t}} \right)^{1 / 2}}_{:= H_t (\xi)} e^{i \langle y - x, \xi \rangle} \mathrm{d} \xi = \langle x \rangle^{- \ell} \mathcal{F}^{- 1} (H_t) (x - y)\)

Since \(H_t \in \mathcal{S} (\mathbb{R}^d)\), we have that \(\mathcal{F}^{- 1} (H_t) \in \mathcal{S} (\mathbb{R}^d)\), and a standard computation involving polar coordinates yields \(\| \langle x \rangle^{- \ell} \|_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}^d)}^2 < \infty\) when \(\ell > d / 2\), so

\(\displaystyle \| K_t \|^2_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}^d)} = \| \langle \cdot \rangle^{- \ell} \|_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}^d)}^2 \| \mathcal{F}^{- 1} (H_t) \|_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}^d)}^2 < \infty .\)

Hence by Theorem 2.11 of [45], it defines a Hilbert–Schmidt integral operator \(A_t : L^2 \rightarrow L^2\) with

\(\displaystyle \| A_t \|_{\mathcal{L}_2 (L^2 ; L^2)} = \| K_t \|_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}^d \times \mathbb{R}^d)}\)

On the other hand, for any \(f \in \mathcal{S}\),

\begin{eqnarray*} \langle x \rangle^{- \ell} (Q_t (- \Delta + 1)^{k / 2} f) (x) & = & \langle x \rangle^{- \ell} \mathcal{F}^{- 1} \left( (| \xi |^2 + 1)^{\frac{k}{2}} \left( \frac{1}{t^2} e^{\frac{- | \xi |^2 - m^2}{t}} \right)^{\frac{1}{2}} \mathcal{F} (f) \right) (x)\\ & = & \frac{\langle x \rangle^{- \ell}}{(2 \pi)^d} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} (| \xi |^2 + 1)^{\frac{k}{2}} \left( \frac{1}{t^2} e^{\frac{- | \xi |^2 - m^2}{t}} \right)^{\frac{1}{2}} \left( \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} e^{- i \langle y, \xi \rangle} f (y) \mathrm{d} y \right) e^{i \langle x, \xi \rangle} \mathrm{d} \xi\\ & = & \frac{\langle x \rangle^{- \ell}}{(2 \pi)^d} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} (| \xi |^2 + 1)^{\frac{k}{2}} \left( \frac{1}{t^2} e^{\frac{- | \xi |^2 - m^2}{t}} \right)^{\frac{1}{2}} e^{i \langle x - y, \xi \rangle} f (y) \mathrm{d} y \mathrm{d} \xi\\ & = & \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \left( \frac{\langle x \rangle^{- \ell}}{(2 \pi)^d} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} (| \xi |^2 + 1)^{\frac{k}{2}} \left( \frac{1}{t^2} e^{\frac{- | \xi |^2 - m^2}{t}} \right)^{\frac{1}{2}} e^{i \langle x - y, \xi \rangle} \mathrm{d} \xi \right) f (y) \mathrm{d} y\\ & = & \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} K_t (x, y) f (y) \mathrm{d} y \end{eqnarray*}

so \(A \equiv \langle x \rangle^{- \ell} Q_t (- \Delta + 1)^{k / 2}\).\(\Box\)

Remark A.8. Weighted Besov spaces \(B_{p, q}^{s, w}\) are defined by multiplying each Littlewood–Paley block with the weight function, “inside” the \(L^p\) and \(\ell^q\) norms. However, it can be shown (section 4.2.2 of [25]) that the norm \(\| w f \|_{B^s_{p, q}}\) is an equivalent norm for \(B_{p, q}^{s, w}\). Moreover, it is well-known that unweighted Besov spaces for \(p = q = 2\) coincide with Sobolev spaces. Hence, in particular, we have

\(\displaystyle \| f \|_{B_{2, 2}^{s, w}} \asymp \| w f \|_{B^s_{2, 2}} \asymp \| (- \Delta + 1)^{s / 2} (w f) \|_{L^2} .\)

On the other hand, by Proposition on p.158 of [25], we have, just as in the unweighted case, the Fourier multiplier property

\(\displaystyle \| f \|_{B_{p, q}^{s, w}} \asymp \| (- \Delta + 1)^{k / 2} (f) \|_{B_{p, q}^{s - k, w}} .\)

Hence

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| (- \Delta + 1)^{s / 2} (w f) \|_{L^2} & \asymp & \| f \|_{B_{2, 2}^{s, w}}\\ & \asymp & \| (- \Delta + 1)^{s / 2} (f) \|_{B_{2, 2}^{0, w}}\\ & \asymp & \| w (- \Delta + 1)^{s / 2} (f) \|_{B^0_{2, 2}}\\ & = & \| w (- \Delta + 1)^{s / 2} (f) \|_{L^2} \end{array}\)

Lemma A.9. Let \(\ell > d / 2\) and \(\mathcal{H} := B^{k, \ell}_{2, 2} = B^k_{2, 2} (\langle x \rangle^{- \ell})\). For all \(T \in \mathbb{R}_+\), if \(k + d / 2 > 1\), then

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [\| B_T \|_{\mathcal{H}}^2] = \int_0^T \| Q_t \|^2_{\mathcal{L}_2 (L^2 ; \mathcal{H})} \mathrm{d} t \lesssim_{\ell, k, d} 1 + T^{k + d / 2 - 1} ; \) (A.5)

if \(d = 2\), \(k = 0\), then

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [\| B_T \|_{\mathcal{H}}^2] = \int_0^T \| Q_t \|^2_{\mathcal{L}_2 (L^2 ; \mathcal{H})} \mathrm{d} t \lesssim_{\ell, k, d} 1 + \log (1 \vee T) . \) (A.6)

Proof. The fact that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} \left[ \left\| \int_0^T Q_s \mathrm{d} W_s \right\|_{\mathcal{H}}^2 \right] = \int_0^T \| Q_t \|^2_{\mathcal{L}_2 (L^2 ; \mathcal{H})} \mathrm{d} t\)

is the infinite dimensional version of Ito isometry (see e.g. (4.30) in [20]). To prove the estimates, we use the previous proof, a change of variables \((x, y) \mapsto (x', x' - y')\) and Plancherel to obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \int_0^T \| Q_t \|^2_{\mathcal{L}_2 (L^2 ; \mathcal{H})} \mathrm{d} t & = & \int_0^T \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} (\langle x \rangle^{- \ell} \mathcal{F}^{- 1} (H_t) (x - y))^2 \mathrm{d} x \mathrm{d} y \mathrm{d} t\\ & = & \| \langle x \rangle^{- \ell} \|_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}^d)}^2 \int_0^T \| \mathcal{F}^{- 1} (H_t) \|_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}^d)}^2 \mathrm{d} t\\ & \lesssim_{\ell} & \int_0^T \frac{1}{t^2} e^{\frac{- m^2}{t}} \left( \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} (| \xi |^2 + 1)^k e^{\frac{- | \xi |^2}{t}} \mathrm{d} \xi \right) \mathrm{d} t. \end{array}\)

Now we focus on the \(\mathrm{d} \xi\) part of the above integral. Considering the change of variables \(\xi \mapsto \sqrt{t} \xi\),

\(\displaystyle \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} (| \xi |^2 + 1)^k e^{\frac{- | \xi |^2}{t}} \mathrm{d} \xi = t^{d / 2} \underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} (t | \xi |^2 + 1)^k e^{- | \xi |^2} \mathrm{d} \xi}_{:= \mathcal{I}}\)

Split

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{I} = \int_{t | \xi |^2 \leq 1} (t | \xi |^2 + 1)^k e^{- | \xi |^2} \mathrm{d} \xi + \int_{t | \xi |^2 \geq 1} (t | \xi |^2 + 1)^k e^{- | \xi |^2} \mathrm{d} \xi =: \mathbb{I}+\mathbb{I}\mathbb{I}.\)

Now since \(e^{- | \xi |^2} \leq 1\), we have

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{I} \leq \int_{| \xi |^2 \leq \frac{1}{t}} 2^k e^{- | \xi |^2} \mathrm{d} \xi \leq 2^k \operatorname{Vol}_d \left( t^{- \frac{1}{2}} \right) \lesssim_{k, d} t^{- \frac{d}{2}}\)

thus

\(\displaystyle \int_0^T \frac{1}{t^2} e^{\frac{- m^2}{t}} t^{d / 2} \mathbb{I} \mathrm{d} t \lesssim \int_0^T t^{- 2} e^{\frac{- m^2}{t}} \mathrm{d} t \lesssim 1.\)

On the other hand,

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{I}\mathbb{I} \leq \int_{t | \xi |^2 \geq 1} 2^k t^k (| \xi |^2)^k e^{- | \xi |^2} \mathrm{d} \xi \leq 2^k t^k \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} | \xi |^{2 k} e^{- | \xi |^2} \mathrm{d} \xi \lesssim_k t^k\)

since Gaussian random variables are in every \(L^p\). Thus when \(k + d / 2 - 2 > - 1\), we have

\(\displaystyle \int_0^T \frac{1}{t^2} e^{\frac{- m^2}{t}} t^{d / 2} \mathbb{I}\mathbb{I} \mathrm{d} t \lesssim \int_0^T t^{k + d / 2 - 2} e^{\frac{- m^2}{t}} \mathrm{d} t \lesssim_{k, d} T^{k + d / 2 - 1} . \) (A.7)

Putting everything together,

\(\displaystyle \int_0^T \| Q_t \|^2_{\mathcal{L}_2 (L^2 ; \mathcal{H})} \mathrm{d} t \lesssim_{\ell} \int_0^T \| H_t \|_{L^2 (\mathbb{R}^d)}^2 \mathrm{d} t = \int_0^T \frac{1}{t^2} e^{\frac{- m^2}{t}} t^{d / 2} (\mathbb{I}+\mathbb{I}\mathbb{I}) \mathrm{d} t \lesssim 1 + T^{k + d / 2 - 1}\)

proving (A.5). The bound (A.6) follows similarly with the term \(t^{k + d / 2 - 2}\) in (A.7) being replaced by \(t^{- 1}\).\(\Box\)

Thus, as \((Q_t)_{t \in \mathbb{R}_+}\) is deterministic hence predictable, we obtained that the stochastic integral is well-defined, and also get by standard results the following

Corollary A.10. For any \(t < \infty\), \(B_t\) is a Gaussian random variable with covariance operator \(\int_0^t Q^2_s \mathrm{d} s\), and \(B_t \in \mathcal{H}\) almost surely.

BCalculus on the space of germs

B.1Computing the trace

Consider \(F, G\) Frechet differentiable functionals on \(\mathcal{H}\). Recall that by Riesz–Frechet representation, for any \(F \in \mathcal{C}^2 (\mathcal{H} ; \mathbb{R})\) we have the identification of \(\mathrm{D}^2 F |_{\phi} \in L^{(2)} (\mathcal{H} \times \mathcal{H} ; \mathbb{R})\) with \(\mathrm{D}^2 F |^{\sharp}_{\phi} \in L (\mathcal{H})\). Similarly, given \(F, G \in \mathcal{C}^1 (\mathcal{H} ; \mathbb{R})\), the continuous bilinear form

\(\displaystyle (v, w) \mapsto \mathrm{D} F |_{\phi} (v) \mathrm{D} G |_{\phi} (w)\)

is identified with a bounded linear operator \(\mathrm{D} F \mathrm{D} G |_{\phi}^{\sharp} \) on \(\mathcal{H}\):

\(\displaystyle \mathrm{D} F |_{\phi} (v) \mathrm{D} G |_{\phi} (w) = \langle (\mathrm{D} F \mathrm{D} G |_{\phi}^{\sharp} ) (v), w \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} .\)

Lemma B.1. For any \(F \in \mathcal{C}^2 (\mathcal{H} ; \mathbb{R})\) we have

\(\displaystyle \operatorname{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}} [\mathrm{D}^2 F |^{\sharp}_{\phi} Q_t Q^{\ast}_t] = \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathrm{D}^2 F |_{\phi} (k^Q_t (\cdot - z), k^Q_t (\cdot - z)) \mathrm{d} z\)

and for \(F, G \in \mathcal{C}^1 (\mathcal{H} ; \mathbb{R})\),

\(\displaystyle \operatorname{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}} [\mathrm{D} F \mathrm{D} G |^{\sharp}_{\phi} Q_t Q^{\ast}_t] = \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathrm{D} F |_{\phi} (k^Q_t (\cdot - z)) \mathrm{D} G |_{\phi} (k^Q_t (\cdot - z)) \mathrm{d} z.\)

Proof. The kernel representation of \(Q_t\) ensures that for every \(f \in L^2\), \(h \in \mathcal{H}\), by continuity of the Bochner integral and \(\| k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \|_{\mathcal{H}} \in L^2 (\mathrm{d} z)\),

\(\displaystyle \langle Q^{\ast}_t h, f \rangle_{L^2} = \langle h, Q_t f \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} = \left\langle h, \int f (z) k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \mathrm{d} z \right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}} = \int f (z) \langle h, k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} \mathrm{d} z.\)

Thus \(Q^{\ast}_t h (z) = \langle h, k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\) for almost every \(z\), thus applying the kernel representation again,

\(\displaystyle Q_t Q^{\ast}_t h = \int k^Q_t (\cdot - z) Q^{\ast}_t h (z) \mathrm{d} z = \int k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \langle h, k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} \mathrm{d} z.\)

Consider a bounded bilinear form \(b \in L^{(2)} (\mathcal{H} \times \mathcal{H} ; \mathbb{R})\) with Riesz–Frechet representation \(A \in L (\mathcal{H})\), i.e. \(b (v, w) = \langle A (v), w \rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\). Then for \((e_n)_{n \in \mathbb{N}}\) ONB of \(\mathcal{H}\), \(u = \sum_n \langle u, e_n \rangle e_n\)

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \operatorname{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}} [A Q_t Q^{\ast}_t] & = & \sum_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \langle A Q_t Q^{\ast}_t e_n, e_n \rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\\ & = & \sum_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \left\langle A \int k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \langle e_n, k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} \mathrm{d} z, e_n \right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\\ & = & \int \sum_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \langle A k^Q_t (\cdot - z), e_n \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} \langle e_n, k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} \mathrm{d} z\\ & = & \int \langle A k^Q_t (\cdot - z), k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} \mathrm{d} z\\ & = & \int b (k^Q_t (\cdot - z), k^Q_t (\cdot - z)) \mathrm{d} z. \end{array}\)

Here the interchange follows via Fubini from Cauchy–Schwarz, Parseval, and the aforementioned fact that \(\| k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \|_{\mathcal{H}} \in L^2 (\mathrm{d} z)\):

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \sum_{n \in \mathbb{N}} | \langle A k^Q_t (\cdot - z), e_n \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} \langle e_n, k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} | & \leq & \sqrt{\sum_{n \in \mathbb{N}} | \langle A k^Q_t (\cdot - z), e_n \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} |^2} \sqrt{\sum_{n \in \mathbb{N}} | \langle e_n, k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \rangle_{\mathcal{H}} |^2}\\ & \leq & \| A \|_{L (\mathcal{H})} \| k^Q_t (\cdot - z) \|_{\mathcal{H}}^2 . \end{array}\)

Applying the above computation with \(A := \mathrm{D}^2 F |^{\sharp}_{\phi} \) and \(A := \mathrm{D} F \mathrm{D} G |^{\sharp}_{\phi} \) yields the results.\(\Box\)

Notation B.2. It follows from (2.7) that

\(\displaystyle \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} k^Q_t (x - \xi) k^Q_t (y - \xi) \mathrm{d} \xi = k^{Q^2}_t (x - y) = \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x - y),\)

thus we write \(\dot{G}_t\) instead of \(Q_t Q^{\ast}_t\) to mean the contractions of Lemma B.1.

Indeed, to highlight the effectiveness of the above abuse of notation, consider \(F\) and \(G\) to be point evaluations at \(x_1, x_2 \in \mathbb{R}^d\). Then \(\mathrm{D} F \mathrm{D} G |_{\phi} = \delta_{x_1} \otimes \delta_{x_2}\) and thus

\(\displaystyle \operatorname{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}} [\mathrm{D} F \mathrm{D} G |^{\sharp}_{\phi} Q_t Q^{\ast}_t] = \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} {\delta_{x_1}} (k^Q_t (\cdot - z)) \delta_{x_2} (k^Q_t (\cdot - z)) \mathrm{d} z = \dot{\mathcal{G}}_t (x_1 - x_2) .\)

More generally, for functionals \(F : \mathcal{H} \rightarrow \mathbb{R}\) that depend on a finite jet of \(\phi\) at a point, we have the following computation.

Example B.3. Fix \(m \in \mathbb{N}\) and consider \(F : \mathcal{H} \rightarrow \mathbb{R}\) given by

\(\displaystyle F (\phi) := f \left( \bigotimes_{| \alpha | \leq m} \partial^{\alpha} \phi (x) \right) = f \circ \left( \bigotimes_{| \alpha | \leq m} (\delta_x \circ \partial^{\alpha}) \phi \right)\)

for an \(f \in \mathcal{C}^2 (\mathbb{R}^N ; \mathbb{R})\) where \(N = N (d, m) \in \mathbb{N}\) is the number of \(d\)-dimensional multiindices of order at most \(m\). Note that this includes, as a particular case, every constant coefficient differential operator \(P = \sum_{| \alpha | \leq m} \gamma_{\alpha} \partial^{\alpha}\) with the induced function \(f_P (a) := \sum_{i = 1}^N \gamma_i a_i\) for all \(a \in \mathbb{R}^N\). Here \(\mathcal{H} := H^{k + m}_{\ell}\) where \(k = k (d)\) is the smallest integer such that \(H^k_{\ell} \hookrightarrow \mathcal{C}\) continuously (Morrey's inequality).

Then chain rule and linearity yields that \(F \in \mathcal{C}^1\), and \(\mathrm{D} F |_{\phi} \in L (\mathcal{H} ; \mathbb{R})\) is given by

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \mathrm{D} F |_{\phi} & = & \mathrm{D} f |_{\left( \bigotimes_{| \alpha | \leq m} (\delta_x \circ \partial^{\alpha}) \phi \right)} \circ \left( \bigotimes_{| \alpha | \leq m} \mathrm{D} \delta_x |_{\partial^{\alpha} \phi} \circ \mathrm{D} \partial^{\alpha} |_{\phi} \right)\\ & = & \left\langle \nabla f \left( \bigotimes_{| \alpha | \leq m} (\partial^{\alpha} \phi (x)) \right), \cdot \right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^N} \circ \left( \bigotimes_{| \alpha | \leq m} \delta_x \circ \partial^{\alpha} \right), \end{array}\)

that is, denoting by \(\Phi_F := \bigotimes_{| \alpha | \leq m} (\partial^{\alpha} \phi (x)) \in \mathbb{R}^N\), we have for every \(v \in \mathcal{H}\) that

\(\displaystyle \mathrm{D} F |_{\phi} v = \left\langle \nabla f (\Phi_F), \bigotimes_{| \alpha | \leq m} \partial^{\alpha} v (x) \right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^N} .\)

Thus

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \operatorname{Tr}_{\mathcal{H}} [\mathrm{D} F \mathrm{D} G |_{\phi}^{\sharp} Q_t Q^{\ast}_t] & = & \langle \mathrm{D} G |_{\phi} \otimes \mathrm{D} F |_{\phi} , \dot{G}_t \rangle\\ & = & \left\langle \sum_{| \alpha_f | \leq m_f, | \alpha_g | \leq m_g} c_{\alpha_f, \alpha_g} \partial^{\alpha_f} \delta_x \otimes \partial^{\alpha_g} \delta_y, \dot{G}_t \right\rangle \end{array}\)

with

\(\displaystyle c_{\alpha_f, \alpha_g} = (- 1)^{| \alpha_f | + | \alpha_g |} {[\nabla f (\Phi_F)]_{\alpha_f}} \cdot [\nabla g (\Phi_G)]_{\alpha_g}\)

where \({[\nabla f (\Phi_F)]_{\alpha_f}} \) denotes the partial derivative of \(f\) corresponding to the multiindex \(\alpha_f\) evaluated at \(\Phi_F\).

B.2Flow equation computations

Proof. (of Lemma 3.1) For simplicity we will sometimes suppress the UV cutoff \(T\) in the notation. Of course

\(\displaystyle \partial_s V_s^{[\ell]} = \partial_s \int v^{[\ell]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \psi_{1 : \ell} \rho (x_{1 : \ell}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell} = \int \partial_s v^{[\ell]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \psi_{1 : \ell} \rho (x_{1 : \ell}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell}\)

and

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\dot{G}_s} V_s^{[\ell]} & = & \frac{1}{2} \operatorname{Tr} \left[ \dot{G}_s \mathrm{D}^2 \left( \int v^{[\ell]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \psi_{\xi_{1 : \ell}} \rho (x_{1 : \ell}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell} \right) \right]\\ & = & - \frac{\beta^2}{2} \int v^{[\ell]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \sum_{j, k = 1}^{\ell} \sigma_j \sigma_k \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (x_j - x_k) \psi_{1 : \ell} \rho (x_{1 : \ell}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell}\\ & = & \int v^{[\ell]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \psi_{1 : \ell} \rho (x_{1 : \ell}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell} . \end{array}\)

Noting that

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \langle \mathrm{D} \psi_{1 : \ell_1}, \dot{G}_s \mathrm{D} \psi_{1 : \ell_2} \rangle & = & \left\langle \sum_{j = 1}^{\ell_1} i \beta \sigma_j \delta_{x_j} \psi_{1 : \ell_1}, \dot{G}_s \sum_{k = 1}^{\ell_2} i \beta \sigma_k \delta_{x_k} \psi_{1 : \ell_2} \right\rangle\\ & = & - \beta^2 {\sum_{j = 1}^{\ell_1}} \sum_{k = 1}^{\ell_2} \sigma_j \sigma_k \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (x_j - x_k) \psi_{1 : \ell}\\ & = & 2 \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell_1}, \xi_{1 : \ell_2}) \psi_{1 : \ell} \end{array}\)

we have, via permutation invariance of the kernels, i.e. e.g. \(v^{[\ell_1]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell_1}) = v^{[\ell_1]}_s (\xi_{\ell_2 : \ell})\) whenever \(\ell_1 + \ell_2 = \ell\), that

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \frac{1}{2} \langle \mathrm{D} V_s^{[\ell_1]}, \dot{G}_s \mathrm{D} V_s^{[\ell_2]} \rangle\\ = & \frac{1}{2} \left\langle \mathrm{D} \int v^{[\ell_1]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell_1}) \psi_{1 : \ell_1} \rho (x_{1 : \ell_1}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell_1}, \dot{G}_s \mathrm{D} \int v^{[\ell_2]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell_2}) \psi_{1 : \ell_2} \rho (x_{1 : \ell_2}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell_2} \right\rangle\\ = & \int \int v^{[\ell_1]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell_1}) \psi_{1 : \ell_1} \rho (x_{1 : \ell_1}) \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell_1}, \xi_{1 : \ell_2}) v^{[\ell_2]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell_2}) \psi_{1 : \ell_2} \rho (x_{1 : \ell_2}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell'} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell''}\\ = & \int v^{[\ell_1]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell_1}) \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell_1}, \xi_{1 : \ell_2}) v^{[\ell_2]}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell_2}) \psi_{1 : \ell} \rho (x_{1 : \ell}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell} . \end{array}\)

Thus, the equation (2.20) is satisfied if \(v_{t, T}^{[\ell]}\) is such that

\(\displaystyle \partial_t v_{t, T}^{[\ell]} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) + v_{t, T}^{[\ell]} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{1 : \ell}) = \sum_{I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell} C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |} v_{t, T}^{[| I_1 |]} (\xi_{I_1}) \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{I_1}, \xi_{I_2}) v_{t, T}^{[| I_2 |]} (\xi_{I_2}) \) (B.1)

with \(C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |}\) combinatorial constants.

It remains to show that if \(v_{t, T}^{[\ell]}\) solves (3.3), then it satisfies (B.1). For brevity, we denote the right-hand side of (B.1) by \(f_t\), so that (3.3) reads as

\(\displaystyle v_{t, T}^{[\ell]} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) = v_{T, T}^{[\ell]} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} - \int_t^T f_s e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} \mathrm{d} s.\)

The Leibniz integral rule says

\(\displaystyle \partial_t \int_{a (t)}^{b (t)} h (t, s) \mathrm{d} s = h (t, b (t)) \partial_t b (t) - h (t, a (t)) \partial_t a (t) + \int_{a (t)}^{b (t)} \partial_t h (t, s) \mathrm{d} s\)

so in particular with \(a (t) = t\), \(b (t) = T\) we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \partial_t \int_t^T f_s e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} \mathrm{d} s & = & - f_t e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, t} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} + \int_t^T f_s \partial_t e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} \mathrm{d} s\\ & = & - f_t + \int_t^T f_s e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} (- \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{1 : \ell})) \mathrm{d} s\\ & = & - f_t - \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \int_t^T f_s e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} \mathrm{d} s. \end{array}\)

Hence

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \partial_t v_{t, T}^{[\ell]} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) & = & \partial_t (v^{[\ell]}_{T, T} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : \ell})}) - \partial_t \int_t^T f_s e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} \mathrm{d} s\\ & = & v_{T, T}^{[\ell]} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} (- \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{1 : \ell})) + f_t + \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{1 : \ell}) \int_t^T f_s e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} \mathrm{d} s\\ & = & v_{T, T}^{[\ell]} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} (- \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{1 : \ell})) + f_t - (- \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{1 : \ell})) \left( \int_t^T f_s e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} \mathrm{d} s \right)\\ & = & (- \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{1 : \ell})) \left( v_{T, T}^{[\ell]} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, T} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} - \int_t^T f_s e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s} (\xi_{1 : \ell})} \mathrm{d} s \right) + f_t\\ & = & (- \dot{\mathbb{G}}_t (\xi_{1 : \ell})) v_{t, T}^{[\ell]} (\xi_{1 : \ell}) + f_t . \end{array}\)

as requested.\(\Box\)

Proof. (of Lemma 5.1) From (5.2) compute

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \mathrm{D} \Gamma^{[\ell]}_{s, T} & = & \sum_{k = 0}^n k \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \phi (y)^{k - 1} \delta_y\\ \mathrm{D}^2 \Gamma^{[\ell]}_{s, T} & = & \sum_{k = 0}^n k (k - 1) \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \phi (y)^{k - 2} (\delta_y \otimes \delta_y)\\ \mathrm{D} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} & = & i \beta \sum_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \sigma_j \delta_{x_j} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ \mathrm{D}^2 \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} & = & - \beta^2 \sum_{j = 1}^{\ell - 1} \sum_{k = 1}^{\ell - 1} \sigma_j \sigma_k (\delta_{x_j} \otimes \delta_{x_k}) \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \end{array}\)

Then from (5.1), as

\(\displaystyle \mathrm{D}^2 q_s^{[\ell], T} (\phi) = \int \Gamma^{[\ell]}_{s, T} \mathrm{D}^2 \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} + \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{D}^2 \Gamma^{[\ell]}_{s, T} + 2 \mathrm{D} \Gamma^{[\ell]}_{s, T} \mathrm{D} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\)

we obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\dot{G}_s} q_s^{[\ell], T} (\phi) & = & \int \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \sum_{k = 0}^n \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \phi (y)^k \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ & + & \int \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \sum_{k = 0}^n \frac{k (k - 1)}{2} \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \phi (y)^{k - 2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (0) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\\ & + & \int \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \sum_{k = 0}^n k \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \phi (y)^{k - 1} 2 \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)} \end{array}\)

Moreover, as

\(\displaystyle \mathrm{D} q_s^{[\ell']} (\phi) = \int \psi_{1 : (\ell' - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{D} \Gamma^{[\ell']}_{s, T} + \Gamma^{[\ell']}_{s, T} \mathrm{D} \psi_{1 : (\ell' - 1)}^{\rho} \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : (\ell' - 1)}\)

and

\(\displaystyle \mathrm{D} V_s^{[\ell'']} = i \beta \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = \ell'' \end{array}$}}} \int v^{[\ell''] (\boldsymbol{b})}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell''}) \sum_{j = 1}^{\ell''} \sigma_j \delta_{x_j} \psi_{1 : \ell''}^{\rho} M_{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_{1 : \ell''}) \mathrm{d} \xi_{1 : \ell''}\)

we obtain for \(\ell', \ell''\) such that \(\ell' + \ell'' = \ell\) by the symmetry of kernels that

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \langle \mathrm{D} q^{[\ell']}_s, \dot{G}_s \mathrm{D} V_s^{[\ell'']} \rangle\\ = & \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = \ell'' \end{array}$}}} \left\langle v^{[\ell''] (\boldsymbol{b})}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell''}) M_{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_{1 : \ell''}) 2 \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell''}, y) \sum_{k = 0}^n k \gamma^{[\ell'] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell' - 1)}, y), \phi (y)^{k - 1} \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \right\rangle\\ + & \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = \ell'' \end{array}$}}} \left\langle v^{[\ell''] (\boldsymbol{b})}_s (\xi_{1 : \ell''}) M_{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_{1 : \ell''}) 2 \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{1 : (\ell' - 1)}, \xi_{1 : \ell''}) \sum_{k = 0}^n \gamma^{[\ell'] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{1 : (\ell' - 1)}, y), \phi (y)^k \psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}^{\rho} \right\rangle \end{array}\)

hence the differential flow equation (2.19) for the coefficient of field content \(\psi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}\) can be written as

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \sum_{k = 0}^n \partial_s \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} \phi (y)^k + \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} \phi (y)^k \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)})\\ + & \sum_{k = 0}^n \frac{k (k - 1)}{2} \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} \phi (y)^{k - 2} \dot{\mathcal{G}}_s (0) + 2 k \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} \phi (y)^{k - 1} \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}, y)\\ = & \sum_{I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell} \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = | I_2 | \end{array}$}}} C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |} v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} (\xi_{I_2}) M_{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_{I_2}) \dot{\mathbb{\not{G}}}_s (\xi_{I_2}, y) \sum_{k = 0}^n k \gamma^{[| I_1 |] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{I_1 - 1}, y) \phi (y)^{k - 1}\\ + & \sum_{I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell} \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol{b} \in \mathfrak{M} :\\ \ell (\boldsymbol{b}) = | I_2 | \end{array}$}}} C_{| I_1 |, | I_2 |} v_s^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{b})} (\xi_{I_2}) M_{(\boldsymbol{b})} (x_{I_2}) \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{I_1 - 1}, \xi_{I_2}) \sum_{k = 0}^n \gamma^{[| I_1 |] (k)}_{s, T} (\xi_{I_1 - 1}, y) \phi (y)^k . \end{array}\)

Matching powers of \(\phi (y)\) we obtain a system of equations for the kernels where corresponding to \(\phi (y)^k\) we have

\(\displaystyle \partial_s \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} + \dot{\mathbb{G}}_s (\xi_{1 : (\ell - 1)}) \gamma^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T} = - \mathscr{S}^{[\ell] (k)}_{s, T}\)

and the statement follows from the Duhamel principle similarly to the proof of Lemma 3.1.\(\Box\)

B.3UV stability estimates

Proof. (of Corollary 3.15) We are going show that for \(\varepsilon > 0\) sufficiently small depending only on \(\beta\), it holds for all \(t \in \mathbb{R}_+\) that

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980}v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, \infty} - v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell), \eta} \lesssim \lambda_t (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}} \langle t \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon} . \) (B.2)

The identically zero kernels, i.e. those with \(\boldsymbol{a} \in \mathfrak{M} \setminus \mathfrak{M}_p\) and \(\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a}) = 1\), are trivial. For the nonzero kernel at \(\ell = 1\), the limit is \(v^{[1] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, \infty} := \lambda e^{\frac{\beta^2}{8 \pi} \mathcal{G}_t (0)}\): since \(t \mapsto \lambda_t\) is increasing,

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980}v^{[1] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, \infty} - v^{[1] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_t = | \lambda_t - \lambda_{t \wedge T} | = \mathbb{1}_{\{ t \geq T \}} | \lambda_t - \lambda_T | = \lambda_t \mathbb{1}_{\{ t \geq T \}} \left( 1 - \frac{\lambda_T}{\lambda_t} \right) = \lambda_t \mathbb{1}_{\{ t \geq T \}} \left( 1 - e^{\frac{\beta^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_{t, T} (0)} \right)\)

and using that \(1 - e^{- x} \leq x\) for \(x \geq 0\) and Lemma A.1

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980}v^{[1] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, \infty} - v^{[1] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} \unicode{x2980}_t \leq \lambda_t \mathbb{1}_{\{ t \geq T \}} \frac{\beta^2}{2} \mathcal{G}_{T, t} (0) \leq \lambda_t \mathbb{1}_{\{ t \geq T \}} \frac{\beta^2}{8 \pi} (\log (t \vee 1) + C) \lesssim \lambda_t \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon} .\)

Moving on to \(\ell \geq 2\), referring back to Section 2.7, we are going to show that the limits \(v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_t\) satisfy

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{llll} v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, \infty} & = & \int_t^{\infty} e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s}} \mathscr{S}^{(\boldsymbol{a})}_{s, \infty} \mathrm{d} s & \operatorname{if} \boldsymbol{a} \operatorname{is}\operatorname{such}\operatorname{that}v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{T, T} = 0\\ v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, \infty} & = & - \int_0^t \mathscr{S}^{(\boldsymbol{a})}_{s, \infty} \mathrm{d} s & \operatorname{otherwise}, \end{array} \) (B.3)

where \(\mathscr{S}^{(\boldsymbol{a})}_{s, \infty}\) are source terms obtained from the limiting kernels; since the system is triangular, we may proceed inductively. Since bounds of (3.29) are uniform in \(T\), they pass to the limits.

For kernels with irrelevant boundary conditions we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, \infty} - v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} & = & \int_t^{\infty} e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s}} \mathscr{S}^{(\boldsymbol{a})}_{s, \infty} \mathrm{d} s - \int_{t \wedge T}^T e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s}} \mathscr{S}^{(\boldsymbol{a})}_{s, T} \mathrm{d} s\\ & = & \int_{t \vee T}^{\infty} e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s}} \mathscr{S}_{s, \infty}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} \mathrm{d} s + \int_{t \wedge T}^T e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s}} (\mathscr{S}_{s, \infty}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} - \mathscr{S}_{s, T}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}) \mathrm{d} s\\ & =: & \mathbb{I}+\mathbb{I}\mathbb{I}. \end{array}\)

The first term is bounded via Proposition 3.14:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \unicode{x2980}\mathbb{I}\unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell)} & \lesssim & \int_{t \vee T}^{\infty} \| e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s}} \|_{L^{\infty}} \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}_{s, \infty}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta (\ell)} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \lambda_t^{| \chi |} \int_{t \vee T}^{\infty} \lambda_s^{- | \chi |} \lambda_s (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \langle s \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}} \langle s \rangle^{- 1} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \lambda_t^{| \chi |} \lambda_{t \vee T}^{- | \chi |} \lambda_{t \vee T} (\lambda_{t \vee T} \langle t \vee T \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_{t \vee T}^c \langle t \vee T \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}} \end{array}\)

Now choosing \(\varepsilon > 0\) to be small enough, depending only on \(\delta\), \(s \mapsto \lambda_s^{- | \chi |} \lambda_s (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \langle s \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}} \langle s \rangle^{\varepsilon}\) is decreasing when i

\(\displaystyle 1 - \ell \delta - | \chi | (1 - \delta) - \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2} + \varepsilon < 0 \quad \Longleftarrow \quad (\ell \geq 4) \vee (| \chi | \geq 1) \vee (\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a}) = 1) \vee (\delta > 1 / 2)\)

since \(\delta > 1 / 4\) and \(\ell \geq 2\); all irrelevant kernels satisfy this condition. Thus

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980}\mathbb{I}\unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell)} \lesssim \lambda_t (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}} \langle t \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon} .\)

To estimate \(\mathbb{I}\mathbb{I}\), we streamline notation by defining \(\langle \langle v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_{s, T}, v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{c})}_{s, T} \rangle_{\dot{\mathbb{G}}}, \Psi_{\boldsymbol{a}} \rangle\) via

\(\displaystyle \langle \mathrm{D} \langle v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_{s, T}, \Psi_{\boldsymbol{b}} \rangle, \dot{G}_s \mathrm{D} \langle v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{c})}_{s, T}, \Psi_{\boldsymbol{c}} \rangle \rangle = \langle \langle v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_{s, T}, v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{c})}_{s, T} \rangle_{\dot{\mathbb{G}}}, \Psi_{\boldsymbol{a}} \rangle\)

where \(\Psi_{\boldsymbol{a}}\) denotes the field content arising from contracting \(\Psi_{\boldsymbol{b}}\) and \(\Psi_{\boldsymbol{c}}\) in the final representation (that is, possibly after a nontrivial post-processing as in Lemma 3.8 or the lowering of the degree of divergence as in cases 2. and 3. of Proposition 3.14); That is,

\(\displaystyle \langle \mathscr{S}_{s, T}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}, \Psi_{\boldsymbol{a}} \rangle = \langle \mathbf{A}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\boldsymbol{d}} (v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{d})}_{s, T}), \Psi_{\boldsymbol{a}} \rangle + {\sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{l} I_1 \uplus I_2 = 1 : \ell\\ \boldsymbol{b}, \boldsymbol{c} \in \mathfrak{M} \end{array}$}}}} \langle \langle v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_{s, T}, v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{c})}_{s, T} \rangle_{\dot{\mathbb{G}}}, \Psi_{\boldsymbol{a}} \rangle\)

where \(\mathbf{A}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\boldsymbol{d}} (v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{d})}_{s, T}) := \mathbf{A} (v^{[2] (\varkappa)}_{s, T})\) introduced in (3.17).

Then using Proposition 3.14 and the induction on (B.2), by the linearity of \(\mathbf{A}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\boldsymbol{d}}\) we obtain

\(\displaystyle \unicode{x2980}\mathbf{A}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\boldsymbol{d}} (v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{d})}_{s, \infty}) -\mathbf{A}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\boldsymbol{d}} (v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{d})}_{s, T}) \unicode{x2980}_s \lesssim \lambda_s (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \langle s \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}} \langle s \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon} \langle s \rangle^{- 1} \) (B.4)

and by the bilinearity of \(\langle v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_{s, T}, v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{c})}_{s, T} \rangle_{\dot{\mathbb{G}}}\)

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{ll} & \unicode{x2980} \langle v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_{s, T}, v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{c})}_{s, T} \rangle_{\dot{\mathbb{G}}} - \langle v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_{s, \infty}, v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{c})}_{s, \infty} \rangle_{\dot{\mathbb{G}}} \unicode{x2980}_s\\ \lesssim & \unicode{x2980} \langle v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_{s, T} - v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_{s, \infty}, v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{c})}_{s, T} \rangle_{\dot{\mathbb{G}}} - \langle v^{[| I_1 |] (\boldsymbol{b})}_{s, \infty}, v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{c})}_{s, \infty} - v^{[| I_2 |] (\boldsymbol{c})}_{s, T} \rangle_{\dot{\mathbb{G}}} \unicode{x2980}_s\\ \lesssim & \lambda_s (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \langle s \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}} \langle s \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon} \langle s \rangle^{- 1} \end{array} \) (B.5)

so that

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \unicode{x2980}\mathbb{I}\mathbb{I}\unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell)} & \leq & \int_t^T \| e^{\mathbb{G}_{t, s}} \|_{L^{\infty}} \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}_{s, \infty}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} - \mathscr{S}_{s, T}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} \unicode{x2980}_{s, \Theta (\ell)} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \lambda_t^{| \chi |} \int_t^{\infty} \lambda_s^{- | \chi |} \lambda_s (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \langle s \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}} \langle s \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon} \langle s \rangle^{- 1} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \lambda_t (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}} \langle t \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon} \end{array}\)

as requested.

It remains to treat kernels with relevant boundary conditions. In the regime we are working in, the only such kernel is the one arising from the \(\ell = 2\), \(\chi (\xi_{1 : 2}) = 0\) dipole when \(\delta \in \left( \frac{1}{4}, \frac{1}{2} \right]\), cf. the discussion preceding Lemma 3.8. Then

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, \infty} - v^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}_{t, T} & = & \int_0^{t \wedge T} \mathscr{S}_{s, T}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} \mathrm{d} s - \int_0^t \mathscr{S}_{s, \infty}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} \mathrm{d} s\\ & = & \int_0^{t \wedge T} (\mathscr{S}_{s, T}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} - \mathscr{S}_{s, \infty}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})}) \mathrm{d} s - \int_{t \wedge T}^t \mathscr{S}_{s, \infty}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} \mathrm{d} s \end{array}\)

The first term is bounded via the induction on (B.2) and Proposition 3.14 similarly to (B.4) and (B.5) and using that the source term is local:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \int_0^{t \wedge T} \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}_{s, \infty}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} - \mathscr{S}_{s, T}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell)} \mathrm{d} s & \lesssim & \int_0^t \lambda_s (\lambda_s \langle s \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_s^c \langle s \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}} \langle s \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon} \langle s \rangle^{- 1} \mathrm{d} s\\ & \lesssim & \lambda_t (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}} \langle t \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon} \end{array} \) (B.6)

since the integral diverges when the kernel is relevant (recall the proof of Lemma 3.9). The second term is trivial: again by locality and Proposition 3.14,

\(\displaystyle \int_{t \wedge T}^t \unicode{x2980} \mathscr{S}_{s, \infty}^{[\ell] (\boldsymbol{a})} \unicode{x2980}_{t, \Theta (\ell)} \mathrm{d} s \lesssim \mathbb{1}_{\{ T < t \}} \lambda_t (\lambda_t \langle t \rangle^{- 1})^{\ell - 1} \mathfrak{l}_t^c \langle t \rangle^{- \frac{\varkappa (\boldsymbol{a})}{2}}\)

and we conclude from \(\mathbb{1}_{\{ T < t \}} \leq \langle t \rangle^{\varepsilon} \langle T \rangle^{- \varepsilon}\). This completes the proof on the claim about the kernel limits.

To prove the statement about \(V^{[\ell], \infty} (\phi)\), note that (B.3) implies that the limiting kernels satisfy the same differential flow equation as the UV-cutoff kernels, and that the symmetries, in particular permutation invariance also extend to the limit. Thus we may conclude from the first part of the proof of Lemma 3.1 and Lemmas 3.8 and 3.14.\(\Box\)
